The same rule already covers `make api-clients`. |

### M1-16 Event log with commit-ordered sequence

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-16/1 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M1-16-event-log` in its own worktree from the head of `int/m1-wave5` (`46d20a0`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. | The launcher's task overrides the merge step of the brief ("Definition of done"). |
| M1-16/2 | 2026-10-12 | A payload must be smaller than `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` (a payload of exactly the limit is rejected), as 04 "Rules for the payload" item 2 ("smaller than") and the fixed requirement 16 of the brief ("below") say; 03 ("up to") and the design note ("at most") read as an upper bound the strict rule also keeps. | The core contract chapters disagree by one byte; the strict reading satisfies every text. |
| M1-16/3 | 2026-10-12 | The appender also rejects a payload whose strings or keys contain U+0000. | jsonb cannot store U+0000 (SQLSTATE 22P05, `untranslatable_character`; [JSON types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-json.html) and [PostgreSQL error codes](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/errcodes-appendix.html), retrieved 2026-10-13); the producer would otherwise get a database error inside the hook instead of a kind internal error at the append. |
| M1-16/4 | 2026-10-12 | `Event.Envelope` and the mapping rules live in the port `internal/sync`; `domain/` holds the validation, the partition arithmetic and the gap check. | The brief asks for the method on the port type and for the rules in domain; the port may import only kernel packages (`.go-arch-lint.yml`), so it cannot call domain. |
| M1-16/5 | 2026-10-12 | `DeliveryFields` carries `SyncEpoch` as well (`sync_epoch`, webhooks only, ADR 0059). | `EventEnvelope` in openapi.yaml has the member, and `TestEnvelope_FieldsMatchOpenAPI` requires the envelope members to equal its properties. |
| M1-16/6 | 2026-10-12 | The dedicated connection is `(*pg.Pool).DialDedicated(ctx, applicationName)` instead of a free function `pg.DialDedicated`. | A connection with the configuration and password source of the pool needs both; the pool already holds them. |
| M1-16/7 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.MigrateUpSteps(pool, fsys, MigrateUpConfig{JobsSchema, Sync}, result)` adds the after-step `event_log_partitions` behind the instance step; `wire.MigrationSteps`, which `pgtest.WithSteps` uses for templates, keeps only the instance step, so test databases have no partitions and the event log tests create theirs with their own size. | `bothy migrate up` must use the configured values (brief scope item 9); pgtest's step function gets no configuration. |
| M1-16/8 | 2026-10-12 | The new partition table is `LIKE event_log INCLUDING DEFAULTS INCLUDING CONSTRAINTS`. | ATTACH PARTITION requires the CHECK constraints of the parent on the attached table ([ALTER TABLE](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-altertable.html), retrieved 2026-10-13); with `INCLUDING DEFAULTS` alone it fails. |
| M1-16/9 | 2026-10-12 | `sync_event_log_ensure_partitions` locks `event_log` in `SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE` mode before it reads the bounds; `sync_event_log_drop_expired` stops at the first partition it keeps, so only a prefix of partitions is ever dropped. | Two concurrent runs (job and `bothy migrate up`) would otherwise create the same table twice; `occurred_at` is the start of a transaction and need not grow with `seq` (`now()` returns the transaction start, [Date/Time functions](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/functions-datetime.html), retrieved 2026-10-13), and a hole in the retained range would break `min_retained_seq` (04 "resync_required"). |
| M1-16/10 | 2026-10-12 | The rows go in with one multi-row insert from arrays, and `pg_notify` runs in the same statement (a data-modifying CTE), not with sqlc `:copyfrom` and not as a second statement. | Every round trip under the counter lock lowers the commit rate (`perf.event_commit_min_per_s`); one statement is one round trip, COPY would need a second one for the notification. |
| M1-16/11 | 2026-10-12 | Without a valid span context the correlation ID of the request in `kernel/reqmeta` (if it is a UUID) becomes `correlation_id`; `trace_context` stays empty. Without a span and outside a request both are NULL. | 03 `event_log.correlation_id`: a request without traceparent gets an ID from the server; `platform/httpserver` keeps that ID in reqmeta, the same the response and the audit entry carry. |
| M1-16/12 | 2026-10-12 | The error line of an insert without a partition carries `bothy.event.seq` (the first seq) and `error.type` (`23514`); the whole range is in the error text. | The log handlers keep only catalogue attributes (`platform/otel/attr`), and the catalogue has no second seq attribute; adding one is a telemetry change outside this ticket. |
| M1-16/13 | 2026-10-12 | Bounds of the new keys: `sync.event_log_partition_size` at least 1, `sync.event_retention` at least 1s, `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` 3 to 1048576, `sync.log_poll_interval` at least 1ms, `sync.tail_batch_size` 1 to 2147483647 (the PostgreSQL `integer` maximum, [Numeric types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-numeric.html), retrieved 2026-10-13), `sync.partition_maintenance_interval` at least 1s, `sync.event_log_partitions_ahead` 1 to 1000. | The chapters give only defaults; `{}` is the smallest payload, 0 partitions ahead would let the next commit miss a partition, and a payload limit above `http.max_json_body_bytes` would never be reached by a request. |
| M1-16/14 | 2026-10-12 | The tail's start step waits until `Run` has read the head (`Tail.Started`) before the public listener opens; a tail that ends with an error calls `Runner.Fail`, and its stop step `event_tail` runs in `PhaseEventTail` without its own budget. | 02 "Start and graceful shutdown" step 8 sets `local_head` before the listener; the stop only cancels a loop. |
| M1-16/15 | 2026-10-12 | ADR 0009 is not changed; its confirmation is held by `TestEventLog_ConcurrentWritersGapless` and `TestTail_ListenConnectionLossLosesNothing`. | The brief extends only ADR 0052, 0053 and 0063. |
| M1-16/16 | 2026-10-12 | `make perf` measured `perf.event_commit_min_per_s` below the threshold on a development machine whose database runs in a VM (one round trip about 0.4ms from the test process); the gate stays as ADR 0063 defines it and the numbers are in the report, not in docs. | Under the counter lock every unit spends about three round trips and the commit, so the rate there is bound by the VM network, not by the pattern; the reference profile runs PostgreSQL locally (02 "Configuration values of this chapter", `perf.reference_profile`). |
| M1-16/17 | 2026-10-12 | The module README of sync keeps its generated sections (purpose, port, dependencies, tables, actions, events, jobs), which `make register-check` compares with the register, and adds the sections of this ticket. | The generated sections are not this ticket's text. |
| M1-16/18 | 2026-10-12 | The appender rejects numbers with an exponent, invalid UTF-8 and unpaired surrogate escapes, and the reads compact the jsonb text before they hand payloads to the sinks. | Red team round 1 (M1): jsonb stores numbers as `numeric` and writes `1e300` out in full ([JSON types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-json.html), retrieved 2026-10-13), so a checked payload could be stored and delivered far above `sync.event_payload_max_bytes`; without exponents the compact form of the normalised jsonb is never larger than the checked bytes. |
| M1-16/19 | 2026-10-12 | The partition maintenance runs `ensure` and `drop_expired` in two units of work; an error of the drop is `ErrDropDeferred` with the result of the first unit, the job is retried by River, and the partition step of `bothy migrate up` ignores it. | Red team round 1 (M2): a DETACH waiting behind a long reader rolled back the partitions created in the same unit, and appends ran out of partitions. |
| M1-16/20 | 2026-10-12 | `sync_event_log_drop_expired` clamps `p_head` to `max(seq)` and `p_cutoff` to `now()`; `sync_event_log_ensure_partitions` refuses a size above 10^12 and more than 1000 partitions ahead, and `sync.event_log_partition_size` has the maximum 10^12. | Red team round 1 (Minor, hardening): bothy_app could drop the head partition with arguments of its choice, and an unbounded size overflows bigint in `p_head + p_ahead * p_size`. |
| M1-16/21 | 2026-10-12 | A sink error while the context of `Tail.Run` has ended is the orderly end of `Run` (nil), not a fatal error; a stored payload `null` renders as `{}` in the envelope. | Red team round 1 (Minor): a sink that honours the context would otherwise turn every shutdown into exit code 1. |
| M1-16/22 | 2026-10-12 | Numbers with more than 131072 digits before or 16383 digits after the point (the `numeric` limits, [Numeric types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-numeric.html), retrieved 2026-10-13) are rejected at the append; only a lock timeout of the drop unit becomes `ErrDropDeferred`, any other drop error is returned unchanged. | Red team round 2 (M1-rest): such numbers passed the appender and failed the unit at the insert with SQLSTATE 22003 (`numeric_value_out_of_range`); a permanent drop failure must not pass silently as deferred. |
| M1-16/23 | 2026-10-12 | Long digit runs (17 to 20 digits) never appear as literals in tests: names and limits are built (`fmt.Sprintf("%019d", ...)`, `math.MaxInt64` as a parameter), and the generic white-label scan (`tools/denylist`, `make test` and `make denylist`) runs on the working tree before every commit of this ticket. | Red team rounds 1 and 2 (B1): the generic rule for account and card numbers matched partition names and the bigint maximum twice; the gate that catches the class exists, the gap was running it before the commit. |
| M1-16/24 | 2026-10-12 | Port onto main, after M1-28: the start step of the event log is 6c (`sync`), after 6b (`authz`, M1-28); both come from the same base, and neither depends on the other. The load test report (`perf/internal/report`) reads the English section "Configuration values of this chapter" of 02 and the place `Load test`; the build notes go into the English ADRs 0052, 0053 and 0063; references name the English chapter files and headings of main. | Translation and conflict resolution only; the behaviour equals the approved ticket. |

## PL Rulings of the PR lane

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| PL/1 | 2026-10-14 | `make test` runs the Go tests with `-coverpkg=./...`: a statement counts as covered when any test of its module runs it, also a test in another package. The gate `coverage` (`quality.coverage.go_min_percent`) and the new-code coverage of SonarQube read this profile. | Many use cases are tested through their HTTP adapter against PostgreSQL (the coming sessions, refresh and step-up flows among them); with per-package coverage the code those tests run counted as uncovered, and the new-code gate failed for code that the suite executes. |
| PL/2 | 2026-10-15 | `make test` gives each Go test binary `quality.test.go_timeout` (`20m`, `GO_TEST_TIMEOUT`) instead of the default ten minutes of `go test`. | With `-coverpkg=./...` (PL/1) every package instruments the whole module; `internal/identity/adapters/httpapi` needed 568 s in the `sonar` job of #84 and passed ten minutes in the `sonar` job of a later pull request twice, so the job failed although no test failed. |
| PL/3 | 2026-10-16 | The handler test harness (`httpservertest.New`) takes the request validator of the chain from `httpservertest.SharedValidator`, built once per process, contract and operation list, like the response validator (M1-31/17). | Every harness built its own validator over the whole contract: with the tests of M1-36 the HTTP test package of identity needed 699 s and 9.5 GB under `-race` and module-wide coverage, and both CI jobs that run `make test` lost their runner; with the shared validator it needs 42 s and 1.8 GB on the same host. `httpserver.Validator` is safe for concurrent use. |
| PL/4 | 2026-10-16 | Sections 1 to 9 of this log move unchanged to `docs/DECISIONS-PLANNING.md`; section 10 and the sections of the build tickets stay here, and new rulings are still recorded here. | The log grew by about 10 KiB per ported ticket and would pass `repo.max_file_kb` (1024 KiB) within the M1 tickets still to land; the briefs forbid raising the limit or excluding a file from the hygiene hooks, and the IDs stay stable (rule 1). |
| PL/5 | 2026-10-17 | Members with design-system goldens install `DsGoldenFileComparator` (`package:design_system/testing.dart`) in `test/flutter_test_config.dart` and pass no `diffThreshold` to alchemist. In a CI golden a pixel counts as different only when one channel differs by more than `dsCiGoldenChannelTolerance`, and the golden passes while at most `dsCiGoldenDiffThreshold` of its pixels differ (D-M4-04-6); a platform golden (macOS only) must match exactly; a different size fails. | The bound of D-M4-04-6 counted every changed pixel: CI goldens recorded on macOS differed on the Linux runner in up to 0.8 % of the pixels of the core component sheets (M4-07) and in 88 pixels of every mark golden of `bothy_viz` (M6-11), nearly all by a few steps of one channel at anti-aliased edges, so CI was red without a visible change. The tolerance stays below the closest dark token pair that marks an edge (a subtle border on a raised surface, held by `golden_comparator_test.dart`), and closer surfaces still fail the exact platform golden in every local `make check`. `sonar.coverage.exclusions` leaves `packages/*/lib/testing.dart` out of the coverage measure, like the test helpers of M3-01/20: `bothy_noise` and `design_system` both have that file, so SonarQube could not resolve the relative path `lib/testing.dart` of their lcov reports and counted the new comparator as uncovered (0 %), although `golden_comparator_test.dart` runs every line of it. |
| PL/6 | 2026-10-18 | The CI jobs `gates` and `sonar` get `timeout-minutes: 90` (before 60 and 45), and `ci-ok` gets 120 (before 90), so that `ci-ok`, which starts with the run and polls the other jobs, outlives the longest job it waits for plus `plan` and the runner queue. | Both jobs run the whole `make test`, `sonar` with coverage before the scanner. On the pull requests of 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18 `sonar` took between 17 and 36 minutes and was twice (pull requests 128 and 132) cancelled at its 45 minute limit while the scanner was still starting, and `gates` took up to 59 of its 60 minutes; a cancelled job leaves the required check `ci-ok` red without a finding. A limit at about one and a half to two times the usual duration keeps a hung run bounded and stops cancelling healthy ones. |
| PL/7 | 2026-10-18 | The serve tests of `internal/wire` wait up to `startupWait` (three minutes, in `serve_test.go`) for the listener lines, the start line and `/readyz`, instead of 30 seconds each. | Under `-race` on a host with a load around 30 the start of a test server (migrations, key check, warming the validators) took longer than 30 seconds, and the pre-push `make check` failed with `/readyz = 503 startup` in tests the change did not touch; integration and port runs since wave 35 saw the same. The shared harness already waits three minutes (M1-71/10); a server that never gets ready still fails. The test stores of `files/adapters/s3` get the same treatment: `testTransferTimeout` (five minutes) replaces the 30 second transfer and egress timeouts, because a Put of several megabytes to the SeaweedFS container failed in CI with `transport: deadline exceeded`, the third time across pull requests. |
| PL/8 | 2026-10-19 | Tests wait in one way: `internal/platform/testkit` holds `Patience` (named value `quality.test.patience`, three minutes) and the waits `Until`, `Receive`, `Context` and `Deadline`; every wait of a Go test for something that must happen uses them instead of its own number of seconds, and a check that something stays fast makes the slow alternative take longer than `Patience` (a drain delay of 30 minutes, a server that holds its connections until the test ends, an egress timeout of twice `Patience`) and compares with `Patience`. The Semgrep rule `bothy-go-test-wall-clock-wait` refuses a deadline variable, a connection deadline, `time.After`, `time.NewTimer`, `context.WithTimeout`, an `http.Client` timeout or an elapsed-time bound with a literal number of seconds or minutes in a `_test.go` file; a bounded give-up or a latency bound under test carries a `nosemgrep` with its reason. It does not see a wait bounded by a constant of the test or by `time.Sleep`, and Dart tests are not covered. `testkit.Integration` marks every test that needs a container runtime (`pgtest.New`, the SeaweedFS helpers, the container tests of `pgtest`); `go test -short` skips them and `pgtest.Main` then starts no container, while `make test` and CI never pass `-short`. Three tests read a clock where they raced: the backupd SIGTERM test waits for the line of the ready stanza instead of the attempt counter of the fake, the serve role test waits for the ready line after `/readyz` answers, and `TestInsertTx_ScheduledAt` compares the schedule with the clock of the database. `startupWait` (PL/7), `waitLong`, the start timeouts of the SeaweedFS helpers and of `pgtest` become `Patience`. Test servers get `startup.dependency_wait` of `Patience` where the database is reachable, both `pgtest` containers run with `max_connections` 400, because slower tests overlap more and the default of 100 ran out under load, and the acquire, statement and lock timeouts of `pgtest.Config` are `Patience`. The poll tests of the event log tail and of the consumers keep their wait for the polled delivery and log its time instead of bounding it. | Repeated reruns of CI and of the pre-push `make check` on the loaded build host came from the same class, fixed one instance at a time before (PL/7, M1-41/15, #97, #117): a test that bounded a correct but slow step by a few seconds (`/readyz`, a SeaweedFS transfer or container start, a consumer round, a SIGTERM of the fake pgBackRest). The failed attempts of the last 150 CI runs that passed on a rerun name, among others, `TestS3RequestsAreCoreS3`, `TestServeStopsChildOnSIGTERM`, `TestServeFailsWhenTheServerExits`, `TestServeRolesStartReadyAndStop`, `TestOwnerRace`, `TestConsumerWakesOnNotify` and `TestListScalesWithManyCheckpoints`; the lane reports add `TestInsertTx_ScheduledAt`, the egress and migrate tests and the wire `/readyz` waits. A condition that never holds still fails, only after `Patience`, well below `quality.test.go_timeout`. |
| PL/9 | 2026-10-19 | The REST contract and the files generated from it as one file each (`docs/api/openapi.yaml`, its byte copy `services/server/api/rest/openapi.gen.yaml` and `sdk/typescript/src/generated/openapi.ts`) have their own size bound, the named value `api.contract_max_file_kb` (2048 KiB), in a second `check-added-large-files` hook; every other file keeps `repo.max_file_kb`, and the general hook leaves out exactly these three files (a test holds both lists and both values). | The contract grows with every API ticket (about 12 KiB for M2-24) and reached `repo.max_file_kb`: with M2-24 it is 1027 KiB, so the next API change could not be committed. Splitting the contract into several files would need every generator, check and test that reads it to read a bundle instead, a change across all lanes; YAML anchors would shrink it but the pinned Dart generator refuses more than 50 aliases. A separate, still bounded limit keeps the growth visible; a split is due before the contract nears the new bound. |
| PL/10 | 2026-10-19 | The pre-push hook runs `tools/hooks/push_plan.py`: it maps the files between the pushed commit and its merge base with `origin/main` to `make check COMPONENTS=<components> AREAS=<areas> LINT_FROM_REF=<base> LINT_TO_REF=<head>`. `COMPONENTS` comes from the component map of CI (`tools/ci/components.py changed`), so the hook selects the component gates as the CI job `gates` does; a path the map sends to `all` (the Makefile, `tools/`, `.github/`, `.semgrep/`, `quality/`, root files other than Markdown, unknown paths) runs the full `make check`, as do `PUSH_FULL=1`, a tag push and a branch without a merge base. `AREAS=tools` (format, lint, types, tests and coverage of the Python tools) is on unless every changed file is a source file of a component, because the tools tests read workflows, manifests, docs and configuration of the whole repository; without it `make test` still runs the tools tests that read component sources (`TOOLS_SOURCE_TESTS`). A renamed file counts with its old and its new path (`git diff --no-renames`). `LINT_FROM_REF` and `LINT_TO_REF` run the file hygiene hooks of `make lint` on the changed files. Every other gate of `make check` runs on every push; CI never sets `AREAS` or the lint refs and runs as before. | Every push ran the full `make check` on a build host shared by up to ten sessions: the Go suite under `-race`, the tools suite, the Dart suite and the JVM tests for a change of one component, which made a push take most of an hour and turned host load into failed pushes. The component selection of CI was already the agreed exception of rule 1 of chapter 23; the hook now uses the same map, and the tools gates, which the map does not cover, get their own conservative switch. A gate the hook left out that fails in CI is a gap of the map, fixed in the map with a test (docs/quality/push-discipline.md). |

## M1-20 Idempotency keys

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-20/1 | 2026-10-12 | Fingerprint (`request_sha256`): SHA-256 over the upper-case method, a line feed, `r.URL.EscapedPath()` (with `/api/v1`, without query), a line feed and the canonical body: the RFC 8785 form when the body is one JSON value without a duplicate member (`platform/jsoncanon` over `github.com/gowebpki/jcs` v1.0.2, Apache-2.0), the raw bytes otherwise, empty without a body. Recorded in 06 "Idempotency" point 6. | 04 and 03 name "method, path and canonical body"; RFC 8785 makes member order, whitespace and escapes irrelevant, so a client may re-serialise its outbox entry. The raw bytes keep a malformed body comparable, and StageBody answers it with a final `400` that is stored under the same fingerprint. |
| M1-20/2 | 2026-10-12 | The header has no effect on an operation without the parameter `IdempotencyKey` and for the anonymous actor; the request runs as without it. Where it acts, a value that is not one UUIDv7 is `400 validation_failed` (`#header/Idempotency-Key`, `invalid_format`) and nothing is stored. Recorded in 06 "Idempotency" point 6. | `idempotency_keys.principal_id` references `principals`, and no public operation declares the parameter; storing per anonymous client would key on nothing stable. |
| M1-20/3 | 2026-10-12 | `jsoncanon.Canonicalize` refuses an integer literal (no fraction, no exponent) that a double cannot hold exactly; the fingerprint then uses the raw bytes. Literals with a fraction or an exponent are rounded as RFC 8785 says. | RFC 8785 rounds every number to a double, so 2^64 - 1 and 2^64 - 2 written out as integers would share one fingerprint and the second request would get the first one's response instead of `422`. The API carries no such integer (ADR 0053), so the rule costs nothing for valid requests. |
| M1-20/4 | 2026-10-12 | The stage runs the rest of the chain in the outermost unit of work without `txn.RightsChange`. A keyed use case that needs `RightsChange` (M1-48 `createGrant`, `revokeGrant`, `addGroupMember`; M1-52 to M1-54) gets the internal error of `platform/pg` for a nested `RightsChange` until the ticket that builds the first such handler resolves it. Recommended there: the route metadata marks rights-changing operations and the stage opens its unit with `RightsChange`, or `platform/pg` lets a nested `RightsChange` run the rights begin hook when the unit has written nothing but the key row (the key row is the first and only lock of `idempotency_keys` in every unit, so taking `permission_version` after it cannot close a cycle). | 02 "Idempotency in the Unit of Work" makes the stage open the unit, and 02 "Flow" lets only the outermost unit declare `RightsChange`; the affected principals are known only inside the use case. Changing `kernel/txn` or `platform/pg` (M1-13) is outside the scope of this ticket, and no handler with `RightsChange` exists yet (`api/operations.yaml` enables no operation). |
| M1-20/5 | 2026-10-12 | A deadlock or lock timeout (SQLSTATE `40P01` or `55P03`, read through `SQLState()`) that reaches `WriteProblem` inside an attempt of the stage ends the attempt with that error, so the unit of work of the stage retries it. | Inside the stage the use case's unit of work joins the stage's one and returns the database error to its handler, which writes a problem; without this the retry of 02 "Lock order" would be lost for every keyed operation. Each attempt reads the body again and starts with an empty buffer (`TestRetry_ReinvokesWithFreshBuffer`, `TestRetry_HandlerDeadlockReachesTheUnitOfWork`). |
| M1-20/6 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed operation with a file body (multipart or octet-stream) answers `500 internal_error`; a keyed `2xx` whose body is not JSON rolls back and answers `500`; a final error without a JSON body is sent but not stored. | The generator refuses all three forms for the contract (`api/routes/gen`), so they can only come from a hand-built operation or a handler bug; failing closed never hashes an upload in memory and never stores a body that `jsonb` cannot hold. |
| M1-20/7 | 2026-10-12 | A stored success that carried a one-time secret is recognised by its body `{"one_time_secret": true}` with a `2xx` status; its replay is `409 conflict`, `reason: one_time_secret`, `hint: create a new one`, `Idempotent-Replay: true`. | The brief fixes the placeholder; the table has no column for the mark, and a contract response never consists of exactly this member. |
| M1-20/8 | 2026-10-12 | Generator checks for every keyed operation of `openapi.yaml`, enabled or not: no query parameter, request body absent or `application/json`, every `2xx` with `application/json` or no content, and a `2xx` with `ETag` has a required integer member `version` (also through `allOf`). | Brief "Generator checks"; checking all operations now keeps a later handler ticket from enabling an operation whose replay cannot be rebuilt (`TestRealSpec_KeyedOperationsReplayable`). |
| M1-20/9 | 2026-10-12 | Built on `feat/M1-20-idempotency-keys` from `int/m1-wave6` (`785916c`) in its own worktree; not merged locally, not pushed; the PR lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00007`. | The launcher's task overrides the brief's "merge locally into the base branch"; several tickets run in parallel, and the integration renumbers migrations if another ticket took the number (ADR 0007 rule 4). |
| M1-20/10 | 2026-10-12 | The method of `kernel/idempotency.Store` that claims a key is `Claim`, not `Begin` as in the brief's sketch; the outgoing port of sync names its insert `Insert`. | The gate rule `bothy-go-tx-only-in-platform-pg` (`.semgrep/`) refuses every `$X.Begin(...)` outside `platform/pg`, because a transaction begun elsewhere skips the phases; the claim begins no transaction, and a gate is not lowered for a name. |
| M1-20/11 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed `2xx` body runs through `SanitizeJSON` before `Store.Complete`: the stored body is the one the response writer sends (null members removed), and a body it would refuse (a null element, an integer beyond 2^53 - 1, not JSON) rolls the unit back and answers `500`. | Otherwise the effect would commit while the client gets `500` from the response writer of `StageRoute`, and every replay would answer `500` as well (`TestRefusedSuccess_RollsBack`). |
| M1-20/12 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed `2xx` without a check of the route's action in the check log (the test of `routeWriter.proven`, 02 9.3) rolls the unit back and is not stored; the response writer answers it with `500`. The generator checks of M1-20/8 also cover a `2XX` range response. | Red team, first round, Major 1: the effect committed and the row was stored while the client got `500`, and the retry then received the held-back body as a replay, which 06 "Idempotency" point 4 allows only for an answer the client was already allowed to get (`TestUnprovenSuccess_RollsBack`). The range case was a Minor of the same round (`TestGenerate_RejectsKeyedOperationWithNonJSONRangeResponse`). |
| M1-20/13 | 2026-10-13 | Port onto main: the chapter references name the English section titles (02 "Idempotency in the Unit of Work", "Lock order", "Rules for jobs", "Job catalogue", 04 "All other writes", 06 "Idempotency"), `jsoncanon_test.go` is listed in `tools/docs/language-allow.txt`, and the migration keeps `00007` with a checksum over the English comments. | The sorting example of [RFC 8785](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8785) section 3.2.3 (retrieved 2026-10-13) has a key with U+00F6, which the language gate refuses even escaped; the test keeps the published vector byte for byte. |
| M1-20/14 | 2026-10-14 | `sonar.coverage.exclusions` also leaves the test helper packages of the platform layer, `services/server/internal/platform/*/*test/` (`httpservertest`, `pgtest`, `jobstest`), out of the coverage measure, as M3-01/20 does for the test helpers of the modules; `sync/app` gets unit tests for the idempotency store and the cleanup. | The port lands after M3-01, and the analysis counted the new harness code of `httpservertest` as uncovered new code, although only tests of other packages call it; the store and the cleanup were covered only through the tests of other packages. |

## M1-29 Rights changes, access.changed and invariants I1 and SR8

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-29/1 | 2026-10-12 | `access.changed` is written only for an affected principal whose difference is non-empty; a change without visible effect still raises the rights version. | Ruling of the brief: later events carry the version in the envelope; an empty event would only make clients reload. |
| M1-29/2 | 2026-10-12 | I1 is checked for all active personal agents on every rights change. | Ruling of the brief: 10 section 5.1 names the minimum per action; the superset cannot miss a case, and 03 section 6.4 already asks the check for shrinking changes. |
| M1-29/3 | 2026-10-12 | The declaration of the brief is `txn.RightsChangeSpec` with the option `txn.DeclareRightsChange`; action and resource kinds are strings and `txn.Ref` names resources and targets. `txn.RightsChange(principals...)` stays and adds up with it; `Options.Covers` also requires the targets and the action of a nested unit. `RightsChangeOf` and `InRightsChange` read the declaration. | `RightsChange` is already the name of the option, and kernel ports never import each other (go-arch-lint: `kernel/txn` may not import `kernel/authorize`). |
| M1-29/4 | 2026-10-12 | The hooks of phase 1 and 3.1 are the method values `Begin` and `BeforeCommit` of `authzapp.RightsHook`, which also implements `authz.RightsChanges.AddAffected` and `VerifyInvariants`. The appender of sync and the recorder of audit are bound after the unit of work exists (`Bind`, start step `authz`); an unbound hook fails every rights change with kind internal. | The recorder writes through the unit of work whose registry holds the hook, so it cannot exist when the registry is sealed; failing closed keeps a rights change from committing without its event and audit. |
| M1-29/5 | 2026-10-12 | The deny entry of a violation goes to `Recorder.RecordDenial`, which aggregates per `audit.deny_aggregate_window` and writes in its own transaction after the rollback. `last_owner` wins over `i1_violation` when both hold. | Brief "Deny audit entry"; without an owner no personal agent can be in order either, and AZ-M26 expects `last_owner`. |
| M1-29/6 | 2026-10-12 | The view of a principal (the input of `access.changed`) counts a person as in a session: the owner role takes effect for an active owner. dm channels are not part of it; a space is visible with read on it or on one of its channels. The input type of `DiffVisible` is `domain.AccessView`. | The view is what the person sees in the app; rights changes never change dm participation; `domain.Snapshot` is the rights snapshot of M1-28 (merged names win). |
| M1-29/7 | 2026-10-12 | For declared targets authz derives the affected principals from the grants in effect on the instance, the target, its space and, for a space, its channels, with group members, the active owners and the effective operators of the channels. Fixed thread operators are not added. | The difference step drops everyone without a change, so the superset is safe; the view of an agent on a channel never depends on a thread it operates. |
| M1-29/8 | 2026-10-12 | `StructureSource.ServedTargets` returns `authz.ChannelRef{ChannelID, SpaceID}`, with a zero `ChannelID` for a space whose default operator is the agent; `StructureSource.SpaceArchived` and `ChannelFacts.Archived` (channel or space archived) are added. `Effective` caps every level at read on an archived channel, owners included, and `Decide` answers a requirement above read there with `channel_archived` (07 section 4.5 point 1). chat fills them from M1-52 to M1-54. | 03 section 6.4 point 3 needs spaces without a channel; test 10a of the brief caps a channel at read through the target path, and the cap is rights logic, which lives only in authz (B7). `grant.create` must refuse archived spaces. |
| M1-29/9 | 2026-10-12 | `AgentPolicySource.PersonalAgents` returns `authz.PersonalAgent{AgentID, CredentialHolderID}`. | `authz.AgentPolicy` already exists (M1-28) with other fields. |
| M1-29/10 | 2026-10-12 | The store refuses writes of memberships and grants and `SoftDeleteGroup` outside a unit declared as a rights change; `InsertGroup` and the new `UpdateGroup` are no rights change. New store reads: `ActiveTriggerGrants`, `ActiveGrantsOnTargets`, `ActiveMembershipsOfGroups`, `MembershipsOfUserInGroup`. | The store doc of M1-27 left the enforcement to M1-29; `group.create` and `group.update` have "Permissions" `no` in 05. |
| M1-29/11 | 2026-10-12 | Inside a unit declared as a rights change the Authorizer neither reads nor fills the snapshot cache, the version memo or the scope memo. | The unit sees its own uncommitted version; an entry cached under it would survive a rollback and could serve another change that commits the same number. |
| M1-29/12 | 2026-10-12 | `grant.revoke` resolves a revoked grant like the stored grant (visibility and the delegation guard decide); every other action still finds no revoked grant. | 06 section 2 "No effect": revoking a revoked grant succeeds without change, which `not_found` would contradict. |
| M1-29/13 | 2026-10-12 | Admin calls that may be without effect (`DeleteGroup`, `RemoveGroupMember`, `RevokeGrant`) decide that in a first unit without rights change and run the change in a second unit declared as one, which checks everything again under the lock. When that second unit finds the call without effect after all (a concurrent call did the change first), it rolls back, so no rights version is raised, and the first unit runs again to record the call without effect. An `UpdateGroup` without change audits `no_change: true` too. | No rights version for a call without effect (R-API-27), while the change itself still runs fully under the lock of `permission_version`. |
| M1-29/14 | 2026-10-12 | `AddGroupMember` closes a membership that is not removed but expired (removed_at, removed_by) before inserting the new one; a membership in effect is `409 conflict`. Claim groups: rename, add and remove are `409 conflict` with reason `claim_managed_group`; the description may change. | The unique index allows one row without `removed_at` per person and group; 07 section 2.6 names the reason for claim groups. |
| M1-29/15 | 2026-10-12 | `CreateGrant` answers a host target with `404` until M2-39 brings hosts; an archived space or channel is `409 conflict` with reason `channel_archived`, checked after `authorize` so an invisible target stays `404`. An optional revoke or delete reason is checked with SEC-IN-4 and audited as `ReasonText`. | No host can exist before M2-39; the order keeps 02 section 9.2. |
| M1-29/16 | 2026-10-12 | `AdminService` takes the caller as `actor.Actor` and puts it into the context for the recorder and the events; a different principal already in the context is an internal error. Group text limits come through `AdminDeps.Limits`; the keys `group.name_max_chars` and `group.description_max_chars` are bound when M1-48 wires the service with its routes. | The recorder reads the actor from the context only (M1-24); the brief adds no configuration key. |
| M1-29/17 | 2026-10-12 | `errs.ProblemExtender` members are merged by the problem writer in name order after the standard members; a standard name (also an absent one) is never taken, nil members and nested nulls are dropped, an internal error carries none. | 10 section 5.4 and ADR 0053. |
| M1-29/18 | 2026-10-12 | The integration tests use PostgreSQL 18 with the real identity PrincipalSource, the real unit of work, sync and audit, and the fakes of structure, agent policy and credentials. Owner, status and operator changes of later tickets are test use cases that change SQL rows or the fakes inside the transaction and declare the action. A personal token of the owner gets `owner_only` for owner actions (SR5), not `step_up_not_possible`. | The brief's test setup; the requirement step decides before the step-up step (M1-28). |
| M1-29/19 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave6` at `785916c`, branch `feat/M1-29-authz-invariants` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language. |
| M1-29/20 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (verdict JA, four minors): the raced call without effect no longer raises the rights version (M1-29/13, `TestRevokeGrant_RacedRevocationIsWithoutEffect`); every view of one change is computed at the time of `Begin`, so a grant that expires during the unit is reported by the expiry sweep and not as part of this change, while `VerifyInvariants` reads the clock at the check; the example of `txn.UnitOfWork.Do` declares with `DeclareRightsChange`. The comment of `identity/app.Store.InsertOwner`, which still names `txn.RightsChange`, stays for M1-31, because the brief allows only the adapter method in `internal/identity`. Closing an expired membership with the caller as `removed_by` stays (M1-29/14). | Findings of the red team fixed in the same ticket where they are in scope. |
| M1-29/21 | 2026-10-13 | Port onto main: the start step `sync` runs before `authz` (6b sync, 6c authz), and `TestAZ_P7_CoversRule16` reads rule 6 of `docs/spec/05-actions.md` from "I1 is checked transactionally on" to "check in [03-data-model.md]". | `startAuthz` binds the rights hooks to the appender of sync and fails without it; main had built authz first because nothing needed the event log there before. The chapter is English on main. |

## M1-33 Devices, device keys and DPoP verification

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-33/1 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave6` at `785916c`, branch `feat/M1-33-identity-devices-dpop` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in the module README (its German headings stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language. |
| M1-33/2 | 2026-10-12 | `listUserDevices` authorizes `device.list` on the resource kind `device` without an id and with `OwnerID` = the path person, not on the kind `user` of the brief's table. An unknown person is `not_found` before `authorize`; `listMyDevices` keeps the brief's resource `user` = caller and `getDevice` the device itself. | With the kind `user` another person is visible (people see the directory) and the requirement `self or owner` fails with `403 forbidden`, which breaks the brief's outcome column and 05 rule 2 ("No permission to see gives 404"). Devices are owned resources (M1-28/D4): visible to their person and to effective owners only, so the device kind gives `404` from `authz` itself and no rights logic moves out of `authz`. The pending rule still answers `403 device_pending` for the own list from a pending device. |
| M1-33/3 | 2026-10-12 | A missing device or person and an invisible one answer the same `404 not_found` without a detail (`app.invisible`). | 02 section 9.2 and 05 rule 2: the bodies must not differ (`TestGetDevice_PendingOwnOnly`, `TestListUserDevices_SelfOwnerOther`). |
| M1-33/4 | 2026-10-12 | `identity.Device` and the API response leave out `verification_code`; M1-38 adds it for the pending device itself. | 08 section 5.2 derives the code from the token pepper and `auth.device_verification_code_digits`, and the brief M1-38 owns approval with the code and its response mapping. |
| M1-33/5 | 2026-10-12 | `TestCredentialSource_Devices` checks active, pending, revoked, a pending device older than any pending window (stays `pending`), a foreign principal and an unknown device (`unknown`). Session and token facts stay not active until M1-34 and M1-44, so a request that names a session or token is unauthenticated (fail closed). | Devices have no expiry of their own; treating a pending device past `auth.device_pending_ttl` as revoked belongs to M1-38 (its brief, design notes of authentication). The brief's word "expired" is covered as the old pending device. |
| M1-33/6 | 2026-10-12 | `kernel/jose` bounds JSON nesting with the constant `MaxJSONDepth = 32`, the default of `http.max_json_depth`. | The kernel reads no configuration and `ParseCompactJWS` has a fixed signature; JOSE objects of the profile nest at most three levels, so the bound only stops hostile input. |
| M1-33/7 | 2026-10-12 | The JWS vectors of ADR 0067 run as `TestJWS_RFC7515ExampleES256` (appendix A.3 through `ParseCompactJWS` and `Verify`) and `TestVerify_RFC8037ExampleEdDSA` (appendix A.4 through the internal signature check, because its payload is plain text and the profile accepts JSON objects only). The thumbprint vectors are those of the brief (RFC 9449 and RFC 8037); the RSA vector of RFC 7638 section 3.1 comes with RSA in M1-41. | The profile has no RSA key type yet; every vector the profile can express is tested. The literal segments stand apart in the test source so that the secret scanner (rule `jwt`) does not take the public examples for tokens; the fuzz seed files write the leading `e` of each segment as `\x65` for the same reason. |
| M1-33/8 | 2026-10-12 | `kernel/jose` has a few exported helpers beyond the brief: `JWKFromPublicKey`, `JWK.Canonical`, `JWK.MarshalJSON`, `JWK.PublicKey`, `ES256Signer`, `EdDSASigner`, `Header`, `AlgES256`, `AlgEdDSA`, `ErrInvalidJWK`, `ErrInvalidJWS`, `ErrSignature`. `ParseCompactJWS` with an empty `typ` checks no type; `zip` is refused besides `crit`, `jku`, `x5u`, `x5c`. | The test helpers, the stored canonical JWK and the token issuers of M1-34 need them; ADR 0067 item 2 lists `zip`. An empty `typ` exists for foreign tokens without one (the RFC 7515 example). |
| M1-33/9 | 2026-10-12 | `device_keys.public_jwk` stores the canonical public JWK (only `crv`, `kty`, `x`, `y`); the attestation is stored as received. The repository has `KeyByJKT` beyond the port for the login tickets. | The canonical form is exactly what the thumbprint covers, so no unknown member of a client survives; 08 section 6.1 keeps the attestation unchanged. |
| M1-33/10 | 2026-10-12 | Device registration also applies SEC-IN-4 to `app_version`, rejects an approver on a pending registration, and has no length limit for the name. | Both are display texts of the device list; an approver only belongs to an active device (03 `approved_by`). No configuration key names a device name limit, so none is invented; the body limit bounds the input. |
| M1-33/11 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy_app` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `devices` and `device_keys`, no `DELETE`. | Revocation sets state and `revoked_at`; the rows are security history (retention class `security`), and the retention job of that class grants its delete in its own migration. |
| M1-33/12 | 2026-10-12 | The jti store keeps no size cap; an insert looks at up to four entries of its shard and removes the expired ones, and a sweeper removes every expired entry each `auth.dpop_proof_max_age` (stop step `dpop_replay_sweeper`, phase HTTP). | 08 section 7.3 names no limit (the window and the rate limits bound it); a full scan per insert would make inserts linear in the shard size. The window length is the interval after which every entry of a window has expired. |
| M1-33/13 | 2026-10-12 | The token pepper is resolved once at the start (step 6d `identity`, after `authz`), hex-decoded and must have 32 bytes, or the start ends. `secrets init` already creates it (`token_pepper`, generator `key`), so M1-09 needs no change. | A missing or malformed pepper must fail the start, not the first request; rotating the pepper changes the nonce key and needs a restart, which only invalidates outstanding nonces (clients retry with the new one). |

| M1-33/16 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.Handlers` embeds `*httpapi.Devices`; the start fills a nil module handler with the one built in step 6d. | M1-33/17 | 2026-10-12 | The DPoP table test is `TestDPoP_IDT14` with one subtest per case (plus a missing header, a header `jwk` of another key and a header without `jwk`); `TestRegister_Validation` runs in `domain/device`, `TestRegister_Persists` and the rules that need the store in `app`. | One table keeps the expected code next to each case; the domain rules need no database. |
| M1-33/18 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.NewChain` takes the request validator of the enabled operations from `enabledValidator`, compiled once per process (`sync.OnceValues`). | With three enabled operations the validator warms their response schemas; under `-race` and a host load above 50 every test server of `internal/wire` paid that again, so readiness waits of 30 s timed out (a timing probe measured 37 s per build on the head and 9.6 s on the base at load 58). ## M1-30 Expiry of grants and memberships

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-30/1 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave7` at `8e30539`, branch `feat/M1-30-authz-expiry` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-30/2 | 2026-10-12 | No migration: `00005_authz_groups_grants.sql` (M1-27) already has `expiry_reminded_at` and `expiry_reported_at` on both tables, both `(expires_at)` indexes and the trigger that sets either grant column once. Only new sqlc queries (`queries/expiry.sql`). | Scope 1 of the brief: add only what the merged migration lacks. |
| M1-30/3 | 2026-10-12 | The jobs are `app.ExpiryService` (`Sweep`, `Remind`) in `app/expiry.go`; their arguments, handlers and `Register` live in the new package `adapters/jobs`, which `internal/wire` calls from `NewJobRegistry`. The selections and marks are the separate port `app.ExpiryStore`, implemented by the PostgreSQL repository and also needed by the rights hook (`RightsHookDeps.Expiry`); the in-memory fake store does not implement it. | `app` may not import `internal/platform` (only adapters may); `sync/adapters/jobs` is the same pattern. A separate port keeps the existing `Store` and its fake unchanged. |
| M1-30/4 | 2026-10-12 | The reporting of an expiry (selection, marker, `grant.changed` or `group.changed`, the audit entry, the views before) runs in the rights begin hook right after `permission_version` is locked, with the time of the change; the events wait for phase 3.1, where the appender accepts them. A sweep batch passes its kind and limit through the context and its work phase only reads `RightsHook.Folded`. The deny entry of a violation in a sweep batch names the resource kind (`grant` or `group`) without id, because a batch has many rows. | One clock and one lock for the selection and both views; the appender refuses events in phase 1. |
| M1-30/5 | 2026-10-12 | Every rights change folds the due, unreported expiries in before anything else (M1-30/4), not only the sweep: they are reported with event, audit entry and marker by the first unit after the expiry, and its `access.changed` carries the action of that unit. The views treat rows not due yet (and in a sweep batch the rows of a later batch) as not expired (`domain.AssumeValid.Unreported`). The brief's `AddAffectedAssumingValid` is not built: the fold records the views before itself. Outside the sweep the fold is not bounded by `authz.expiry_sweep_batch_max`. | A view must be what the client was told before the change and what `authorize` decides after it. With the expiry left to the sweep, three red team rounds reproduced lost losses and a reported gain that `authorize` denies (a membership and its group grant expiring together, a change between an expiry and the sweep, a new grant to a group of an expired member). The sweep runs every `authz.expiry_sweep_interval`, so a change folds the rows of at most one interval unless the worker was down. |
| M1-30/6 | 2026-10-12 | An invariant violation in a batch is logged as ERROR `expiry batch rejected by an invariant` with `bothy.action` and `error.type` (`i1_violation` or `last_owner`); the agent ids are in the deny audit entry the rights hook records. | The log handlers drop every attribute outside the signals catalogue, which has no agent id; the audit entry is the durable record. |
| M1-30/7 | 2026-10-12 | The reminder excludes grants of deleted groups and memberships of deleted groups in the selection itself. | A row skipped after the selection would never be marked and would fill every batch again. |
| M1-30/8 | 2026-10-12 | `access.expiring` carries `target` (kind, id, level, capabilities) only for space and channel grants; it is absent for the instance and for hosts. | Brief Scope 4.3 and 07 section 9.4. The schema `AccessTargetKind` also knows `host` for `access.expiring`; host grants exist from M2-39, whose ticket decides whether a host reminder names its target. |
| M1-30/9 | 2026-10-12 | `group.changed` carries the group with `member_count` = the memberships in effect at the time of the change, counted in phase 3.1 after the use case wrote its own memberships. | `Group.member_count` is required; counting at commit keeps it right when a change that adds or removes members also folds an expiry of the same group. |
| M1-30/10 | 2026-10-12 | `internal/wire` builds the service with the system clock. | A sweep batch uses the clock of the rights hook (M1-30/4); the reminder reads its own once per batch. |
| M1-30/11 | 2026-10-12 | `authz.Register` now declares `grant.changed`, `group.changed` and `access.expiring` next to `access.changed`. | authz appends them from this ticket on; the registration check rejects a second module for them. |
| M1-30/12 | 2026-10-12 | Configuration: `authz.expiry_sweep_interval` (duration, default `1m`, at least `1s`), `authz.expiry_reminder_lead` (duration, `48h`, at least `1s`) and `authz.expiry_sweep_batch_max` (int, `500`, 1 to 2147483647, the range of the SQL limit parameter). | 07 section 14 gives the defaults; the bounds keep every value usable. |
| M1-30/13 | 2026-10-12 | Recipients of `access.expiring` follow 07 section 9.4 and the brief: for a grant to a group all active owners and `granted_by`. The note of `access.expiring` in 05 section 6 (generated from `actions.yaml`: "to the subject (for groups, the person)") is not changed here. | The brief rules this recipient set; the catalogue note is outside the files this ticket may change and goes to the specification lane. |
| M1-30/14 | 2026-10-12 | Both jobs are registered in the one job registry every role builds (as `sync.partition_maintenance` and `sync.idempotency_cleanup`); only `worker` and `all` run handlers, because only they start River (M1-14). The registration tests check queue, period and arguments, not the role. | An `api` process must accept exactly the kinds a worker runs; the role split is the job runtime's and is tested there. |
| M1-30/15 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (NEIN, one reproduced major): fixed through `AssumeValid.Unreported` (M1-30/5) with `TestExpirySweep_MembershipAndGroupGrantTogether` and `TestExpirySweep_TwoGrantsOnOneTargetInTwoBatches`. Minors fixed: the host target follows 07 (M1-30/8), the active owners are read once per reminder batch, and adapter tests cover the membership selections and marks. Minors kept: the invariant log without agent ids (M1-30/6); `grant.changed` of a channel target carries no `space_id` while `internal/wire` passes the empty structure source (until M1-53, as for the rights hook of M1-29). | Findings of the red team fixed in the same ticket where they are in scope. |
| M1-30/16 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 2 (NEIN, one reproduced major of the same class: a revocation or removal between an expiry and the sweep reported nothing, and a new grant in that window reported an `added` the client already had): fixed in one place, the views of the rights hook (M1-30/5), with the regression tests `TestExpiryWindow_*`. | The second finding of the same class: fixed for the class, not the instance. |
| M1-30/17 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 3 (NEIN, one reproduced major: a new grant to a group whose membership had expired unreported told the expired member `added` for a target `authorize` denies): fixed for the class by folding due expiries into every rights change (M1-30/4, /5), with `TestExpiryWindow_NoPhantomGain`, `TestExpiryWindow_GroupDeleteAndReAdd` and the changed `TestExpiryWindow_NewGrantAfterExpiry`. `TestExpirySweep_ExactlyOnce` makes its failing run with a vetoing subscriber of `authorize.AccessChanged`, because the sweep's events now go through the appender of the rights hook. | Third finding in the same area: the mechanism now has one rule (report a due expiry before computing any view) instead of assumptions in the views. |
| M1-30/18 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 4 (NEIN, one reproduced major: an expiry folded into a person's rights change was audited with that person as actor, with device, session and IP pseudonym): the audit entries of folded expiries are written from a context without the request principal and without the client IP (`systemContext`, the values are hidden by their types because the keys of `kernel/actor` and `kernel/reqmeta` are unexported); the correlation id of the change stays, so the entry points to the request that folded it. Test `TestExpiryWindow_FoldAuditIsSystem`. Minor fixed: `member_count` (M1-30/9). Minor kept: a denied admin request folds before its use case authorizes and then rolls the fold back; correct, only work. | System actions never pretend a human did them (05 rule 1.10, R-AZ-15); the brief asks the audit row without actor. |
| M1-30/19 | 2026-10-13 | Port onto main: chapter references name the English files (07-permissions.md, 03-data-model.md), and the background job rows of the authz README are English. | The specification is English on main; no behaviour changes. |

## M1-31 People, owners and own profile

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-31/1 | 2026-10-12 | Built on `feat/M1-31-identity-people` from `int/m1-wave7` (`8e30539`) in its own worktree; not merged locally, not pushed; the PR lane ports it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's local merge and `git worktree remove`. |
| M1-31/2 | 2026-10-12 | Resolves M1-20/4: the Idempotency-Key stage opens its unit with `txn.AllowLateRightsChange()`. The first nested `Do` that declares a rights change in such a unit runs the rights begin hook, its function and the rights check hook in this order inside the ambient transaction (`txn.UnitControl.Declare`, `platform/pg` `lateRightsChange`); sync and audit run at the end of the outer unit; a second rights change in the same unit is an internal error. | The stage has written only its key row when the use case starts, so `permission_version` is still locked before any domain row (03 "Lock order"), and only the use case knows the affected principals. Running the check at the end of the nested unit hands `i1_violation` and `last_owner` to the handler, so the stage stores them like any final `409`. |
| M1-31/3 | 2026-10-12 | The actor-based use cases are the ports `identity.People` and `identity.Owners` with the DTOs of the brief (`UserView` and `OwnerView` serve as user and owner entry); `identity.Directory` stays the actor-free read port of other modules. | The brief adds the methods to `Directory`, whose contract (M1-26) is "does not call authorize"; two meanings in one interface would trap the modules that use it. Only the names differ. |
| M1-31/4 | 2026-10-12 | `listUsers` and `listOwners` authorize `user.read` on the resource kind `user` without id and with `OwnerID` = the caller, not on the instance. | The catalogue gives `user.read` only the kind `user` (`authorize` fails closed with an internal error for `instance`). The descriptor owned by the caller is visible to every caller (M1-28/D4), so the requirement "human; apps with scope" decides: people and apps read the directory, an agent gets `403 forbidden` (`subject_kind_not_allowed`) as test 11 asks. |
| M1-31/5 | 2026-10-12 | An owner with a personal token gets `403 forbidden` with `reason: owner_only` for the five owner writes, not `step_up_not_possible` as the brief's test 2 states. | Same as M1-29/18: the requirement step decides before the step-up step, and with a token the owner role does not take effect (SR5). The rights logic stays in authz. |
| M1-31/6 | 2026-10-12 | `updateMyProfile` by an agent or app is `403 forbidden` with `reason: subject_kind_not_allowed`, decided by identity after `authorize` allowed `self.profile.update`. | The requirement of `self.profile.update` is `self`, which an agent naming itself meets; the profile is a row of `users`, which only people have (03 users), so the use case refuses the kind like a missing resource of the wrong kind, with the reason code of 07 section 4.1. |
| M1-31/7 | 2026-10-12 | `createUser` emits `user.updated`; `addOwner` for a person that is not active is `409 conflict` (`reason: principal_not_active`), for an owner `409 conflict` (`reason: already_owner`); `enableUser` on an active person is an audited call without effect (`200`, `no_change: true`, no event, no rights version); `revokeOwner` without an active owners row is `404`. | Rulings of the brief; the reasons name the cause (06 section 5 point 3). |
| M1-31/8 | 2026-10-12 | `local_account: true` on `createUser` is `400 validation_failed` with pointer `/local_account` and code `not_enabled` until M1-37 adds enrollment codes; M1-37 replaces this rule. | Interim rule of the brief; `not_enabled` is the field code of 06 section 5 point 3 for a function this server version does not build yet. |
| M1-31/9 | 2026-10-12 | `addOwner`, `revokeOwner` and `enableUser` authorize once per request in a first unit without rights change (also loading the resource and checking reason and state), then write in a second unit declared as the rights change, which locks the person (the owner changes also the active owners rows, in ascending id) and checks the state again but not the authorization. Every rights change is serialized by the lock on `permission_version` of the rights begin hook, so I1 and SR8 hold for `enableUser` without the owners lock. Two owners revoking each other at once give one `204` and one `409 last_owner`. A raced `enableUser` that finds the person already active answers as without effect (no event); inside the Idempotency-Key stage the rights version of that attempt may have been raised. | A request is judged by the rights at its start (R-RE-02, one rights version per request); SR8 and I1 guard the result under the lock (test 5 of the brief). A refused call never raises the rights version. |
| M1-31/10 | 2026-10-12 | `q` of `listUsers` uses `starts_with(lower(column), lower(q))` instead of `LIKE` with escaped `%` and `_`. | The same literal prefix match without an escape rule to get wrong; held by test 11. |
| M1-31/11 | 2026-10-12 | Locales are parsed by `adapters/locale` (`golang.org/x/text/language`, vendor group `xtext`, now a direct dependency of `go.mod`) behind the port `app.Locales`; the domain imports `time/tzdata` for `time.LoadLocation` on hosts without zoneinfo. A time zone must also have the canonical form of an IANA name (first a pattern; since red team round 2 the generated list `zoneNames` of `tools/zonegen`, see M1-31/17), because the server image does carry a system zoneinfo directory whose other files (`posixrules`, `right/...`, `posix/...`) and spellings (`Europe//Berlin`, `./UTC`, lower case on a case-insensitive file system) `time.LoadLocation` accepts too. | The module register grants vendors to adapters only (go-arch-lint `depOnAnyVendor: false`); x/text is BSD-3-Clause and was already in `go.sum`. Red team round 1, Major 4. |
| M1-31/12 | 2026-10-12 | `getMe` reads the rights version through `instance.Versions`, pins it with `authorize.WithPermissionVersion` and then authorizes `self.read`, so `permission_version` is exactly the version of the check. | The version of the Authorizer's request memo is private to authz; pinning needs no new port. |
| M1-31/13 | 2026-10-12 | Responses are validated by `httpservertest.ResponseValidator` over `github.com/pb33f/libopenapi-validator` (already the request validator's library, JSON Schema 2020-12 through `santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` inside it) and the harness option `ValidateResponses`, not by a new `test/apitest` package with a direct `santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema` dependency. The test authenticator `httpservertest.Actors` with `InSession` and `WithToken` builds the principals of the tests. | The brief allows "the response validator of the merged tree"; reusing the library of the request side adds no dependency and no component outside the module register. |
| M1-31/14 | 2026-10-12 | A handle with upper case is `400` (`pattern`) before uniqueness is checked, so "duplicate handle in other case" of test 8 is a `400`; a duplicate handle in the same case and a duplicate e-mail in any case are `409 conflict` (`/handle`, `/email`). | The check and the API pattern allow lower case only (M1-26); the unique index on `lower(email)` covers e-mail. |
| M1-31/15 | 2026-10-12 | The schema `User` has no e-mail member, so `getUser`, `listUsers`, the owner list and `user.updated` never carry an e-mail address; only `getMe` reports the caller's own. `audit.reason_max_chars` (19 SEC-AUD-5) is not enforced on the reasons of `enableUser`, `addOwner` and `revokeOwner`: the key is not registered yet and the brief adds no key; the body limit of the chain bounds them. | Contract of `docs/api/openapi.yaml`; the length rule belongs to the ticket that registers the key. |
| M1-31/16 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (`NEIN`, four Majors), fixed with a test each: (1) `GetUser`, `UpdateUser`, `EnableUser`, `AddOwner`, `RevokeOwner` and the device reads `getDevice` and `listUserDevices` of M1-33 call `authorize` before they answer a missing person, owner role or device with `404`, so an anonymous caller, a pending device or a person without owner role gets the same answer for an existing and an unknown id (`checkThenFound`; the second occurrence of the class, fixed for both tickets); (2) `q` of `listUsers` must be valid UTF-8 without control or bidi characters (`400`, `#query/q`), a cursor whose handle is no valid handle is `cursor_invalid`, and SQLSTATE `22021` maps to `400`; (3) a locale longer than 35 characters (RFC 5646 section 4.4.1) is `400 validation_failed` with the field code `too_long` before it is parsed; (4) the strict zone form of M1-31/11. Minors fixed: a failed late rights change fails the whole unit even when its caller swallows the error, and a nested unit the late declaration covers may join while it runs; an empty `locale` or `time_zone` on `createUser` is `400` like on `updateUser` (`CreateUserCmd` takes pointers); the privacy test captures the access log of `slog.Default`; the response validator test has the undeclared status. Not changed: REST times are truncated to milliseconds but encoded by `encoding/json` without trailing zeros (also in M1-33); 06 section 2 lets clients read every RFC 3339 form, and the encoding belongs to the generated layer of all operations, not to this ticket. | Rules of 07 section 4.2 and 02 section 9.2 (no existence oracle), SEC-T06 (no unexpected 5xx), SEC-IN-4. |
| M1-31/17 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 2 (`NEIN`: two Blockers, two Majors), fixed with a test each: (B1) the handler tests build one response validator per process, contract and operation list, over the contract pruned to the mounted operations like the request validator (`httpservertest.SharedResponseValidator`, validation serialized; one build under `-race` went from about 72 s to about 6 s and the package from 275 s to 55 s in this run), so the HTTP test package of identity stays far below the 10 minute limit of `go test -race`; (B2) the use cases, the people queries, `httpservertest` and `kernel/txn.UnitControl` have tests in their own packages (`identity/app/people_test.go`, `adapters/postgres/people_test.go`, `httpservertest_test.go`, `txn/late_test.go`), so the coverage of new code does not depend on tests of other packages; (M1) a cursor handle is checked against the handle pattern only, not `principal.handle_max_chars`, so a handle created under a larger limit still pages; (M2) a time zone must be one of the IANA names of the toolchain's tzdata, generated into `domain/zones.gen.go` by `tools/zonegen` (without `Factory`, which ICU refuses), which makes the rule the same on case-insensitive and case-sensitive hosts. Minors fixed: `UpdateUser` authorizes before it locks the row; a recovered panic in a late rights change fails the unit; the locale bound counts characters and its value is pinned by a test; the docs of the device reads, the owners lock of `enableUser` and the `CheckSearchPrefix` comment. M1-33/2 ("an unknown person is not_found before authorize") is superseded by M1-31/16 for `listUserDevices`. Not changed: a raced `enableUser` and the swallowed `errRaced` of authz (M1-48 must hand it on behind the stage, noted at `txn.AllowLateRightsChange`); the reason length of SEC-AUD-5 (M1-31/15); unchecked rights writes after a late rights change in the same unit (no caller; the rule is in the godoc). | Same rules as round 1; the test-time limit and Sonar's new-code coverage are gates of CI. |
| M1-31/18 | 2026-10-13 | The test binary of `internal/wire` builds the request validator of the enabled operations in `TestMain` (`WarmEnabledValidator`, `export_test.go`) before any test starts. | Second occurrence of the class of M1-33/18: with thirteen enabled operations the one build per process grew, and under `-race` and a host load around 25 the first serve tests (`TestServeTLSPublicListener`, `TestConsumerLagGaugesInApiRole`, `TestStart_*`) waited on it inside their 30 s readiness wait and timed out in `make check`, while they pass alone. Building it before the tests removes the build from every per-test deadline, whatever later tickets enable. |
| M1-31/19 | 2026-10-13 | Port onto main: the paragraph on rights changes behind the Idempotency-Key stage stands in 02 section 8.4 of the English chapter, and the chapter references name the English files and section titles (03 "Lock order"); M1-31/16 names a too long locale as `400 validation_failed` with the field code `too_long`. | The specification is English on main, and the problem code gate accepts only codes of 06 section 5; no behaviour changes. |
| M1-31/20 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: `tools/zonegen` takes the default zip from `$GOROOT`, which `go generate` sets for the generator, and is a usage error (exit 2) without `-zip` and `GOROOT`; it no longer starts `go env GOROOT` (`TestDefaultZipFromGOROOT`). | SonarQube Cloud rated the command started through `PATH` as a vulnerability on new code; the generator needs no command of its own. |

## M1-32 Signing keys, JWKS and rotation

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-32/1 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave7` at `8e30539`, branch `feat/M1-32-signing-keys` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs (their German parts stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language. |
| M1-32/2 | 2026-10-12 | `audit_checkpoint` is a valid purpose of `rotateSigningKey` (`200`), unlike the brief's test 7; `terminal` is refused with `400 validation_failed` (`/purpose`, `not_supported`) until M2-39 manages it. | `SigningKeyPurpose` in openapi.yaml lists `audit_checkpoint` and `terminal`, and 19 section 10 rotates `audit_checkpoint` keys only on owner request through `signing_key.rotate`; without it a leaked checkpoint key (case D of the disaster recovery runbook, "rotate all purposes") could never be replaced. The brief's premise that the schema lacks the purpose is wrong for the specification it cites. |
| M1-32/3 | 2026-10-12 | An owner with a personal token gets `403 forbidden` with `reason: owner_only`, not `step_up_not_possible` as the brief's test 7 says. | 07 section 4.2 checks the requirement (step 6) before step-up (step 8), and a personal token never carries the owner role (SR5, `authz.PrincipalFacts.IsOwner`), so `authz` (M1-28) refuses at step 6. Both answers are `403`; changing the order of `authz` is outside this ticket. |
| M1-32/4 | 2026-10-12 | Audit details carry a `kid` (`new_kid`, `previous_kid`, `kid`) as the hex form of the thumbprint, 64 lower-case characters (`app.KIDCode`). | A `kid` is base64url with upper-case letters, which `audit_details.v1` refuses in a code (SEC-AUD-5: `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.:-]*$`); hex is the same SHA-256 value and converts back without loss, and the schema needs no new value kind. |
| M1-32/5 | 2026-10-12 | `Rotate`, the job and `EnsureActive` run their key transaction in a unit of work of their own (`txn.Detach`), also inside the Idempotency-Key stage. | Private files of retired keys are deleted after the commit; inside the stage's transaction a later rollback would restore rows whose files are gone. A repeat after a failed store of the response repeats the rotation: `scheduled` returns the same `next` key, `emergency` creates one more key, both harmless. |
| M1-32/6 | 2026-10-12 | `getJwks` authorizes `instance.discovery.read` for the principal of the request (anonymous allowed) in `Keys.JWKS`. | The proof of the check (02 "Proof of the check") turns any successful response without a check of the route's action into `500 internal_error` (cause `authz_not_checked` in log and metric). |
| M1-32/7 | 2026-10-12 | `EnsureActive` retires every unretired key whose private file is missing and creates a fresh active key when none is left (`domain.LostTransitions`), logging `signing key file missing, key retired` at level error. Narrowed by M1-32/16: a key store that holds none of the unretired keys is refused. | After a restore without the key store (case B, an old bundle) the restored rows name files that do not exist: without the repair no access token could be signed, so no owner could sign in to rotate. 16 section 5.4 already names the consequence of lost signing keys (tokens invalid, devices sign in again). Every role must mount the same key store; the README says so. |
| M1-32/8 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy secrets export` adds the key store under `signing-keys/` with the flag `--signing-key-dir` (default `/var/lib/bothy/signing-keys`, skipped with a note on stderr when the default does not exist); `secrets init` skips `signing-keys/` in its source. | The command reads no configuration, so the default of `secrets.signing_key_dir` is repeated; Compose mounts the key store read-only in `secrets-init` at that path. A decrypted bundle is the source of `init` in case B, which would otherwise refuse the key files as unknown. |
| M1-32/9 | 2026-10-12 | The private key of a loaded key sits in a `secrets.Value` (the seed); the expanded key exists only during `Sign`. `Signer` checks that the loaded key belongs to the public key of the row. | SEC-SEC-3 and the redacting type of M1-09; a swapped file must not sign under another `kid`. |
| M1-32/10 | 2026-10-12 | New telemetry attributes `bothy.signing_key.purpose`, `bothy.signing_key.kid`, `bothy.signing_key.from_state`, `bothy.signing_key.to_state` (logs of key transitions only). | The log handlers drop every key outside the catalog; the brief asks for one line per transition with purpose, `kid` and both states. |
| M1-32/11 | 2026-10-12 | `Provider.Put` and `Provider.Delete` return `error` only and take a parsed `secrets.Ref` and a `secrets.Value`; the file driver implements `Writer` (with `Delete`). Writes are allowed only directly in `secrets.signing_key_dir`, not in subdirectories. | The names of M1-09 win over the brief's sketch (`ref string`, `Secret`); the key files never need a subdirectory, so the narrower rule costs nothing. |
| M1-32/12 | 2026-10-12 | The migration is `00010_server_signing_keys.sql` with the table exactly as 03, including the purpose `terminal` in its check and the EdDSA check for `invocation`, `terminal` and `webhook_out`. | Next number on the base; PLAN row "Addendum of 2026-10-10" asks M1-32 to create the purpose `terminal` with its table without using it. |
| M1-32/13 | 2026-10-12 | When the plan of a key change, recomputed under the advisory lock, asks for a key that was not generated before (another process changed the keys between the read and the lock), the transaction rolls back, a key is generated and the change runs again, at most three times, then `409 conflict`. | Red team round 1, major 1: `EnsureActive` could leave a purpose without an active key and still succeed (`TestEnsureActive_LostNextPromotedConcurrently`). |
| M1-32/14 | 2026-10-12 | The readiness check `signing_keys` also fails for a purpose whose active key cannot sign (unreadable private file, or a file of another key), through `Keys.Unready`. | Red team round 1, minor: a key store with unreadable files (a changed `secrets.signing_key_dir`, a role without the volume) looked ready while `Signer` failed. |
| M1-32/15 | 2026-10-13 | Port onto main: the migration keeps `00010_server_signing_keys.sql` with a checksum over the English comments, the chapter references name the English files and section titles, the start check of the key store stands in step 2 and the retirement of keys without a file in step 6 of 02 section 15.1, and M1-32/6 names the problem as `500 internal_error` with the cause `authz_not_checked`. | The specification is English on main, and the problem code gate accepts only codes of 06 section 5; no behaviour changes. |
| M1-32/16 | 2026-10-19 | `EnsureActive` first checks the whole key store: when the database has unretired keys and `secrets.signing_key_dir` holds the private file of none of them, the start ends with an error naming `secrets.signing_key_dir` and `signing_key.accept_lost_keys` and changes no key. Only with the new key `signing_key.accept_lost_keys = true` (default `false`, 19 section 16) does the start retire them and create fresh keys as in M1-32/7. A partial loss (some files present) keeps the automatic repair; a file that exists but cannot be read counts as present. | CodeRabbit on the pull request of M1-32, reproduced with `TestEnsureActive_EmptyKeyStoreKeepsKeys`: one role on a new or mistyped volume retired every key of all purposes, which cannot be undone, signed out every device and left the other roles with active rows whose files they lack. Chapter 02 (start step 6) and disaster recovery case B described the retirement as the consequence of a restore without the key store; the steering decided to fail closed and keep the stored keys, and both now name the consent key; the consent key keeps case B with a bundle without `signing-keys/` possible. |

## M1-34 Sessions, access tokens, refresh families and logout

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-34/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave8` at `f6c34de`, branch `feat/M1-34-identity-sessions` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs (their German parts stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language. |
| M1-34/2 | 2026-10-13 | Session, device and key state are read per request, not cached: the Authenticator reads session, device and the device's active `dpop` key in one indexed query and reports `SessionActive`, `DeviceState` and `DeviceKeyActive` in the actor; `authz` takes these facts through the optional port `authorize.CredentialObserver` in `check_active` instead of the facts it cached for the rights version. A principal without observed facts (API tokens until M1-44, test principals) keeps the credential source. Logout and refresh reuse do not raise `permission_version`. 04 section 8.5 ("a cache that is tied to the permission version") holds for grants; for sessions and devices of people this ruling applies. | The brief's ruling: revocation then works across processes at once (AZ-P9 for sessions, `TestAuthenticate_RevocationAcrossInstances`) without a rights change for every logout; the cost is one indexed query per authenticated request. A session never comes back, so the cached flag could only be stale in the allowing direction. |
| M1-34/3 | 2026-10-13 | `listUserSessions` authorizes `session.list` on the resource `user` (the catalogue's only resource kind of the action) and answers the decision `forbidden` with reason `not_self` for another person with `404 not_found`; an unknown person is `404` after `authorize` as well. | 05 rule 2: the sessions of another person are not readable for the caller, so the answer is `404`, as the brief asks. With the kind `user` authz sees a visible person (the directory) and refuses at the requirement; the catalogue lists no owned kind for `session.list` (unlike `device.list`, M1-33/2), and changing 05 is a core contract change outside this ticket. |
| M1-34/4 | 2026-10-13 | A refresh of a person in status `pending` succeeds like one from a pending device; `disabled` and `deleted` are `401 session_revoked`. | The brief says "principal not active", but 08 R-ID-14 gives provisioned people tokens with the rights of a pending device, and 05 section 2.5 lets them call `auth.token.refresh`; refusing would log them out before the owner approves them. |
| M1-34/5 | 2026-10-13 | A missing required `DPoP` header is answered by the request validator of `platform/httpserver` with `401 invalid_dpop_proof` and the challenge `DPoP error="invalid_dpop_proof", algs="ES256 EdDSA"`, without `DPoP-Nonce`. | The validator stage runs before the handler, so the generated decoder never sees the request; the chain has no nonce source, and the next proof failure answers with the nonce. The rule holds for every operation with the parameter (refresh and the login operations). |
