## M1-39 Local accounts with password and TOTP

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-39/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e5`, branch `feat/M1-39-identity-local-accounts` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. If M1-40 enters first, both entries of `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` and both `PrincipalSource` methods stay. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-37/1). |
| M1-39/2 | 2026-10-14 | TOTP is enrolled in two steps as the spec states (08 section 2.5, `LocalCredentialSet` with `password`, `totp_enroll`, `totp_code`): `totp_enroll` seals a new 20-byte secret, sets `totp_enabled_at` and `totp_last_step` to NULL and returns the otpauth URI once; a valid `totp_code` sets `totp_enabled_at` (if NULL) and the step. The members of one request apply in the order `password`, `totp_enroll`, `totp_code`, and any rejected member rolls the whole request back; a re-enrollment of an established path makes it pending again until confirmed. The URI is marked with `idempotency.MarkOneTimeSecret`. | Brief "SetLocalCredential"; the spec counts the path as established only with a confirmed secret, so a new, unconfirmed secret cannot keep it established. |
| M1-39/3 | 2026-10-14 | `authz` learns "credentials exist" from `PrincipalSource.HasEstablishedLocalCredential(ctx, userID)` (a row of an active identity of the person with `totp_enabled_at IS NOT NULL`). The map `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` lives in the new file `authz/app/stepup.go`: step 8 itself is in the pure `authz/domain/decide.go`, which may not call a port, so `gather` asks the source for the person of the resource (its id for a `user` resource, else its owner) and sets `Input.NoCredentialYet`; `decidePrincipalRules` then skips only the freshness check of a `conditional` action, never the check "human with a session". `passkey.register` stays "yes" until M1-40 adds its entry. | 07 section 4.2 step 8 and B7 (the rule stays in `authz`); the brief fixes the map name and signature for a clean merge with M1-40. |
| M1-39/4 | 2026-10-14 | The otpauth issuer is the host of `server.public_url`; label `issuer:login name`, every part URL-escaped. | Brief "TOTP": the display name lives in the instance settings (M1-46), which this ticket must not depend on. |
| M1-39/5 | 2026-10-14 | AEAD scheme of the TOTP secret: AES-256-GCM, key = the 32 bytes behind `auth.totp_key_ref` as hex (the form `bothy secrets init` writes for `totp_key`, generator `key`), 12-byte random nonce, AAD `"totp/v1" \|\| 0x00 \|\| identity_id`, stored `0x01 \|\| nonce \|\| ciphertext and tag`, the sealing ref in `totp_key_ref`. The start resolves the current key once in the identity step (`wire.TOTPBox`); a missing key or another length stops it. `Open` of a row with another ref resolves that ref through the secret provider (bounded by 5 seconds, its port has no context). | Brief "Sealing", 19 secrets table: a new key applies to new rows while old rows stay readable; the identity in the AAD keeps a copied ciphertext from opening for another person. |
| M1-39/6 | 2026-10-14 | Common passwords: SecLists `10k-most-common.txt` at `3a5f62f` (MIT), stored only as the first 16 hex characters of `SHA-256(lowercase(NFC(password)))`, sorted and unique (adapter README). The NFC normalization lives in `adapters/passwordpolicy` (vendor group `xtext` now also allows `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm`); `domain.CheckPassword` gets the NFC form, and app gets the extra outbound port `PasswordNormalizer` beside the three ports the brief fixes. | The domain and app layers may not import vendors (02 "Layers within a module"); no word list in the repository (white-label scan, size). |
| M1-39/7 | 2026-10-14 | Failure window: the column `local_credentials.fail_window_started_at` (03) starts with the first failure; a failure at or after its end starts a new window; reaching `auth.login_fail_limit` sets `locked_until`, clears count and window and writes a deny entry on the person with `{lock: true, identity_id}` in the same unit of work as the counter. A wrong TOTP code with the right password, a person that is not active and a path without confirmed TOTP count as failures of an existing row; a locked row counts nothing. Every rejection is one aggregated denial `{cause, identity_id}` (no identity for an unknown name), never the login name, the password or the code. | 08 section 13 (failures per account: password and TOTP), brief step 6; the login name could be a mistyped password. |
| M1-39/8 | 2026-10-14 | Exactly one Argon2id computation per rejected request: `Verify` for an existing row, also while locked, else `VerifyDummy` against a hash computed at start with the current parameters. Only a successful login with changed parameters computes a second hash (the rehash) after the verification. `TestIDT21_UnknownNameTimingEqualsWrongPassword` takes the samples in pairs back to back and compares the median of the pair differences, which cancels the drift of a loaded machine; without the race detector it measures the real Argon2id, under it (as in `make check`) Argon2id is instrumented to hundreds of milliseconds with a spread far above `authz.not_found_timing_tolerance`, so the test swaps in a hasher of constant cost and measures the database work, where an unknown name could still differ. `TestDummyUsesConfiguredParameters`, `TestStaleParametersCostLikeUnknownNames` and the case `TestLockedAccountStillHashes` hold the one computation of equal cost. The integration cases run as subtests of `TestLocalAccounts` on one world, as M1-44/13, and the timing test runs in parallel with the package. | 08 section 13, ID-T21; a rehash only happens after the password was proven, so it reveals nothing about unknown names. |
| M1-39/9 | 2026-10-14 | The Argon2id floors are the minimums of the configuration keys (`auth.argon2id_memory_kib` 19456, `auth.argon2id_iterations` 2, `auth.argon2id_parallelism` 1), so the start and `bothy config check` stop with a problem that names the key (ID-T24); the hasher refuses them too. Upper bounds keep a configuration from exhausting the server: memory 4194304 KiB, 1000 passes, parallelism 255 (the limit of the library), `auth.password_min_length` 1024. | Brief "Argon2id"; the configuration loader already reports every key problem before the start. |
| M1-39/10 | 2026-10-14 | `wire.(*Identity).BindLocal(sealer, hasher)` builds the local accounts after `BindSessions` in the start step `sessions`; `nil` builds the Argon2id hasher of the configuration, tests pass a counting one. `bothy setup` does not build them. The allow entry of `local_credential.set` carries `{identity_id, password_changed, totp_enroll_started, totp_confirmed}`. | The login completion of `BindSessions` is a collaborator; the host commands never log in with a password. |
| M1-39/11 | 2026-10-14 | The login verifies the password on a read of identity, person and row without locks and only then opens the unit of work, which reads them again with the row `FOR UPDATE` and the provider and identity share-locked, and decides there (lock, counter, TOTP step, login). A row whose hash changed in between is rejected without counting (cause `credential_changed`); an unknown name runs the same lookups in its unit of work. This deviates from step 3 of the brief, which locks the row before the hash. | With the lock held during Argon2id, parallel attempts on one known name queued behind each other while those on an unknown name did not, so the time revealed accounts and an attacker could hold the row of a person; the counter stays exact because it is decided under the row lock (code review M1-39). |
| M1-39/12 | 2026-10-14 | Every verification of `passwordhash` lasts at least the running average cost (weight 1/4) of the most expensive parameter set it has seen; the average of the configured parameters is learnt at construction. | Code review M1-39 (Major): after a change of `auth.argon2id_*` the rows keep their old parameters until their next login rehashes them (08 section 2.5), so without padding a wrong password on such a row took another time than an unknown name, which verifies with the configured parameters. |
| M1-39/13 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken byte for byte from the integration squash with the integration fixes INT-W18/1 to INT-W18/7, apart from the points named here. The integration base also holds M2-39 and M2-40 (terminal), which are not on main, so nothing of them is ported: `setHostState` stays out of the enabled list of `api/operations.yaml`, the host route rows and the `Hosts` handler stay out of `api/routes/real_test.go` and `wire/routes.go`, and the host and terminal tables stay out of `migrations/privileges_test.go`. The migration is renumbered to follow `00027_chat_thread_tags.sql` without a gap: `00029_identity_local_credentials.sql` (INT-W18/3) becomes `00028_identity_local_credentials.sql`, with the number in the changelog and the module README, and its checksum is recorded. The comments of the migration, its test, `queries/local_credentials.sql`, `authz/sources.go`, `authz/app/rules_test.go` and the new identity packages use the English chapter files and terms (`03-data-model.md`, `08-identity.md`, 03 principle 9, the condition `has_credentials` of 07 section 4.2, the step-up value `conditional`), so the checksum of the migration differs from the integration file. The multi-byte cases of `TestPasswordRules` use Greek letters instead of German umlauts, because the language gate (D-W-94) rejects umlauts in tracked files; they still count twelve and eleven code points of more than one byte. the new keys of `wire/config_test.go` name the chapter `08-identity.md`. The changelog entry, the module README (status line, port list and the new section on local accounts), the secrets stack README, the key rows and the generator paragraph of `docs/operations/configuration.md`, the new section of `docs/REUSE.md` and these rulings are written into the English text at the same places. Generated files are regenerated with the generators of the ported tree, not merged. | `main` is English, holds M1-38 to M1-55 but not M2-39 or M2-40; the migration numbers of main must follow without a gap. |
| M1-39/14 | 2026-10-19 | The field error code constants `PasswordTooShort` and `PasswordCommon` in `identity/domain/password_rules.go` carry `NOSONAR`. | SonarQube Cloud reports the name `PasswordTooShort` as a possible hard-coded credential (Go rule S2068) and rates new code C for security; the values are the public error codes `too_short` and `common_password` of the API, not secrets. |
| M1-39/15 | 2026-10-19 | Port onto main after M2-39: the migrations of this ticket and of the M1 tickets ported after it follow 00028_terminal_hosts.sql, each one number higher than on the integration line. | M2-39 landed first with migration 00028. |

## M2-39 Host records and the target host

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-39-1 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket is built on `feat/M2-39-terminal-hosts` from `int/m1-wave14` (`fd504d9`) in its own worktree and is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it (task of the workflow over the brief's local merge). The base copy of chapter 24, the terminal part of chapter 03 (`hosts.reported_session_users`, `host_session_users`, the composite key of `terminal_sessions`, R-DM-39 to R-DM-41, `host.os_user_max_chars`) and the host schemas of `openapi.yaml` lag `plan/spezifikation`; this branch carries the plan text of these parts, merged three-way so the base edits of M1-03, M1-15, M1-50 and M2-38 stay, and regenerates the REST models and the SDK clients. | Ruling T12, the heart of the ticket, exists only in the plan text; carrying it verbatim is no deviation from the core contract, and the plan port then brings identical content. |
| D-M2-39-2 | 2026-10-14 | Every transition into a state other than `active` ends the open sessions of the host in the same transaction; where the table names no close reason (into `pairing` or `confirming`) the reason is `host_rekeyed`. A request for the state a host already has is no transition and answers without effect. `pairing` and `confirming` cannot be retired (24 3.1 draws no such edge; spec gap in the report). | When leaving `disabled` there are normally no open sessions; ending them anyway costs one indexed query and closes any row a crash left behind. |
| D-M2-39-3 | 2026-10-14 | `TerminalOn` is true for an effective owner on every host and for a grant `terminal` in effect; the exception of I6 for owners on a `personal` host (07 2.12 point 3) is decided by the guard SR11 of M2-40 with `i6_holder_only`, after the requirement. | Before M2-40 no host can be `personal` (only `confirmHost` and `setHostLlmCredentials` set it), so the atom does not repeat the guard. |
| D-M2-39-4 | 2026-10-14 | `host.read` is `All(HumanSession, Any(Owner, Assignee, Terminal))`, `host.manage` `All(HumanSession, Owner)`. A requirement whose first part is `HumanSession` is a gate: `authorize` and `Scope` evaluate it right after the liveness and pending checks, before snapshot, resolution and visibility, and answer `forbidden` with `subject_kind_not_allowed`; 07 4.2 says so in one paragraph. | 05 4.14 refuses agents, apps and personal tokens with `subject_kind_not_allowed` (TM-T17, AZ-M59), which visibility first would turn into `404`; the answer depends only on the principal, so it reveals nothing (AZ-P4). |
| D-M2-39-5 | 2026-10-14 | `access.changed` lists the hosts with `TerminalOn` per principal: over every host for an effective owner and over the host grants otherwise; a host is `added` with `capabilities: ["terminal"]` and no level, `removed` with kind and id only, never `changed`. State and holder do not enter the set, so `setHostState` and a holder change write no `access.changed`; `owner.add`, `owner.remove` and `createHost` report hosts like every other target. A rights change on a host target affects every active owner, the current holder and the subjects of the grants on the host (groups expanded). | M2-46 can end sessions from `removed` entries alone; the set follows 07 2.12 point 3, which names neither state nor holder. |
| D-M2-39-6 | 2026-10-14 | A grant on a host needs an existing host (`404` otherwise) and refuses a retired one with `409 conflict` without a reason; `grant.changed` of a host grant has an empty scope. `getGrant` and `listGrants` with `target_kind=host` read host grants like instance grants (owner only); a non-owner gets `404` for a host grant and for the host filter, the answer of an unknown id. | A retired host is final (24 3.1), a grant on it could never work; the holder sees the host, not the grants on it. |
| D-M2-39-7 | 2026-10-14 | `listHosts` asks `Scope(host.read)` (new members `HostIDs`, ascending, and `AllHosts`, memoised per request only) instead of authorizing `host.read` on the instance, then filters each item with `authorize` in filter mode; it lists hosts in every state, retired ones included, ordered by `lower(name)` then `id`, with a keyset cursor bound to the caller. | The catalogue gives `host.read` only the resource `host`, so the brief's `ByKind{instance}` cannot be reached; `Scope` runs the same liveness checks and the gate. Retired hosts stay readable records (retention `security` after `retired_at`), and the contract has no filter parameter. |
| D-M2-39-8 | 2026-10-14 | A name taken by a live host (without regard to case, also when the unique index fires in a race) is `409 conflict` without a reason; `updateHost` on a retired host is `409 conflict`; `disabled_at` describes the current disabled state and is cleared on enable and retire; every `host.manage` write with an effect is a rights change, also a pure rename; a write without effect raises nothing and audits `no_change`. | Like group names in M1-29; the audit keeps the history; 05 rule 1.7 (Rechte ja) and 06 section 2 "Ohne Wirkung". |
| D-M2-39-9 | 2026-10-14 | A session user name must equal a reported name exactly, including case (else `400 validation_failed` with `errors[].code` `session_user_not_on_host`); uniqueness per host stays without regard to case (03). Two new `errors[].code` values of `validation_failed`: `unknown_user` (a person id that is unknown, not a human or deleted) and `mutually_exclusive` (`assigned_user_id` with `clear_assigned_user = true`); 24 section 13 names them. | The host service compares `osu` exactly with `host_service.session_users` (24 5.3), so a case variant would fail there with `4104`. |
| D-M2-39-10 | 2026-10-14 | `terminal_session.changed` comes with M2-44; `EndSessions` is the one path of every server end and writes `terminal.terminate` as the system (no actor) with `session_id`, `close_reason`, `bytes_in`, `bytes_out` and `duration_ms`. | No session can be opened before M2-44, so no client can miss the event. |
| D-M2-39-11 | 2026-10-14 | `reported_session_users` is shown only once a host has been paired (`paired_at` set); `connected` comes from `Presence`, bound to `NoConnections` until M2-43 replaces it with the registry of the control connections. | The schema says "Absent before pairing"; without the route `/hosts/connect` no host is connected, so `false` is the truth. |
| D-M2-39-12 | 2026-10-14 | `host.updated` carries the host without `noise_public_key` (the fingerprint stays, it is a digest); `key_changed` stays absent until M2-41. `host.manage` audits one `update` entry when name or holder change and one `session_user` entry per assignment and per end with the name in the new reserved member `os_user` (`auditing.OSUserName`, pattern of `host_session_users.os_user`); never the host name. | "never keys" (05 6) means no key member; one entry per assignment matches 24 10.1 "per assignment or end" and keeps the details flat. |
| D-M2-39-13 | 2026-10-14 | The pairing code is the token kind `hpc` of ADR 0061, issued by `identity.Tokens.NewHostPairingCode` (redacting secret and HMAC with the token pepper, no database); the authenticator refuses it before any lookup. `createHost` keeps the `Idempotency-Key` of the base contract (M1-03/6) instead of `auth_flow` of the brief: the handler marks the one-time secret, a replay answers `409 conflict` with `reason: one_time_secret`. | `createHost` must return a code (openapi `HostPairingCode`, the check `(state = 'pairing') = (pairing_code_hash IS NOT NULL)`), and identity owns the token format and the pepper; the merged contract wins over the brief. M2-41 adds the redemption and reuses `PairingCodes`. |
| D-M2-39-14 | 2026-10-14 | The signing-key purpose `terminal` gets an active key at every start; like `webhook_out` it rotates only on owner request, its retiring window is `terminal.session_token_ttl` plus `auth.clock_leeway`, and `getJwks` does not publish it. `rotateSigningKey` accepts `terminal` now; the clause of M1-32/2 that refused it until M2-39 is superseded. | 19 10 names no interval for it, and the host service fetches the keys over its control connection (M2-43). |
| D-M2-39-15 | 2026-10-14 | AZ-M60 of 07 11.1 names a host R3 without holder on which Alex holds no grant, instead of R1. | R1's holder is Alex (row AZ-M54), and a holder reads the host (07 2.12 point 5), so the old row contradicted the visibility rule. |
| D-M2-39-16 | 2026-10-14 | Names that differ from the brief: the fixtures live in `services/server/test/terminaltest` and the HTTP world in `services/server/test/terminalworld` (test code, outside the coverage measurement), the tests are split over `terminal/app`, `adapters/postgres` and `adapters/httpapi`; `Precondition` replaces `IfMatch int64` in the commands; `People.User` returns a found flag; the checksum vectors of `apitoken` carry no kind, so `hpc` adds no vector (the format test covers it); the test world authenticates with the test authenticator of `httpservertest` (sessions, step-up times and tokens through the fake credential source), like the worlds of M1-48 and M1-51. `audit.reason_max_chars` enters the schema here (M2-24 adds the same key). | `make coverage` measures each package by its own tests (D-M2-24-23); `go-arch-lint` admits test support only under `test/`. |
| D-M2-39-17 | 2026-10-14 | `httpserver.DecodeCursor` refuses a cursor key with a NUL character in any string with `400 cursor_invalid`, for every list. | The cursor has no signature (M1-18), so a client can forge a key whose string PostgreSQL text cannot hold (SQLSTATE 22021, a `500`); the red team of M2-39 found it in `listHosts` after M1-31/16 had the same class, so the codec fixes it once for all lists. |
| D-M2-39-18 | 2026-10-18 | Port onto main from the integration squash of `int/m1-wave15` with the integration fixes INT-W15/3 to /5: the migration is `00028_terminal_hosts.sql` (00023 to 00027 are M1-52, M1-53 and M1-55 on main; content and checksum unchanged, `terminalVersion` 28); `authz.AccessViewOf` takes spaces as paths with the archived flag and the space levels of M1-52 and the hosts of M2-39 (`spaces []Path, hosts []ids.ID`, all four maps); the start steps register chat and then terminal, and authorizer, rights hook and admin get both the structure and the host source. The contract of the session users (03 4.14, 24, `openapi.yaml`) is already on main, so its port commit is not taken; the spec additions of this ticket (07 3 and 4.2, 08 12, 19 secrets and SEC-AUD-5, 24 2.2, 10.1 and 11) are written into the English chapters, the module README is English, and generated files are regenerated, not merged. | Tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each as one pull request with the integration fixes it needs, and `main` is English. |

## M2-40 Terminal rights, fresh step-up and the guards SR10 to SR12

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-40-1 | 2026-10-14 | `terminal.read_own` is `All(HumanSession, ByKind{terminal_session: Any(Self, Owner), host: Auth})`: on a host, "self or owner" means "own sessions on a host you can see, owners all"; the list query of M2-44 restricts the rows to the caller's `user_id` for people who are not owners, so on the host only visibility counts. A `terminal_session` is visible to its person and to effective owners whatever the requirement (else `404` with `not_self`), so the Owner gets `forbidden` `not_self` on `terminal.attach` of a foreign session. | A host has no owner of its own sessions; the requirement can only gate the list, the rows are the use case's filter. |
| D-M2-40-2 | 2026-10-14 | Fresh step-up evaluates only its own branch in step 8: the quick reattach first (only `terminal.attach` of a `detached` session from the same device and login session within `terminal.quick_reattach_window`, equal allowed), else a fresh step-up (`terminal.step_up_max_age`, equal allowed, `passkey` or `device_key`, not consumed). A console session fails closed until M6-18 adds the passkey rule with `userVerification = required`. `identity.StepUp.ConsumeFresh` checks the freshness again on the locked session row (the first domain row lock of the action's transaction) with the rule of `authorize` (`authz.FreshStepUpCheck`), never on the actor of the request. | Two requests with one confirmation both pass `authorize`; only the lock orders them, so the second opening sees the consumption (08 6.3 point 4, 03 6.3). Console sessions do not exist before M6-01. |
| D-M2-40-3 | 2026-10-14 | The guards of `terminal.open` and `terminal.attach` run after step 8 in the order SR10 server host, SR10 assignee, SR11, SR12; the first failure wins. | The order of 07 section 5 and 24 6.1 step 2; a server host is closed to every person who is not an owner, assigned or not, so its reason tells the person what would help; a person without a fresh step-up learns `step_up_required` first. |
| D-M2-40-4 | 2026-10-14 | The difference of `access.changed` and the preview effects use `TerminalEffective` (07 2.12 point 3 with the I6 clause): a switch to `personal` gives `removed: [{kind: host, id}]` for every other owner, a switch back `added` with `capabilities: [terminal]`. SR10 and SR12 are guards, not part of effective `terminal`, and never appear in the difference; a state or assignee change still writes no `access.changed` (D-M2-39-5). | The declaration raises the rights version (03), so the I6 clause belongs to the view; 04 8.2 reports the additional right itself, and SR10 depends on `reported_is_server_host`, which raises no rights version. |
| D-M2-40-5 | 2026-10-14 | I6 is verified in phase 3.1 of every rights change, like I1 (M1-29): for every host with `llm_credentials = personal` the grants valid at `t` (not revoked, `expires_at` after `t`, active subject, live group); a group grant there is always a violation, a grant to a user other than the holder too; owners do not count. | `user.enable` of a person whose grant became dormant before the switch to `personal` would make a grant to another person valid again; 03 6.8 names only the actions where a violation starts from the request. |
| D-M2-40-6 | 2026-10-14 | With several violations in one change the problem code is the first of `last_owner`, `i1_violation`, `i6_violation`, and `conflicts` lists the entries of that code; the deny entry carries the violations of that code (`details.violations` with `host_id` for I6). The brief's order (`i1`, `i6`, `last_owner`) is not taken. | M1-29 already ruled `last_owner` before `i1_violation` (without an owner no personal agent can be in order either); keeping that and putting I6 last changes no existing answer. |
| D-M2-40-7 | 2026-10-14 | `setHostLlmCredentials` refuses `credential_holder_id` with `none` or `organization` with the new `errors[].code` `not_allowed` (documented in `HostLlmCredentialsSet` and 24 13); a `retired` host answers `409 conflict`; every other state accepts a declaration, and `confirmHost` (M2-41) sets it again at the confirmation. `ApplyLlmDeclaration` writes the declaration on a locked host and ends the foreign sessions; the caller audits and appends `host.updated`, so `confirmHost` can reuse it with its own audit. | `retired` is final (24 3.1); a silently ignored holder would hide a client error. |
| D-M2-40-8 | 2026-10-14 | `RecordSessionUserReport` compares a report with the stored one by the set of names and each name's ordered `workdirs`; an equal report writes nothing and raises no rights version. A different report locks the host, compares again, writes the report with the next host version, ends with `access_revoked` the sessions of every person whose assigned name vanished and appends `host.updated`; it writes no `host.manage` audit entry (`host.pair` and `host.connect` audit their own calls). | A host repeats its report after every reconnect, and a version bump per reconnect would empty every rights cache. |
| D-M2-40-9 | 2026-10-14 | The rights hook accepts `host.pair` and `host.connect` as the action of a declared rights change, although 05 marks their column "Permissions" `no`. | 03 (`grants`, paragraph on `permission_version`) counts `reported_session_users` as rights state, which only these actions write; 05 rule 1.7 says that actions with "Permissions" `yes` raise the version, not that no other may. |
| D-M2-40-10 | 2026-10-14 | `updateHost` that changes or clears the assignee ends, in its unit of work, the sessions of the old assignee on the host with `access_revoked` unless the old assignee is an active owner (`People.IsActiveOwner`, bound to `identity.Directory.User` without `authorize`); sessions of owners and of the new assignee stay. A change of the owner status or of `reported_is_server_host` is M2-46's. | Only the old assignee can lose SR10 by this change; every other person who is not an owner was already refused by SR10. |
| D-M2-40-11 | 2026-10-14 | The preview of a grant on a host renders `summary.host`, then denial, I6 violation, `terminal_not_effective`, group reach, missing expiry; no abilities and no capabilities line. The cause of `terminal_not_effective` for a person is the first failing guard (server host, assignee, session user); a group gets one hint with the number of its active members in effect whom SR10 or SR12 would deny, none for 0; no such hint when an I6 violation names the host. | The abilities and capabilities lines describe levels in the tree, which a host has not; the I6 refusal makes the hint moot. |
| D-M2-40-12 | 2026-10-14 | 07 4.1 gains the reason `terminal_no_session_user` (SR12), section 5 the rule SR12; 19 SEC-AUD-5 the I6 form `{code, host_id, credential_holder_id, principal_ids}` of `violations`. | The brief names both as present, but the merged chapter lacked them (spec gaps). |
| D-M2-40-13 | 2026-10-14 | Names that differ from the brief: `kernel/authorize.Principal` gains `StepUpMethodName`, `StepUpConsumedTime` and `ClientComponentName` (only `actor.Actor` implements it); the domain mirrors `authorize.TerminalFacts` as `TerminalSessionFacts` and takes the action as a string, because `authz/domain` imports no kernel port; `domain.HostFacts` gains the stored host facts and stands for the brief's host facts of the guard; `ValidateDeclaration` returns one `kernel/errs` field error like M2-39's checks; `terminal.SetLlmCredentials` carries a `Precondition` instead of `IfMatch int64`; `actor.Actor` stands for `actor.Principal`; `wire.IdentityWrites.Fresh` passes the `FreshStepUpCheck`; `ActiveGrantsOnHosts` of M2-39 serves for the brief's `ActiveGrantsOnHost`; the work is built on `feat/M2-40-terminal-authz` from `int/m1-wave15` and not merged locally (task of the workflow over the brief's local merge). | The merged names and types of M1 and M2-39 win over the brief (Boundaries). |
| D-M2-40-14 | 2026-10-18 | Port onto main from the integration squash of M2-40 on top of M2-39: German chapter names and labels in code, comments and goldens (`requirements_table.go`, `rightschange.go`, `sources.go`) follow the English chapters; the spec additions of M2-40 to 07, 08, 19 and 24 and the terminal README are carried over in English; the ruling table of M2-40 is its own section; the generated files (REST, SDKs, configuration reference, config schema) are regenerated. Tests: the placeholder stubs of `stepup_fresh_test.go` come from `stepup_new_test.go` of main (`stubAudit` for `stubRecorder`), `decide_rules_test.go` no longer expects `terminal_session` without a visibility rule (M2-40 adds it), and German request reasons in tests are English. | The English documents on main win (PR lane task); the integration branch still carries the German chapters. |

## M2-41 Pairing, confirmation and re-pairing of hosts

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-41-1 | 2026-10-15 | A pairing code is stored as HMAC-SHA-256 with the pepper of `auth.token_pepper_ref` over the full code string, the same derivation as `api_tokens.token_hash`; `identity.Tokens.HashHostPairingCode` parses form, checksum and kind `hpc` first and returns `identity.ErrTokenMalformed` before any HMAC. | One derivation for every token of ADR 0061; the prefix `bothy_hpc_` already separates the kinds, so no extra label is hashed. |
| D-M2-41-2 | 2026-10-15 | One issuer for `createHost`, `issueHostPairingCode` and the fallback of `confirmHost`: `app.Service.issueCode` over `PairingCodes.Issue` (bound to `identity.Tokens.NewHostPairingCode`). Case a of the brief: M2-39 already minted `hpc` codes through `domain/apitoken` and the identity port, so its names stay. | The format and the hash derivation exist once. |
| D-M2-41-3 | 2026-10-15 | `pairHost` runs its write as the rights change `host.pair` (with the people of the valid assignments), although 05 gives `host.pair` Permissions `no`. | 03 counts `reported_session_users` as rights state (D-M2-40-9), so the rights version rises first. |
| D-M2-41-4 | 2026-10-15 | A redeemed code leaves `pairing_code_hash` and `pairing_expires_at` NULL. | The index on `pairing_expires_at` serves only `pairing`, and a used code must never match again. |
| D-M2-41-5 | 2026-10-15 | The key and report rules of `pairHost` with their `errors[].code` values: `invalid_key` (Noise key not strict base64url of 32 bytes or a low-order point; JWK `kty`, `crv` or `x` wrong), `unknown_member` (any JWK member besides `kty`, `crv`, `x`), `empty`, `duplicate`, `too_long`, `pattern_mismatch`, `limit_reached`, `invalid_format`, the textcheck rules for labels; every finding is returned, in body order. | The host service is untrusted input, and a host without session users can never give a terminal (HD1). |
| D-M2-41-6 | 2026-10-15 | Only a well-formed prefix (upper-cased, exactly `terminal.fingerprint_confirm_chars` characters of the Crockford alphabet) counts as an attempt; `I`, `L`, `O` and `U` are not mapped. | A typo of the form must not burn an attempt; the client may normalise before sending. |
| D-M2-41-7 | 2026-10-15 | `409 fingerprint_mismatch` of `confirmHost` keeps its effect under an `Idempotency-Key`: the handler calls `idempotency.KeepEffectOnFinal`, and the stage stores the answer with `Store.Complete` in the same unit and commits; only this answer is ever marked, a marked `401` or `5xx` still rolls back. The new problem member `attempts_remaining` is carried by `errs.FingerprintMismatch` through the existing `errs.ProblemExtender`. | Every failed attempt must count also under a key (TM-T19); screen A8 (M4-37) shows the remaining attempts and no other field carries them. |
| D-M2-41-8 | 2026-10-15 | `confirmHost` compares the prefix before anything of the body that needs the database (the people of `session_users`, the credential holder, the assignment rows); it writes one `confirm` audit entry that also carries the declaration, plus one `session_user` entry per assignment, all with the same correlation. A replay with the same key gets the stored answer and never burns a second attempt. | A wrong host must never reach an assignment or a declaration. |
| D-M2-41-9 | 2026-10-15 | The fallback after `terminal.fingerprint_max_attempts` failures: state `pairing`, the three key columns NULL, `key_fingerprint` kept, `pairing_code_hash` the HMAC of a fresh code that is never shown with `pairing_expires_at` now, `fingerprint_failures` 0, the next version, `host.updated` with `key_changed`. | 03 demands a hash exactly in `pairing`, and no code may work until the owner issues one. |
| D-M2-41-10 | 2026-10-15 | Dropping keys keeps `key_fingerprint`; `Host` and `host.updated` omit `key_fingerprint` and `noise_public_key` while the host has no keys; `pairHost` with the retained fingerprint is `409 conflict` `host_key_reused`, with the login key of another host `host_key_in_use`. | Refuses a re-sent key pair (T-TM-05). Honest limit: the server keeps no dropped key, so a host service that keeps one of its two keys is not detected; M2-42 generates both anew. |
| D-M2-41-11 | 2026-10-15 | The visibility of `host` adds: any principal when the requirement of the action is exactly `Public` (only `host.pair`). | The pairing code is the proof, checked by the use case before `authorize`, as for the refresh token. |
| D-M2-41-12 | 2026-10-15 | `key_changed` is set exactly when stored keys were dropped (re-pairing from `confirming`, `active` or `disabled`, and the fallback); `key_generation` rises only at `pairHost`. Audit details: `pairing_code` with `from_state`, `to_state`, `old_fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `sessions_ended`, `reason`; `confirm` with `result`, `fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `attempts_remaining`, `llm_credentials`, `credential_holder_id`, `declared_server_host`, `reason`; `host.pair` with `fingerprint`, `old_fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `platform`. Fingerprints as compact lower-case codes; the host is the resource, not a detail; `from_state` and `to_state` follow M2-39 instead of the brief's `state_before` and `state_after`. | Devices pin keys by generation (24 5.5); one vocabulary for state changes in the audit. |
| D-M2-41-13 | 2026-10-15 | New keys `terminal.report_label_max_chars` (default `100`) and `terminal.report_max_entries` (default `64`) of chapter 24; the start check `fingerprint_keys` requires `terminal.fingerprint_confirm_chars` between 1 and the length of the compact fingerprint and `terminal.fingerprint_max_attempts` at least 1. | Limits only through named keys; M2-43 reuses the report keys for `report`. |
| D-M2-41-14 | 2026-10-15 | The job `terminal.sweep` (and its clause on expired codes) is not built here; an expired code is rejected at redemption, and its hash stays while the host is `pairing`. | No ticket row names the job; follow-up. |
| D-M2-41-15 | 2026-10-15 | Names that differ from the brief: `PairingCodes.Issue` instead of `New`; the port methods take `actor.Actor` and commands carry `terminal.Precondition` (D-M2-39-16); `RepairEffects(state, disabledReason)`; field errors are `errs.FieldError`; `Cache-Control: no-store` comes from the chain for every API answer, not from the handler; `issueHostPairingCode` declares `Idempotency-Key` in the contract and marks its answer as a one-time secret like `createHost` instead of `x-idempotency-exempt`; `TestPairingRoutes_AZP1` is part of `TestAZP1_HostRoutes`; `StorePairing` writes `reported_session_users` with the encoder of the report of M2-40 in the pairing statement instead of calling `ApplySessionUserReport`, which would write a second version and event; the instance id comes from `instance.State.Load`; the test world makes Alex an owner by a fixture row; 08 section 12 already named the kind `hpc`. | The merged code of M1, M2-39 and M2-40 wins over the brief (Boundaries). |
| D-M2-41-16 | 2026-10-15 | The spec chapters on this base are German, so the changes to 02, 06 and 24 are written in German like those of M2-40; the planning rulings D-W-65, D-W-101 and D-W-102 of main change no contract and are not ported. Working directory labels are part of `Host.reported_session_users`, so they appear in the `hosts` row and in `host.updated`; `TestPairingNeverLeaksSecrets` checks the audit, problem bodies and logs for them. | Follow the language of the chapter; the brief's leak list would contradict the Host schema of M2-39. |
| D-M2-41-17 | 2026-10-19 | Port onto main from the integration squash of M2-41 on top of M2-40: the spec additions to 02, 06 and 24, the threat model row and ADR 0061 are written into the English documents of main (this supersedes the language part of D-M2-41-16), the terminal README and the requirement label are English, and the generated files (REST, routes, SDKs, configuration reference, config schema) are regenerated. | `main` is English; the integration branch still carries the German chapters. |

## Integration int/m1-wave18

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W18/1 | 2026-10-14 | M1-39 enters as one squash of `82693e5..160c3ce` onto `int/m1-wave17` (`e2f9eb4`). ## M2-01 Worker consumers of the event log

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-01-1 | 2026-10-12 | No migration seeds `event_consumer_cursors`. In the roles `worker` and `all`, `ConsumerService.Ensure` inserts the row of every registered consumer in one unit of work before the runner starts, with `INSERT ... SELECT $1, COALESCE(max(seq), 0) FROM event_log ON CONFLICT (name) DO NOTHING`; unregistered names have no row and no lag series. The column default `0` of 03 stays. Place: `00007_event_consumer_cursors.sql`, `queries/consumers.sql`, 02 11.5 item 5. | A seeded row for a consumer that arrives in a later release (`push`, M3-19) would make it replay every event retained since the migration; a new consumer starts at the head like the first connection of an agent consumer (04 15.1 item 2). Reading `max(seq)` in one statement is safe because a reader that sees `seq = k` sees every smaller number (04 3.2 item 3); two workers starting together run the same statement and one row wins. |
| D-M2-01-2 | 2026-10-12 | `domain.PlanRound`: numbers contiguous from `last_seq + 1` are processed; when `last_seq + 1` lies below `min(seq)` and the first number read equals `min(seq)`, the events in between were removed by retention, the round processes from `min(seq)`, logs `event consumer lost events to retention` once at error level with `bothy.consumer.name` and `bothy.event.seq` and continues; every other jump, hole or repeat is `ErrConsumerGap`, the round rolls back and the consumer stalls. `min(seq)` is read in the round's transaction only when the first number does not follow the cursor. Place: `domain/consumer.go`, `app/consumers.go`, 02 11.5 item 6. | The removed events cannot come back and stalling would not help; a webhook subscription detects its own gap through `last_delivered_seq` (11 6), and the alarm fires long before. 04 3.2 items 3 and 4 rule out other holes, so they are bugs; unlike the tail's `ErrGap` the worker does not exit, the cursor just never passes the hole. The round holds `ACCESS SHARE` on `event_log`, so no partition is detached between the reads. |
| D-M2-01-3 | 2026-10-12 | A handler error, a handler panic (recovered into kind internal, the panic value is not kept), a database error and `ErrConsumerGap` roll back the whole round: no job, no cursor move, the next attempt at the next wake or poll; no event is ever skipped. The runner logs the first failure after a success at error level (`event consumer round failed`, `bothy.consumer.name`, `error.type` as SQLSTATE, `consumer_gap`, `timeout`, `canceled` or the `kernel/errs` kind) and the next success at info level (`event consumer recovered`). Each consumer has its own goroutine, so a stalled one never holds back another. Place: `app/consumer_runner.go`, 02 11.5 item 7. | One failing event must stall only its consumer, visibly through the gauge and the alarm, rather than lose an event; edge-triggered lines keep a database outage, during which the worker pauses (02 14.4), from flooding the log. |
| D-M2-01-4 | 2026-10-12 | Before the cursor moves, the round asks the appender of `sync/app` (its per-unit `txn.Key` buffer, `bufferedEvents`) whether the unit buffered durable events; if so it fails with kind internal and the unit rolls back. Place: `app/appender.go`, `app/consumers.go`, 02 11.5 item 8. | The consumer would read its own output, and the round would take the `event_seq` counter lock and lengthen the serialized commit phase (02 11.7); ADR 0051 lets a round insert jobs only. |
| D-M2-01-5 | 2026-10-12 | Two observable gauges, `bothy.event.consumer.lag` (`{event}`, `head_seq - last_seq`, floored at zero) and `bothy.event.consumer.pending_age` (`s`, now from `kernel/clock` minus `occurred_at` of the oldest event after the cursor, zero at the head), with the only attribute `bothy.consumer.name` from the closed set `sync.ConsumerNames()`; one callback calls `Consumers.States` (one read of all rows with the head, then one read per row, bounded by `database.statement_timeout`), and a failed read drops the points. Every role registers them in start step 6b. The registry `internal/platform/otel/metrics` of M1-68 is not merged, so they live in `internal/sync/adapters/otelmetrics` with the meter of the global provider; `sync` already had the `otel` vendor group. `bothy.consumer.name` is in `otel/attr`, the collector allowlist and signals.md 5.3. Place: `adapters/otelmetrics/consumers.go`, signals.md 4.2. | The plan row asks for `head_seq` minus `last_seq`, but `sync.event_retention` is a time window, so the alarm needs the age too. A stopped or missing worker must still show a growing lag, so the api role exports it as well; telemetry never blocks (17 rule 4). |
| D-M2-01-6 | 2026-10-12 | Alarm `BothyEventConsumerLag`, severity `page`, `max by (bothy_consumer_name) (bothy_event_consumer_pending_age_seconds{job="bothy/bothy-server"}) > 900` for `5m`, on the new key `consumer.pending_alert_after` (place Server, duration, default `15m`, at least `1s`). The start check `consumer_alert_below_retention` requires it strictly below `sync.event_retention` and names both keys and no value. The rule file holds the number; `alert-thresholds` keeps it equal to the default. Place: `bothy-alerts.yaml`, alerting.md 3.2 and 4, 02 13 item 4 and 22, `configkeys_consumer.go`, `configchecks.go`. | A normal worker restart takes at most `shutdown.total_timeout` (`60s`) plus a few `consumer.poll_interval` (`1s`), far below `15m`, and the `for` of the neighbouring lag alarm adds five minutes; `15m` is far below the `30d` retention, so the operator has weeks before any event is lost. `page`, because a standing consumer stops pushes and outgoing webhooks, which people notice at once (alerting.md 1). |
| D-M2-01-7 | 2026-10-12 | One `LISTEN event_log` connection per process for all worker consumers, opened with `pg.Pool.DialDedicated(ctx, "bothy-consumer-wake")`, apart from the tail's connection; the listening loop of the M1-16 tail moved into the shared type `listener` in `adapters/postgres`, which both use. A notification wakes every consumer through a channel of capacity one with a non-blocking send; the payload is not read. The connection is reconnected with exponential backoff from `consumer.poll_interval` up to `database.listen_reconnect_backoff_cap`, and every consumer also polls every `consumer.poll_interval`. Without a registered consumer the runner starts nothing and opens no connection. Place: `adapters/postgres/listen.go`, `waker.go`, 02 11.5 item 9. | In role `all` the ordered loop of the tail must never depend on the consumers; polling covers a lost connection and nothing is lost because the cursor lives in the database (02 14.4). The production list is empty in M2-01, and an idle connection per worker would serve nobody. |
| D-M2-01-8 | 2026-10-12 | After a full batch (`consumer.batch_size` events) the next round of that consumer starts at once (drain); after a short batch, an idle round, a locked row or a failed round the consumer waits for the next wake or poll. Place: `app/consumer_runner.go`. | A backlog drains without waiting a poll per batch, while `consumer.batch_size` keeps each round, and with it the `ACCESS SHARE` lock that a detach of the partition maintenance waits for, short. |
| D-M2-01-9 | 2026-10-12 | The stop step `event_consumers` lies in `lifecycle.PhaseConsumers` (before River's phase) with budget `0`, the rest of `shutdown.total_timeout`: the runner starts no new round and waits for the running ones; when the budget runs out the round's context is cancelled and the round rolls back. An unexpected end of the runner (the waker ends early, a panic outside a handler) goes through `Failed()` to `lifecycle.Runner.FailOn`, and the process exits `1` like after an unexpected stop of the River client. Place: `wire/sync.go`, `wire/serve.go`, 02 14.5, 15.2 and 15.3. | 02 15.3 gives this step no own budget; either way no job is lost or doubled, because cursor and jobs commit together. A process whose consumers ended would look healthy while pushes and webhooks stop. |
| D-M2-01-10 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M2-01-worker-event-consumers` in its own worktree from the head of `int/m1-wave6` (`785916c`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. New text in the German chapters (02, alerting.md, signals.md, ADR 0051, threat model) is written in English. The tests take two hooks of `wire.Deps`: `ConsumerHandlers` (replaces the empty production list in worker and all) and `ConsumerGauges` (replaces the registration on the global meter provider), because `otel.Setup` installs one global provider per process and parallel serve tests would overwrite it. | The launcher's task overrides the merge step of the brief ("Definition of done") and requires English output. The hooks follow the pattern of `Deps.Registrations` and let the serve tests prove that worker and all run a registered consumer and that role api exports the gauges without a runner. |
| D-M2-01-11 | 2026-10-13 | Port onto main: the migration is `00009_event_consumer_cursors.sql` (00007 and 00008 are M1-20 and M1-33), the start step `identity` of M1-33 stands in `components()` right after `authz` and before `routes`, held by `TestStartSequenceModuleSteps`, and `closeTimeout` lives only in `listen.go`, which the log tail and the waker share. | M2-01 rebuilt the start sequence as `components()`; a step left in the old list would start in no role, and the device handlers must exist when the routes are mounted. The same renumbering as the integration (ADR 0007 rule 4). |

## M4-03 Accent variants

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-03-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The tool's ruff configuration selects no `D` rules; the new code carries docstrings anyway, no lint rule is added. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request. |
| D-M4-03-1 | 2026-10-14 | The rules member `accent_variants` of `design/design-rules.json` names the default (`spruce`), the seven accents of 01 section 12 and the eight swapped roles. `tokenkit/accents.py` validates it (default in the accents, every accent a palette scale, every role an opaque colour in both themes) and raises a `RuleError` naming the member. A role is swapped in a theme only where it aliases `palette.spruce.<step>` directly; otherwise it is kept and shown as "not swapped". | One place for the list the server, the apps and the gate share; a role that does not follow the brand scale (dark `color.focus.ring` on `gorse`) must not change with the accent. |
| D-M4-03-2 | 2026-10-14 | Step search: a variant starts on the same step of the chosen scale (nearest step by number if missing, a tie goes darker in light and lighter in dark) and, while one of its pairings fails, moves one step towards more contrast (darker when the role is the darker colour of the failing pair, else lighter) until all pass; a scale end, a flat scale or a revisited step fails the build with `accent <a>, theme <t>: no step of <scale> meets <pairing>`. `moved_from` records the start step and `contrast.md` shows it. With today's palette no variant moves. | A fixed step on a lighter scale cannot always meet AA, while 01 section 12 promises that every listed accent works; the moved steps stay visible. |
| D-M4-03-3 | 2026-10-14 | `DsAccent` is a separate `ThemeExtension` with one constant per theme and accent (`lightSpruce` ... `darkMoss`) and `DsAccent.of(key, brightness)`, an unknown key giving the default; it is not a fourth resolver modifier. `DsColors` keeps its fields. In CSS the light/dark default theme block of an accent also covers an element without `data-theme`, and a `prefers-color-scheme` block covers the other theme, as for the base roles; `data-accent` sits on the same element as `data-theme`. | The tool allows a token to depend on one modifier only, and an accent role always combines with the theme; existing code keeps compiling. |
| D-M4-03-4 | 2026-10-14 | Guard scope: the palette guard "Brand and accent" keeps checking the base roles only; accent variants of an instance are not checked against it. The Discord guard covers every scale. Recorded below the ban list of the design README. | 01 section 12 allows `heather`, `rowan` and `amber` deliberately as the instance owner's choice from the checked palette, while the product brand stays spruce; contrast applies to the variants in full. |
| D-M4-03-5 | 2026-10-14 | An accent without a passing step is reported as a failed gate (exit 1, nothing written, the other gate messages still listed); an invalid `accent_variants` member stays an error of the rules file (exit 2), as for other rule errors. | The brief's test asks for exit 1 when a variant falls below AA; an invalid rules file is a different failure. |

## M7-19 API reference in the docs site

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M7-19/0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. Rulings use the row form of this file (`M7-19/<n>` under a ticket heading) instead of the brief's `D-M7-19-<n>` in section 10, as the other ported tickets do. The two `__init__` methods of the hook carry docstrings because main's ruff configuration requires them (rule `D107`). | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| M7-19/1 | 2026-10-14 | Generator (Design note 1): an in-repository MkDocs hook, `tools/docs/mkdocs_api_reference.py`, renders both documents to static Markdown with the pinned `pyyaml` and `mkdocs`; no new dependency. Compared with the pinned Redocly CLI 2.62.1 `build-docs` (plus the AsyncAPI HTML template for the gateway). Evidence: `redocly build-docs docs/api/openapi.yaml` rendered the OpenAPI 3.1 document without errors, but its default page loads `redoc.standalone.js` from `cdn.redocly.com` and a Google Fonts stylesheet, is one 6.9 MB HTML file outside the site's navigation and search, and renders no AsyncAPI; the gateway would need `@asyncapi/cli` and its template as a further npm tree (not in `tools/node`, not tried). The hook renders the 304 operations, 35 webhook events, 560 schemas and 93 gateway messages offline, with no script, in the site's theme, search and strict link and anchor validation; Apache-2.0 as the repository; maintained here. This replaces the suggestion `redocly build-docs` of ADR 0045. | Requirement 9 and 13 (no CDN, no external asset) and requirement 12 (both references in every build); the static hook meets them without bundling a JavaScript application. |
| M7-19/2 | 2026-10-14 | Generated pages live only in the build: `api/reference/rest.md`, `api/reference/schemas.md` and `api/reference/gateway.md` are added as generated files in `on_files` and listed in the nav under API; nothing is committed. A committed file at one of these paths, a missing document, an unresolved local `$ref` or two equal anchors fail the build. Anchors are `op-<operationid lower case>`, `schema-<Name>`, `response-<Name>`, `header-<Name>`, `security-<name>`, `channel-<key>`, `operation-<key>` and `message-<key>` (characters other than letters, digits, `_` and `-` become `-`). Text of the documents is used as Markdown with `<` escaped outside code spans; a code span wrapped over two lines is joined. `docs/api/README.md` names the site paths in code, not as links, because the pages do not exist for `make linkcheck`. | Requirement 12: a stale generated file cannot be committed; the strict build validates every generated link and anchor. |
| M7-19/3 | 2026-10-14 | External asset rule: `tools/docs/external_assets_check.py site` runs in `make docs` after the build and fails on a `<script src>`, a `<link href>` with rel `stylesheet`, `preload`, `modulepreload` or `prefetch`, or a CSS `url(...)` or `@import` (in `<style>`, `style` attributes and `.css` files) whose URL names a host. Anchors and links that load nothing (`rel="canonical"`) pass, `data:` URLs pass, an empty site fails. Requests a bundled script makes at run time are out of its reach; the known one is Material's Mermaid loader (CDN, accepted in ADR 0045 "Consequences"), which the reference pages do not use. | Requirement 13 in the form the brief gives (a check over the built HTML); the run-time loader is an existing, recorded decision and outside this ticket's scope. |
| M7-19/4 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held: `test_external_assets_check.py` and `test_mkdocs_api_reference.py` were written and run red (module not found) before the check and the hook existed. | Honest record of the order of work. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-24-0 | 2026-10-19 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken from the integration squash of `int/m1-wave19` with the integration fixes that belong to this ticket: INT-W19/4 (`CheckAppScope` takes the token scopes, `Scope` and `AdminScope` pass them, `TestScope_AppTokenScopes`), INT-W19/5 (`NewApps` uses the structure source of chat and the host source of terminal, both principal writers stay in `wire.Identity`), INT-W19/7 (`AppsDeps.Grants` is the Admin service of the authz step, the handlers of apps join through `(*Apps).fillHandlers`), the apps part of INT-W19/8 (`test/appsworld` with the identity keys, the instance settings, the full Admin service and the rows of its space and channels; `TestAuthenticateAPIToken_AppInstallation` sets `DevicePendingTTL`) and INT-W19/9 (`RevokeSubjectGrants` appends one `grant.changed` with `change: revoked` per revoked grant). The spec additions are written into the English chapters (02, 07, 08, 11, `configuration.md`, `threat-model.md`) at the same places; code comments, SQL comments and the module READMEs name the English chapter files and headings, so the checksums of both migrations are recorded anew. | D-M2-24-1 | 2026-10-13 | The module `apps` may use `authz` (new edge in 02 4.4, `architecture/modules.yaml` and the C4 component diagram): uninstall revokes the grants of the app through `authz.Admin.RevokeSubjectGrants`, and the subscription rule asks `authz.AppScopes`. `identity` declares `AppInstallations`, `apps` implements it in `adapters/identitysource`, `internal/wire` binds it. | 11 4.3 asks uninstall to revoke grants, and the scope table of 05 2.6 lives only in `authz` (05 rule 1.1); `authz` is a lower layer and `identity` never imports `apps`, so the graph stays acyclic. |
| D-M2-24-2 | 2026-10-13 | `AppManifest` in `openapi.yaml` follows 11 3.1 field for field (required `schema_version`, `slug`, `name`, `description`, `publisher`, `version`, `min_api_version`, `scopes.required` and `scopes.optional`; optional `homepage_url`, `events`, `delivery`, `commands`, `components`, `requested_grants`, `incoming_webhooks`, `data_egress`), with inline objects and `additionalProperties: false` everywhere; `requested_grants` uses the shared enums `TargetKind`, `Level` and `Capability` so `trigger`, `manage`, `export`, `approve_actions` and `host` reach the rule check. Every example that shows a manifest uses the new form. | The YAML lacked members that 11 3.1, 3.2 and 03 `apps.manifest` require; the change that finds the contradiction aligns the YAML with the chapter (SPEC 13 point 5), and the rules can only name a refused value they receive. |
| D-M2-24-3 | 2026-10-13 | Validation pipeline: the request validation checks the schema first (`unknown_member`, `invalid_enum`, ... as `400 validation_failed`, also for `validateManifest`); then the handler hands the manifest bytes as received to the pure `domain.ValidateManifest` with an `Env` of catalogue facts and configuration. `validateManifest` answers `200` with `valid` and every rule error relative to the manifest root, stores nothing and writes no audit entry; `installApp` and `upgradeAppManifest` answer `400 validation_failed` with the pointers under `/manifest`, or `400 webhook_host_not_allowed` when every error is a host error. Codes come from the M1-18 vocabulary (`required`, `invalid`, `too_large`, `too_long`, `too_many_items`), plus `webhook_host_not_allowed` and `command_name_reserved` where 06 and 09 name the reason; the detail names the rule, never the value. | SEC-IN-1 checks the shape before any rule; one rule function serves the dry run and the install, so both report the same errors. |
| D-M2-24-4 | 2026-10-13 | Subscribable events: a durable type of the catalogue without audience `self` whose one receive action is in the scope table (with a scope or none), or `interaction.created` and `command.invoked` (receivers `agent_or_app`, scope `events:subscribe`); every other type is refused (`access.changed`, `draft.*`, `agent.invocation`, `grant.changed`, `host.updated`, ...). The scope of the receive action must be requested, and `events:subscribe` whenever `events` is not empty. `delivery` is required with events, commands or components; `events_url` required for `webhook` and forbidden otherwise; `interactions_url` required for commands or components without the gateway; `data_egress` required with events or `interactions_url`. Manifest commands: at most `command.max_per_principal`, names unique without regard to case and not reserved, description at most `command.description_max_chars`, option names unique, no target (`scope_kind` absent or `instance`, no `scope_id`). | 11 3.1 and 3.2 rules 1, 2 and 5; 05 2.6 row "Event subscriptions"; 11 4.1: a manifest never names a target. |
| D-M2-24-5 | 2026-10-13 | Delivery addresses are absolute `https` URLs with a host, without user information and fragment, whose host (lower case, without port) equals an entry of `webhook.allowed_hosts` without regard to case and without wildcards; otherwise the manifest is refused with `webhook_host_not_allowed`. `homepage_url` must be absolute `https`, its host is not compared because the server never loads it. 11 3.2 rule 3 now says that the operator adds a missing host to the process configuration. | `webhook.allowed_hosts` is process configuration that changes only at start (configuration.md 1, 03 9); the consent cannot add a host "in the same confirmed action" (spec gap, sentence changed in 11). |
| D-M2-24-6 | 2026-10-13 | `installApp`: authorize `app.install` on the instance before anything is loaded; handle = `slug`, display name = `name`; every required scope must be granted, any optional scope may be; absent `event_subscriptions` means none; one unit of work declared as a rights change (permission_version first) creates principal, `apps` and the `active` installation and writes the allow entry. An app whose installations are all uninstalled is reinstalled: same principal, status back to `active`, display name and manifest columns replaced, a new installation; its revoked tokens and grants stay revoked. A handle taken by another principal and a concurrent install of the same slug answer `409 conflict`. | 11 4.1 and 03 6.3; reusing the principal keeps the authorship of the app's messages (11 4.3) and the unique `lower(handle)`. |
| D-M2-24-7 | 2026-10-13 | `upgradeAppManifest` (`app.install`): same `slug`; changed content needs another `version` (a lower one is allowed, rollback); an identical manifest with identical lists is without effect (`200`, no rights version, audit `no_change`); granted scopes and subscriptions are subsets of the current ones (new ones need `setAppScopes`), so what the new manifest drops acts at once; the upgrade never adds a grant; it is itself the consent for new delivery hosts (owner, step-up), which must be in `webhook.allowed_hosts` and are named in `delivery_hosts_added`. | 11 4.2 (changed: "New scopes and events need a new consent"); `manifest_version` names the content in the console and the audit; a host change is consented by the step-up action that uploads it. |
| D-M2-24-8 | 2026-10-13 | Installation, grants and tokens are separate steps: `app.install` creates no token and no grant; grants come per target with `grant.create` (M1-29 `Admin.CreateGrant`, HTTP with M1-48), tokens with `createToken`. `requested_grants` only inform the consent. 11 4.1 says so below the diagram. | `AppInstall` has no member for targets and `App` no secret; the sequence diagram of 11 4.1 shows the whole consent flow, not one transaction. |
| D-M2-24-9 | 2026-10-13 | Calls without effect are decided before the rights change: `upgradeAppManifest`, `setAppScopes`, `suspendApp`, `resumeApp` and `uninstallApp` first load in a unit without rights change and, when nothing changes, write the allow entry with `no_change: true` there; otherwise the rights change locks `apps` and the installation and checks again (a concurrent equal change ends as no change). `suspendApp` and `resumeApp` of an uninstalled app answer `409 invalid_state_transition`; `setAppScopes` and `upgradeAppManifest` of an uninstalled app `409 conflict`. | 02 8.1: phase 1 of a rights change always raises `permission_version`, and R-API-27 forbids a rights version for a no-op (the M1-29 pattern). |
| D-M2-24-10 | 2026-10-13 | App tokens and the scope check: `createToken` for an app locks the active installation FOR SHARE (none, suspended or uninstalled: `409 conflict` `app_not_installed`); scopes absent or empty take the granted scopes at this moment, present ones must be granted (`400` at `/scopes/<i>`). The authenticator reads the installation of an app token on every request; an app request needs the action's scope in `GrantedScopes` and in the token scopes (`actor.Scopes`); without a token (webhook delivery) the installation alone decides. A removed scope acts on the next request; a scope granted later needs a new token. | Decides the open points of 08 12; replaces the part of M1-28 D6 that token scopes are not evaluated. The share lock serializes with an uninstall that holds the installation FOR UPDATE, so no live token is created for an uninstalled app. |
| D-M2-24-11 | 2026-10-13 | The installation is part of the credential: the API token path (REST and the gateway `identify`) applies the installation check, `Revalidate` fails with `app_not_installed` when the installation is no longer active or no longer grants a scope the actor carries, and the rights snapshot of an app without an active installation is empty (`PrincipalFacts.AppInstallationActive`), so suspend and uninstall write `access.changed` for the app's targets and resume adds them back. Known gap of the core contract (04 8.2): a change of the granted scopes alone changes no visible target, writes no `access.changed`, and an open gateway connection learns it only at its next `identify`, `resume` or `Revalidate`; REST and webhook deliveries see it at once. `TestAppAccessChanged_SuspendResumeUninstall` asserts the gap so a later fix shows up. | Answers "how long-lived connections recheck the state of the installation" (08 12); the gateway (M1-64, M1-65) is not on the base, so its test waits for those tickets. |
| D-M2-24-12 | 2026-10-13 | Ports and wiring: `apps/app` uses consumer interfaces of its own (`Principals` with `IdentityPrincipals` over `identity.Directory` and the new `identity.PrincipalWrites`, `Tokens`, `Grants`, `Rights`, `Scopes`, `Cursors`); `internal/wire` builds the `authz.AdminService` for the grant revocation over the pool and binds the PrincipalSource of identity with the installations of apps everywhere (Authorizer, rights hook, expiry, admin). The new start step `apps` (6e) runs after `identity`. | The domain imports only the kernel core (02 5); the app layer turns facts and configuration into `domain.Env`. |
| D-M2-24-13 | 2026-10-13 | `listApps` lists apps whose latest installation is `active` or `suspended`, ordered by `lower(slug)` then principal id, keyset cursor bound to the caller; `getApp` returns any app, uninstalled included, with its latest installation. `listApps` and `validateManifest` authorize `app.read` on the collection of apps (resource kind `app` without id): authz treats that collection as visible like the instance, so a non-owner gets `403 forbidden` with the reason `owner_only`; one app stays invisible to a non-owner (`404`, AZ-P4). | 06 4 point 2 (container check); the catalogue gives `app.read` only the kind `app`, so the collection is that kind without an id (`TestAppCollection_OwnerOnly`). |
| D-M2-24-14 | 2026-10-13 | Audit details (flat, ids, codes, integers, booleans and lists of codes; never names, descriptions, addresses, `data_egress` or the manifest): install `app_id`, `installation_id`, `slug`, `manifest_sha256` (lower-case hex), `granted_scopes`, `event_subscriptions`, `delivery_hosts`, `reinstall`, `reason`; upgrade adds `previous_manifest_sha256` and the added and removed manifest scopes, events and commands, `delivery_hosts_added`, `granted_scopes_removed`, `event_subscriptions_removed`; scope change the added and removed scopes and subscriptions; suspend and resume `app_id`, `installation_id`; uninstall also `tokens_revoked`, `grants_revoked`, `reason`; `no_change: true` for a call without effect. `manifest_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the manifest bytes as received, `apps.manifest` keeps the parsed content as jsonb. A delivery host that is no audit code (an address literal, longer than a code or outside its characters) stands in `delivery_hosts` and `delivery_hosts_added` as `sha256:` and the first 32 hex digits of its SHA-256. | SEC-AUD-5; the hash names exactly what the owner uploaded; an allowed host must never make the audit entry and with it the install fail, and an address never reaches the audit (20 4.5). |
| D-M2-24-15 | 2026-10-13 | ADR 0022 confirmation: a `depguard` rule `no-plugin-in-server` in `services/server/.golangci.yml` forbids the standard-library package `plugin` in every file of the server module; the red run on a throwaway file in a scratch worktree is quoted in the ticket report and the file was never committed. | The vendor allow list of `modules.yaml` already refuses a WebAssembly or other runtime in `make arch`; `plugin` is standard library and not covered there. |
| D-M2-24-16 | 2026-10-13 | `bothy_app` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `apps` and `app_installations` and no `DELETE`; retention classes `account` and `config` (03). The trigger `apps_principal_kind_check` (SQLSTATE `23514`) refuses an `apps` row whose principal is not of kind `app`. | Rows are never deleted (03 1 point 7, M1-12 D4); same pattern as `identity_users_kind_check`. |
| D-M2-24-17 | 2026-10-13 | `platform/httpserver` keeps the JSON body that `StageBody` checked in the request context (`httpserver.RawJSONBody`); the app handlers hash and store the manifest member exactly as received from it. | The generated strict server hands a handler only the decoded value, and a re-encoding would change the bytes the hash must cover (11 3.1). |
| D-M2-24-18 | 2026-10-13 | The base lacks `routes.SpecVersion`; this branch carries the M1-46 generator commits that add it (`feat(api): spec version, gateway protocol versions and problem catalogue from the contract`, `style(api): gofmt of the generator meta test`) unchanged, so `min_api_version` is compared with the real spec version and the later merge with M1-46 brings identical content. | The brief asks to extend the generator "exactly as M1-46 Scope item 10 describes"; taking M1-46's own commits is the exact form and avoids a second, diverging implementation. |
| D-M2-24-19 | 2026-10-13 | The ticket is built on `feat/M2-24-apps-install` from `int/m1-wave12` (`da08f58`) in its own worktree and is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it. Rulings stand in this section in the table format of the base. M1 tickets outside the closure on the base: M1-31 (yes); M1-19 (yes); M1-46, M1-48, M1-51, M1-53 to M1-57, M1-64, M1-65 (no). The AZ cases and the `access.changed` assertions run in tier 1 (real installation, token, authenticator, Authorizer and rights hook, grants from `authz.Admin.CreateGrant`, structure from the fake source); tier 2 and the gateway test wait for M1-56, M1-57, M1-64 and M1-65. | The launcher's task takes precedence over the brief's merge step; the brief allows only merged tickets (design note 17). |
| D-M2-24-20 | 2026-10-13 | Names that differ from the brief: the token scopes of the actor are the existing field `actor.Actor.Scopes` (no new `TokenScopes`), read through the new `authorize.Principal.AppTokenScopes`; the principal port methods are `identity.PrincipalWrites` (`CreatePrincipal`, `SetPrincipalStatus`, `UpdateDisplayName`); `audit.reason_max_chars` is registered in `configkeys_audit.go` (its area), the other keys in `configkeys_apps.go`; the scope table port also lists the scopes (`AppScopes.Scopes`). | The brief says to add `TokenScopes` only if missing and to follow the merged names; M1-51 may reuse `PrincipalWrites` for agents. |
| D-M2-24-21 | 2026-10-13 | A manifest whose JSON holds the escape of NUL (`\u0000`) or of a surrogate that is not part of a pair is invalid at the root pointer (`invalid`); values taken from a manifest (delivery hosts, command names, the slug) enter the audit through one helper that writes a value that is no audit code as `sha256:` and 32 hex digits. | PostgreSQL refuses both escapes in `jsonb`, and the audit refuses codes longer than 64 bytes or address literals; without the rule and the helper a manifest that passes every check made install or upgrade answer 500 (red team M2-24, SEC-IN-4, SEC-T06). One rule and one helper fix the class instead of each value. |
| D-M2-24-22 | 2026-10-13 | A keyed call (`Idempotency-Key`) of `suspendApp`, `resumeApp`, `uninstallApp`, `setAppScopes` or `upgradeAppManifest` that loses a race decides the call without effect inside its rights change: the plan runs again under the locks and writes the allow entry with `no_change`, and the rights version rises once. | The idempotency stage runs the handler in one unit, so the rights change is a late one whose error fails the whole unit even when swallowed; the losing caller got 500. Design note 9 g accepts the one extra version step for this rare case; unkeyed calls still roll back and plan again without it. |
| D-M2-24-23 | 2026-10-13 | The integration world of the module apps lives in `services/server/test/appsworld`; its tests are split over `internal/apps/app`, `internal/apps/adapters/httpapi` and `internal/apps/adapters/postgres`. | `make coverage` measures each package only by its own tests, so the use cases and the repository counted as uncovered while the HTTP tests ran through them, and the server total fell below the threshold. Code under `test/` is test code (02 section 6, Code layout) and outside the measurement. |

## M8-01 Discord read client

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M8-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commit; the English docs of main win. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. The brief relies on no core rule and no contract change; D-W-51, D-W-100, D-W-120 and D-W-124 are planning rulings in this file. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request (precedent D-M3-17-8 and M1-23/3). |
| D-M8-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The tool code lives under `services/server/cmd/bothy/importtool/` (D-W-124 (1)); the client is `cmd/bothy/importtool/discord`. `TestDiscordClientImportsNoServerModule` holds that `go list -deps` of the package contains no package under `internal/` except `internal/platform/egress` and `internal/kernel/...`. | `bothy import` is a subcommand of the one binary (component `cmd`), which may use every package; the test keeps the tool on the public API (22 2, R-IM-01). |
| D-M8-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | Rate-limit guard: the route key is the path template plus the guild or channel ID; the bucket is the `X-RateLimit-Bucket` Discord last named for the route, else the path template, again plus that ID. A missing or unparsable wait (`retry_after`, `Retry-After`, and also `X-RateLimit-Reset-After` with `Remaining: 0`) counts as `import.discord.max_rate_wait`; any wait above it ends the call before sleeping with `ErrRateWaitTooLong`, also the wait for an exhausted bucket. After a waited-out `429` the bucket's refill time is cleared, so the retry does not wait twice. The retries after the last allowed `429` end with the new sentinel `ErrRateLimited` (status `429`). `5xx` (`ErrServer`) and transport errors are never retried in the client. | 22 4.3 items 1 to 4: fail towards waiting, never towards hammering (RK-76); the mirror's backoff is M8-05, an import run ends with exit `1` (22 10.3). |
| D-M8-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | `TestClientHasOnlyGet` type-checks the non-test files of the package from source against export data (the approach of `internal/wire/counters_static_test.go`): exactly one call of `http.NewRequest` or `http.NewRequestWithContext`, whose method argument is the object `net/http.MethodGet`; no `http.Request` literal and no use of the field `Method` of a request; no exported name, method or field that matches `post`, `put`, `patch` or `delete` (case-insensitive); no exported function with a parameter named like a method or verb. `TestFakeSeesOnlyGet` drives every exported method against the fake, which answers every other method `405`; `TestNoTestPackageInBinary` lists the dependencies of `cmd/bothy`. | 22 16 "Discord client without a write method", ADR 0043 ruling 1; the literal and field checks close the other ways to send another verb with the one construction. |
| D-M8-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | `MessagesAfter`, `MessagesBefore` and `MessagesAround` return messages in ascending snowflake order, sorted by the numeric value, whatever order Discord used. | Discord returns newest first; the import appends strictly in Discord order (22 6.2 `thread_seq`), so no caller may see another order. |
| D-M8-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | Additions to the names of the brief (D-W-100): `Config.Egress` carries the `egress.*` values of the tool (allowed hosts, timeout, response size limit), because only an operator rule with `allow_private` may open the loopback fake and a consumer policy never can; the host policy of the client names exactly the host and port of `import.discord.api_base` over `https`. The `User-Agent` URL is the constant `https://github.com/OWNER/bothy` (the placeholder of `repo_url` in `mkdocs.yml`), the version comes from `Config.UserAgentVersion`. Further sentinels beside the brief's: `ErrRateLimited`, `ErrUnexpectedStatus` (any other status, a redirect included), `ErrDecode` (a body that is not the expected JSON, without its content), `ErrInvalidID`, `ErrTokenHostMismatch`; `CallError` has a `Hint` (the missing `MANAGE_THREADS`). `Attachment.URL` and `Embed.URL` are of type `SignedURL`, whose print forms show scheme, host and path only and whose `Raw` returns the full link (M8-02). `Deps.Sleep` is a `Sleeper` function. The fixture holds archive pages explicitly; a request beyond the last page gets the last page again. Fixture and test IDs are invented 8-digit strings, because the white-label rule of `tools/denylist/generic.rules` flags 17 to 20 digits as real Discord snowflakes; the client accepts 1 to 20 digits that fit an unsigned 64-bit number. | The brief allows following the code where names differ; each addition keeps a rule of the brief (no proxy, token only to the API base, no secret in output) provable. |
| D-M8-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | `TestTokenOnlyToAPIBase` checks the unexported header setter directly with another host, plain `http` and another port (refused, no header), and a redirect of the fake to a second host name, which the client does not follow (`ErrUnexpectedStatus`, one request, all on the API host). | Every method builds its URL from the API base, so only the setter itself and a redirect can aim a request elsewhere. |

## M7-01 Image bothy-postgres with pgBackRest

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The image lives in `services/server/images/bothy-postgres/` (Dockerfile, `Dockerfile.dockerignore`, `entrypoint.sh`, `check-pgbackrest-version.sh`, README), not in `images/bothy-postgres` of the plan. | The image is an artifact of the component `server` (18 2); there the component path filter of `tools/ci/components.py` sees it. |
