The brief's `15-client-architecture.md`, `04-sync-protocol.md` and `09-agents.md` are `15-client-architektur.md`, `04-sync-protokoll.md` and `09-agenten.md` on the base. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-16-1 | 2026-10-15 | The store depends on no transport and no `api_client` type: whole documents come through the port `RunDocumentFetcher.fetchRun(RunId)` and frames through `FrameTicker.requestFrame(callback)`; `run.updated` payloads come in as decoded maps. The ticker is a request per dirty period instead of a stream of ticks, so an idle store asks for no frame. | Goal and Design note 1 of the brief; M4-17 wires the ports to the dispatcher of M4-14, `getRun` and the scheduler of the app. |
| D-M4-16-2 | 2026-10-15 | An invalid patch (an `op` other than `add`, `replace`, `remove`, a missing target or parent, a missing `value`, a malformed pointer, the root pointer, a path through a non-container, `ops` that is not a list of objects, a frame with `ops` but without `base_version`) is a gap: the store refetches the whole document and never applies a part. `applyRunPatch` copies the root and the touched branches only and leaves its input unchanged. | Design note 2 of the brief; a partial state is a state the server never had (04 11.3 item 4). |
| D-M4-16-3 | 2026-10-15 | Coalescing per frame: every change marks its run dirty and requests one frame; at the frame each listener of a dirty run gets the newest document (null when the run is not focused, ended or has no document) unless it has seen exactly that state. A new listener gets the current state at the next frame, not synchronously. | Design note 1 and 15 7.4: the interface draws at most once per frame. |
| D-M4-16-4 | 2026-10-15 | During a refetch of a run its frames with ops wait in a queue; when the fetch returns, the fetched document replaces the local one only when its version is newer, and the queued frames run through the rule again in arrival order (older ones drop, one that still does not fit refetches again). A `snapshot` applies at once also during a fetch. A failed fetch keeps the last good document and drops the queue, so an always failing fetch cannot loop; the next frame or `focus` retries. Each fetch takes a new generation number; a result counts only while its run still waits for that number, so `unfocus`, `onRunEnded` or a newer focus make it stale. The frame's `version` is written into the document after a patch or a snapshot. | Design note 1 of the brief. |
| D-M4-16-5 | 2026-10-15 | `RunDocument` reads `stall_warned_at` from the raw map and gives null without it; the API schema `Run` of this base does not name the field (requirement 10 of the brief; the report recommends the optional field). A step without `ordinal` sorts after those with one in document order, a step whose parent is missing is a top-level step, and steps on a parent cycle appear at the top level, so every step appears exactly once. | Requirement 10 and Design note 3 of the brief; the server assigns `ordinal`, a step is never lost from the view. |
| D-M4-16-6 | 2026-10-17 | The ticket was ported from its integration base onto main with only its own commit; main's sections stay and this section joins them, the integration rows of the base are not carried over. | Port onto main (PR lane). |

The member is never written. | A missing translation breaks the build and a stale generation fails (brief note 2), without touching the working tree. |

The fixtures write the dash and the emoji as JSON escapes, so the fixture files pass the repository's text rules while the parsed values hold the characters. | The property reserves the unassigned code points of the emoji blocks, so later emoji fall inside the ranges; escapes keep the `text-rules` hook valid for every file. |

Rows with alternatives become one pair each, a parenthesis in the German cell is a note. The generator is a Dart tool (`packages/l10n/tool/term_table.dart`) that `test/term_table_test.dart` also runs as the check, reading the chapter by walking up to the repository root; it reads `docs/spec/01-product.md`. | The template is English (brief note 4); a Dart tool keeps the Markdown parsing next to its only consumer without a new Python tool package. |

"Sending delayed" has no wording in 01 yet; the key follows the brief. | Brief note 8: the keys M4-07 and M4-18 need first. |

## M4-10 Device key for macOS and iOS

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-10-0 | 2026-10-17 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win (chapters `08-identity.md` and `15-client-architecture.md`), and text this ticket adds is English. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M4-10-1 | 2026-10-15 | `device_key` is one Flutter plugin package with a folder per platform and a Pigeon interface (`DeviceKeyHostApi`: `ensureKey`, `keyInfo`, `sign` (async), `attestation`, `deleteKeys`), not a set of federated packages. Errors cross the channel only as the codes `user_cancelled`, `key_invalidated`, `not_available` and `internal` with a fixed text; the Dart side keeps only the code, and a platform without the plugin (Pigeon's `channel-error`, or `MissingPluginException`) reports `not_available`. A missing key on `sign` or `attestation` is `not_available`; an enclave key the Secure Enclave no longer accepts is `key_invalidated`: the enclave loads any blob and rejects it only when it signs, with CryptoTokenKit `corruptedData` or `objectNotFound` (canceledByUser is `user_cancelled`), and a blob that no longer yields the registered public key counts the same (red team M4-10). Which code a real biometrics change produces is not measured on a device with Touch ID. | 15 3 names one member `device_key`; the Pigeon interface is the boundary federation asks for. Fixed texts keep key material and server addresses out of logs. |
| D-M4-10-2 | 2026-10-15 | The public JWK (`crv`, `kty`, `x`, `y`, base64url without padding) and the RFC 7638 thumbprint are computed in Dart from the coordinates the native side returns; the native side returns signatures as `R \|\| S` (CryptoKit's `rawRepresentation`, so no DER conversion is needed), and the Dart side rejects any other length. | One implementation for every platform (M4-11, M5-01 reuse it). |
| D-M4-10-3 | 2026-10-15 | Access control: `dpop` with `[.privateKeyUsage]` and `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`; `step_up` with `[.privateKeyUsage, .biometryCurrentSet]` when biometrics are enrolled, otherwise `[.privateKeyUsage, .userPresence]`, and `WhenPasscodeSetThisDeviceOnly` on iOS and macOS (the Data Protection keychain knows the same class). Without an enclave the software key's keychain item carries `.userPresence` for `step_up`, with the same accessibility classes, and `hardwareBound` is false. Biometrics count as enrolled unless the device has no sensor or reports `biometryNotEnrolled`, so a key created while the lid is closed or the sensor is locked out is still bound to them (code review M4-10). 15 9 is updated to this. | 08 6.1 requires invalidation on new biometrics, while a Mac without Touch ID must still confirm with the device password (brief note 3). |
| D-M4-10-4 | 2026-10-15 | The core is the Swift package `DeviceKeyCore` (CryptoKit, Security and LocalAuthentication for the biometrics check) inside the plugin's Swift package folder, `darwin/device_key/DeviceKeyCore`, and the plugin package depends on it by the path `DeviceKeyCore`. The plugin uses Swift Package Manager only (enabled by default in Flutter 3.47.7), without a podspec. The shared access group of the `dpop` key comes from the Info.plist key `DeviceKeyAccessGroup`, read when the plugin registers; the extensions read the same key. | Flutter links a plugin's Swift package through a symbolic link, so a path leaving its folder (`../DeviceKeyCore`) would resolve beside the link; the extensions (M5-04, M5-06) add the same core package (15 9). The pinned Flutter no longer needs CocoaPods for plugins (brief note 4). The plugin registrar takes no arguments, so the build configuration reaches the plugin through Info.plist. |
| D-M4-10-5 | 2026-10-15 | `testHardwarePathOnAppleChip` runs only where `SecureEnclave.isAvailable` and otherwise reports the skip `secure enclave not available on this host`; it signs only with the `dpop` key. `testKeychainRoundTrip` skips when the test process cannot write to the Data Protection keychain (`errSecMissingEntitlement` for an unsigned test binary). `darwin/run_ios_sim_tests.sh` prints a SKIP line and exits 0 on a host without Xcode and its simulator; with Xcode a missing iPhone simulator fails. The enclave form of the step-up key is tested through the policy the manager passes to the enclave (`KeyPolicy.policy`), because creating an enclave key with `WhenPasscodeSetThisDeviceOnly` needs an unlocked user session, which a build host reached over ssh does not have (`errSecInteractionNotAllowed`). | CI runners are virtual machines without an enclave (brief note 5); the hardware path counts as proven only on a host with one. |
| D-M4-10-6 | 2026-10-15 | The Swift tests use Swift Testing instead of XCTest, and the first entry of `platform-tests.json` is `["sh", "darwin/run_core_tests.sh"]` instead of a bare `swift test`: the script passes the Swift Testing paths of the command line tools when no Xcode is installed and runs plain `swift test` otherwise. | The command line tools ship Swift Testing but no XCTest, so a Mac with an Apple chip and without Xcode can prove the Secure Enclave path; Xcode 16 on the `macos-15` runner runs Swift Testing with `swift test` and `xcodebuild test`. |
| D-M4-10-7 | 2026-10-17 | `sonar-project.properties`: `**/Tests/**` counts as test code (SwiftPM test targets), the Pigeon output `*.g.swift`, `*.g.kt`, `*.g.cpp` and `*.g.h` is excluded like the other generated code, and the native platform code under `packages/*/darwin/**`, `packages/*/android/**` and `packages/*/windows/**` is out of the coverage measure; it stays in the analysis for issues. | SonarQube Cloud read `DeviceKeyCoreTests.swift` as product code (47 hard-coded URI findings and a keychain finding in the test probe) and counted the Swift sources as uncovered (5.6 % coverage on the pull request): the Swift suite runs on the macOS runner through `platform-tests.json` and writes no report the Linux scan can read. The Windows and Android members of `device_key` are the same case. |

## M5-01 Device key for Android

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M5-01-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave23` at `d2d32fa`, branch `feat/M5-01-device-key-android` in its own worktree; this build neither merges locally nor pushes, the PR lane lands it. Every rule the brief cites (R-CL-04, R-CL-05, R-ID-11, D-L10) is on the base, so nothing is ported from `main`. The base names the chapters `docs/spec/15-client-architektur.md` and `08-identitaet.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Names that differ from the brief: the Kotlin packages are `devicekey.flutter` (plugin, generated `DeviceKeyApi.g.kt`) and `devicekey.core` (core module), without the product name (02 6); the plugin has its own Gradle wrapper (Gradle 9.3.1 with a pinned distribution checksum) and AGP 9.1.0, Kotlin 2.4.0 and compile SDK 36 of the pinned Flutter's plugin template; the instrumented tests run with `connectedDebugAndroidTest` and the JVM tests with `testDebugUnitTest`. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's landing step; merged and tool layouts win over the brief's names (D-W-100, D-W-120). |
| D-M5-01-1 | 2026-10-15 | Key parameters (brief note 2): alias `<prefix>.<sha256(scope)>.<purpose>` (lowercase hex, prefix `device_key`, tests `test.device_key.<uuid>`), `KeyPairGenerator` `EC` in `AndroidKeyStore` with `secp256r1`, purpose `SIGN`, digest `SHA256`. `dpop` without user authentication. `step_up` with `setUserAuthenticationRequired(true)`, from API 30 `setUserAuthenticationParameters(0, AUTH_BIOMETRIC_STRONG or AUTH_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL)`, before it `setUserAuthenticationValidityDurationSeconds(-1)`, and `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true)`. From API 28 StrongBox is tried first and the key is generated again without it on `StrongBoxUnavailableException`; below API 28 an `SDK_INT` check skips it. A missing step-up key is created only when `BiometricManager.canAuthenticate` accepts the authenticators of the API level, otherwise `not_available`. | 08 6.1, 15 9; the alias keeps server addresses out of the Keystore, as on Apple (D-M4-10-3). |
| D-M5-01-2 | 2026-10-15 | The attestation is the key's certificate chain from `setAttestationChallenge`: the DER certificates, leaf first, concatenated (the channel member is bytes and the DER framing separates them). Android binds the challenge when it creates the key, so `attestation` creates a missing key with the challenge and returns its chain, and returns null for an existing key and where the device does not attest (a chain of one certificate); the app asks for the attestation before `ensure` when it registers. | M4-10 defined the member as bytes without an encoding; returning a chain with an old challenge would look like a fresh attestation. |
| D-M5-01-3 | 2026-10-15 | Device credential rule: from API 30 the step-up accepts strong biometrics or the device credential (the prompt then has no negative button); on API 26 to 29 strong biometrics only (the prompt shows the negative button from Dart), and a device without enrolled biometrics on those levels gets `not_available` for `step_up`. Written into 15 9 and the Android row of 08 6.1. | A per-operation key cannot be unlocked with the device credential before API 30 (brief note 3); the app explains that step-up needs biometrics there. |
| D-M5-01-4 | 2026-10-15 | Signatures: `SHA256withECDSA`, DER converted to `R \|\| S` with strict DER parsing (minimal integers, at most 33 bytes, left padding to 32). `hardwareBound`: from API 31 `KeyInfo.securityLevel` StrongBox or trusted environment, before it `isInsideSecureHardware`. Errors leave with a fixed text and never with a cause: `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException` is `key_invalidated`; `UserNotAuthenticatedException` and, from API 33, `android.security.KeyStoreException` with `ERROR_USER_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` (what the Keystore throws at `sign` for a step-up signature without the prompt) are `not_available`; below API 33 that case is `internal`, because the class is hidden there. Prompt errors: cancel, negative button and system cancel are `user_cancelled`; no hardware, nothing enrolled, no device credential and lockout are `not_available`; others `internal`. A step-up signature without prompt texts is `internal`. | Brief note 4; the emulator at API 36 showed the `KeyStoreException` path, not `UserNotAuthenticatedException`. |
| D-M5-01-5 | 2026-10-15 | `apps_platform.py` knows the system `android`, which is no host: its commands run on any host when `adb devices` lists exactly one device in state `device`, print `SKIP ... android: no device` without one (or without `adb`) and fail with two or more; `--system android` runs only them. The CI job `apps-android` (public phase, `ubuntu-24.04`, kvm enabled, Temurin 17, `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner` pinned by SHA, API 26 and 36, `x86_64`, `google_apis`) runs `apps_platform.py --system android` and feeds `ci-ok` like `apps-macos`. `make test` runs `./gradlew -p <module> testDebugUnitTest` for every Gradle module with a tracked `src/test/` under `packages/*/android/`; the jobs `gates` and `sonar` install Temurin 17 and the Android SDK (`android-actions/setup-android`) when such a wrapper is tracked. The run script sets a temporary PIN on an emulator without one (and clears it at the end), because the step-up key needs a secure lock screen; it never touches a physical device's lock screen. | Brief note 5 and its requirement that the JVM tests run in `make test`; an emulator can run on Linux and macOS, so a host check alone cannot select it. |
| D-M5-01-6 | 2026-10-15 | The Kotlin core is the Gradle library module `android/devicekeycore` with its own build file and tests. The plugin library compiles the module's main sources into itself (`sourceSets`), and its standalone `settings.gradle.kts` includes the module for the tests. An app (the push service of M5-05, the share activity of M5-06) uses `DeviceKeyCore.forContext` through its dependency on the plugin project `:device_key` and does not include `devicekeycore` as a second project, which would duplicate the classes. Both build files list the same dependencies (`androidx.biometric` 1.1.0); the plugin adds `kotlinx-coroutines-android` for Pigeon's `suspend` handler. | Flutter includes a plugin as one Gradle project and ignores its settings file, so a project dependency on the module would break every app build that does not include it by hand. |
| D-M5-01-7 | 2026-10-15 | M4-10 had no prompt texts on the channel, so the Pigeon source gains `PromptTextsMessage` (`title`, optional `subtitle`, `negativeButton`) as an optional last parameter of `sign`; the Swift host ignores it. The Dart API stays source compatible: `DeviceKeyClient.sign` takes an optional named `prompt` of the new type `StepUpPrompt`. Pigeon writes `analyzer: exclude: [android/**]` into the member's `analysis_options.yaml` when it generates Kotlin; the folder holds no Dart, so it stays. | Brief note 1; the texts come from `packages/l10n` (rule 8). |
| D-M5-01-8 | 2026-10-17 | The ticket was ported from its integration base onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win (the Android rows of `08-identity.md` 6.1 and `15-client-architecture.md` 9 and the `devicekeycore` paragraph there). The CI job `apps-android` with a matrix becomes the two jobs `apps (Android API 26)` and `apps (Android API 36)`, and `ci-ok` accepts each as `success` or `skipped` through a rule of `tools/ci/ci_ok.py` instead of the `needs` list of the base. | `ci_ok.py` on main requires its jobs by name and has no `needs` (the same reason as D-M7-01-7 for `postgres-image`). `sonar.test.inclusions` also names `**/androidTest/**`, so the instrumented tests count as tests like the SwiftPM tests of D-M4-10-7. |

## M4-11 Device key for Windows

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-11-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave23` at `d2d32fa`, branch `feat/M4-11-device-key-windows` in its own worktree; this build neither merges locally nor pushes, the PR lane lands it. Every rule the brief cites (R-CL-04, R-CL-05, R-CL-12, R-ID-11, D-L10) is on the base, so nothing is ported from `main`. The base names the chapters `docs/spec/15-client-architektur.md` and `08-identitaet.md`; text added there is English (as D-M4-10-0). Names that differ from the brief: the core has, besides `key_provider.h`, `cng_key_provider.cpp`, `device_key_store.cpp` and `.h`, the headers `cng_key_provider.h` and `marker_store.h` with `credential_marker_store.cpp` (the marker of note 4 behind its own interface), and the tests have `fake_key_provider.h`, `test_support.h`, `main.cpp` and `device_key_store_test.cpp`; no `der.cpp`, because `NCryptSignHash` returns `R \|\| S` for ECDSA. `codegen-check` already hashes every file under the member, so the Makefile only gains a comment that the C++ outputs are covered. `packages/device_key/analysis_options.yaml` gains `analyzer: exclude: [windows/**]`, which the pinned Flutter's `flutter pub get` writes itself once a plugin has a `windows/` folder ("Upgrading analysis_options.yaml to exclude build and platform directories"); the folder holds no Dart code. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's landing step; merged layouts win over the brief's names (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-11-1 | 2026-10-15 | Provider choice per key at creation, never migrated: a new `dpop` key goes to `MS_PLATFORM_CRYPTO_PROVIDER` when it opens and `NCryptIsAlgSupported(BCRYPT_ECDSA_P256_ALGORITHM)` succeeds, otherwise to `MS_KEY_STORAGE_PROVIDER` with `hardwareBound = false`. A marker (generic credential in the user's Credential Manager, `CRED_PERSIST_LOCAL_MACHINE`, target name = key name, blob `platform` or `software`) records the provider; a key is opened only there. Without a marker (a stop between `NCryptFinalizeKey` and writing it) an existing key is looked up in the platform provider, then the software provider, used where it is and the marker written. A marker without an openable key (`NTE_BAD_KEYSET`, `NTE_NO_KEY`, `NTE_NOT_FOUND`) is `key_invalidated` and nothing is recreated; a marker whose provider no longer opens is `not_available`. Keys are per user (no `NCRYPT_MACHINE_KEY_FLAG`), ECDSA P-256, export policy `0` set before `NCryptFinalizeKey`. | A key moved between providers would change the JWK and need a new device (08 5.4); the marker is how the plugin tells "not created yet" from "lost" (brief note 4). Which status a real TPM clear produces is not measured. |
| D-M4-11-2 | 2026-10-15 | No step-up key on Windows: `Ensure(step_up)` fails with `not_available`, and the plugin never offers a key whose confirmation only the plugin enforces. The DPoP key signs with `NCRYPT_SILENT_FLAG`. Sources, retrieved 2026-10-15: strong key protection (`NCRYPT_UI_FORCE_HIGH_PROTECTION_FLAG`) prompts for a password whose "exact behavior is specified by the KSP" (learn.microsoft.com, `X509PrivateKeyProtection`); the Microsoft KSPs accept that password from the process through `NCRYPT_PIN_PROPERTY` and "cache this value so that the user is only prompted once per process" (Key Storage Property Identifiers); a Microsoft answer on per-use reauthentication for Platform Crypto Provider keys names only a PIN or a custom KSP (Microsoft Q&A 1076208); Windows Hello keys (`KeyCredentialManager`) are RSA and sign with SHA-256 and PKCS #1 (Windows Hello for apps). Not checked on a Windows host: no Windows machine was reachable. Recommendation (08 6.1): a Windows Hello `RS256` step-up key needs `RS256` or `PS256` in 03 `device_keys.alg` and in the JOSE profile of ADR 0067, a core contract change; until then the step-up of a Windows client uses the other methods of 08 6. | 08 6.1: the confirmation must come from the person, not from code in the process; a password the process can set or that is cached per process does not meet that, and `ES256` is the only algorithm of the contract (R-CL-04). |
| D-M4-11-3 | 2026-10-15 | The CTest suite uses a minimal harness `windows/core/tests/check.h` (`TEST`, `CHECK`, `REQUIRE`, `SKIP`) without a third-party framework and without exceptions: one executable runs the test named by its argument, CTest registers one test per name, exit code 77 is a skip (`SKIP_RETURN_CODE`). The core library is built with `_HAS_EXCEPTIONS=0` like Flutter's `apply_standard_settings` for the plugin, and every operation returns a `Status` instead of throwing. | No new dependency for a small suite; the same exception setting on both sides of the plugin boundary. |
| D-M4-11-4 | 2026-10-15 | `tpm_path_when_available` skips with `platform crypto provider not available on this host` when `MS_PLATFORM_CRYPTO_PROVIDER` does not open or lacks P-256, and the platform half of `keys_are_not_exportable` prints the same reason; the fallback runs always, through `FakeKeyProvider` (TPM missing, or a TPM without P-256 simulated on a software provider handle) and against the real software provider. Every test key and marker starts with `device-key-test-`; `main.cpp` deletes leftovers in both providers and the Credential Manager before and after each test. | Brief note 5; CI runners have no TPM, so the TPM path counts as proven only on a host with one. |
| D-M4-11-5 | 2026-10-17 | The ticket was ported from the integration squash of wave 27 (built against a base that already held M5-01) together with the integration fix INT-W27/2, which keeps the Windows plugin overriding `Sign` after M5-01 added the prompt texts to the Pigeon method; the English chapters of main take the spec changes (08 6.1 and K-ID-4, 15 9). | Port onto main (PR lane); M5-01 is merged before this ticket. |

## M7-22 Audit log checkpoints in object storage

The brief names these rows D-M7-22-1 to D-M7-22-4; they follow the numbering of this log (M7-22/2 to M7-22/5), and M7-22/1 and M7-22/6 are rulings of the build.

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M7-22/0 | 2026-10-16 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English (chapter and ADR names of main). | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| M7-22/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M7-22-audit-checkpoints-s3` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. The brief names the English chapter files of `main` (`19-security.md`, `12-files.md`, `03-data-model.md`, `20-privacy.md`); on this base they are `19-security.md`, `12-files.md`, `03-data-model.md` and `20-privacy.md`, and text this ticket adds there is English. Section 8 of `docs/operations/backup-restore.md`, which the brief builds on and which this base lacked, comes from `main` as its own commit. The process rulings D-W-100 and D-W-125 of `main` are not ported: they change no code. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; the brief's names follow `main` (D-W-100: the merged names win). |
| M7-22/2 | 2026-10-14 | Test service (D-M7-22-1): the SeaweedFS image the files driver pins (`chrislusf/seaweedfs:4.48` by digest, M3-02/13). Its S3 gateway supports bucket Object Lock with a default retention in mode `COMPLIANCE` (`CreateBucket` with `ObjectLockEnabledForBucket`, `PutObjectLockConfiguration`, `GetObjectLockConfiguration`; in a manual probe against the pinned image a locked version answered `DeleteObject` with its version ID with `AccessDenied`) and conditional writes (`PutObject` with `If-None-Match: *` on an existing key answers `412 PreconditionFailed`), shown against the pinned image by `TestLockCheck`, `TestConformance`, `TestObjectKeys` (retention mode of a stored object) and `TestOverwriteKeepsFirstVersion`; the SeaweedFS wiki page "Amazon S3 API" (https://github.com/seaweedfs/seaweedfs/wiki/Amazon-S3-API, retrieved 2026-10-14) lists `GetObjectLockConfiguration` and `PutObjectLockConfiguration` as supported and links "S3 Object Lock and Retention" and "S3 Conditional Operations" (`If-None-Match`). No second test service is pinned. The tests read the image from `internal/files/adapters/s3/seaweed_test.go`, so Renovate moves one pin. The tests of `adapters/httpapi` create locked buckets with `weed shell` (`s3.bucket.create -withLock`) inside the container and need no S3 client. | Design note 1: one free, pinned service with both features keeps one pin and the version the stack runs; the negative case "no lock" runs on the same service with a bucket without Object Lock. A real cloud bucket is out of scope (D-W-125, no cost). |
| M7-22/3 | 2026-10-14 | Write once and key form (D-M7-22-2, D-M7-22-3): the object is `[<prefix>/]<instance_id>/<seq with 20 digits, leading zeros>.jws`, the layout of the file sink. `Put` reads the oldest version of the key first (the same bytes count as stored, other bytes are `ErrCheckpointConflict`), then writes with `If-None-Match: *` and an SHA-256 checksum; on `412` or `409` of a concurrent writer the stored bytes decide the same way. `List` pages through `ListObjectVersions` below `[<prefix>/]<instance_id>/` (page size 1000, the tests 10), takes the oldest version of every checkpoint key, skips delete markers and other names, and returns the checkpoints in `seq` order. | The brief suggested `checkpoints/<seq>.jws`; the instance ID keeps two instances in one bucket apart, as the file sink does, and the same width keeps the key order the `seq` order. A service that ignores the header only adds a version on a locked bucket, and the oldest version is the one signed first; a bucket with a default retention requires an integrity header on uploads. |
| M7-22/4 | 2026-10-14 | New keys (D-M7-22-4): `audit.checkpoint_s3_endpoint` (empty, required with `s3://`), `audit.checkpoint_s3_region` (`us-east-1`), `audit.checkpoint_s3_use_path_style` (`true`), `audit.checkpoint_s3_access_key_ref` and `audit.checkpoint_s3_secret_key_ref` (`file:<secrets.dir>/audit_checkpoint_s3_access_key` and `..._secret_key`), all chapter 19; no time or size key of its own: the egress client of purpose `audit_checkpoint` keeps `egress.timeout` and `egress.max_response_bytes`. `retention.audit` (`365d`, at least `1d`, chapter 20) enters the schema with this first reader. The secret catalogue rows are supplied and go to the service `server` only. The cross-check `audit_checkpoint_sink` validates the endpoint, region, path style and the `allow_private` entry of an `http` endpoint at load (the rules of `file.s3.*`, copied into `s3sink.Config` across the module boundary). The SDK is allowed in `internal/audit/adapters/s3sink` (module `audit` gets `adapter_vendors: [awssdk]`, depguard and `TestS3SDKOnlyInAdapter` name the package); its interface holds only `PutObject`, `GetObject`, `ListObjectVersions` and `GetObjectLockConfiguration` (`TestAPIMethodSet`). | Own endpoint and credentials separate from file storage and backup (runbook 8 item 1). The brief also named `migrate` for the secret files "as the job runs there"; the job `audit.chain_verify` runs only in the server (roles `worker` and `all`), and `bothy migrate` never reads the keys, so the narrower list follows least privilege. |
| M7-22/5 | 2026-10-14 | Lock check: the target passes only with Object Lock enabled and a default retention in mode `COMPLIANCE` whose period (days times 24 hours plus years times 365 days) is at least `retention.audit`; `GOVERNANCE`, a lock without default retention, a bucket without Object Lock and a shorter period fail with `s3sink.ErrNotImmutable`, naming `audit.checkpoint_sink` (and `retention.audit` for the period), never the endpoint. `bothy config check` runs it for an `s3://` sink with or without `--secrets` (it needs the keys and one request; `--secrets` reports the two references as skipped unless the sink is `s3://`), and the start step `audit_log` builds the sink through the same `wire.CheckpointSink` and stops the start. | Brief item 15 and runbook 8: Governance can be lifted with `s3:BypassGovernanceRetention`; a year counted as 365 days never overstates a lock. |
| M7-22/6 | 2026-10-14 | The selection of the sink by scheme stays in `internal/wire` (`wire.CheckpointSink`, which `Audit.BindLog` now receives), not in `internal/audit/app` as the brief's scope lists it; the conformance suite is `internal/audit/app/sinktest` (only tests import it, `TestFilestestNotInBinary` also holds it out of the binary). | `app` may not import adapters (go-arch-lint, ADR 0008); the M1-50 code already selected the sink in `wire` (D-W-100: follow the merged code). |
| M7-22/7 | 2026-10-15 | `s3sink.Sink` keeps the body of every version it read in memory, by key and version ID (a version never changes; objects without a version ID are never cached), and reads the bodies a `List` has not seen with at most 16 concurrent `GetObject`s. A process reads each checkpoint once; every later run of `audit.chain_verify` costs one `ListObjectVersions` pass and one `GetObject` per new checkpoint (`TestListScalesWithManyCheckpoints`). | Red team NEIN: the job lists the sink on every run, and one sequential `GetObject` per checkpoint against a remote store at 40 ms per request passed the one-minute job timeout of River after about 1400 checkpoints, about two months at `audit.checkpoint_interval = 1h`; from then on no checkpoint was signed. The cache holds a few hundred bytes per checkpoint. |

## M4-08 Domain package

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-08-0 | 2026-10-16 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The rulings D-W-115 and D-W-116 it builds on are on main in docs/DECISIONS-PLANNING.md and are not copied. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-08-1 | 2026-10-15 | Typed IDs are extension types over the canonical lowercase UUID text that implement `Object`; besides the twelve kinds of the brief there are `ForumTagId`, `DraftId` and `QuietHoursId`, because those entities have IDs of their own. `parse` takes the 36-character form in either case; its `FormatException` names only the length of the input. `Uuid7Generator` takes the clock and the randomness as functions; `rand_a` is a counter seeded with 11 random bits at each new millisecond, incremented within it, and on overflow the timestamp advances by one, so IDs of one generator are strictly increasing, also when the clock goes back. | Note 1 of the brief; a seed below half the range leaves at least 2048 IDs per millisecond before an overflow (RFC 9562 6.2 method 1). |
| D-M4-08-2 | 2026-10-15 | Every enum carries the wire code and a member `unknown` (code empty) that `fromCode` returns for any other text; `AgentRuntimeState.unknown` is the value of the spec itself and the fallback. `test/enums_test.dart` maps every enum of the entities to its schema or property in `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and fails when a value of the spec has no member. `SystemCode` also has `agent_session.resume_failed` of 03, which the API schema of this base does not list yet. | Note 2 and requirement 17 of the brief (06 13 item 3). |
| D-M4-08-3 | 2026-10-15 | The event catalogue is generated by `tools/catalog/event_catalog_dart.py` from the `events:` list of `docs/spec/actions.yaml` into `lib/src/rules/event_catalog.g.dart`, because `action_catalog.py` has no output hook; `make event-catalog` writes it and `make codegen-check` compares. `resourceKind` is the leading word of `resource`. `decideApplyForEvent` returns `unknownType` for a type the catalogue does not know; a type that carries `resource.version` but arrives without one is not applied (`ignoreStale`), because only a greater version may change the object. | Notes 3 and 5 of the brief, 04 9 item 3. |
| D-M4-08-4 | 2026-10-15 | Grouping: text and voice are one kind class, run anchors and cards each their own, system lines never group, and a negative time difference does not group. Forum order: descending by `lastActivityAt` (or `createdAt` for `ForumSort.created`), ties by ID descending so every device shows the same order; `ForumSort.unknown` sorts by activity. Cost labels: `CostLabel.finalAmount` stands for the brief's `final`, a reserved word in Dart; an unknown billing mode is labelled information only. | Note 4 of the brief, 01 6.1 and 6.7. |
| D-M4-08-5 | 2026-10-15 | `AppConfig` is the client's single source of the values of place `App` and of the server keys of D-W-115 and D-W-116 (`auth.device_pending_ttl` included; `terminal.max_session_duration` follows with M4-36). `tools/configdoc/app_config.py` reads the generated block of section 4 of the configuration reference by column position, derives the kind from the default (`boolean`, `duration`, `bytes`, `byteRate`, `bitRate`, `pixels`, `integer`, `decimal`, `list`), keeps the server keys as a constant with the reason, fails when one is missing or has another place, and writes `lib/src/config/app_config_keys.g.dart` with the table and one typed getter per key named after the key without its first segment (a collision fails the generation). `make app-config` writes it; `make codegen-check` compares whenever `packages/domain` is tracked, for every component selection, because the check needs only Python. | Note 6 of the brief; reading by position works for the German and the English reference alike. |
| D-M4-08-6 | 2026-10-15 | `mergeReadState(local, incoming, reset: false)`: an incoming state with an older version changes nothing; a newer one keeps `lastReadSeq` monotonic (the higher position stays) and brings the server's counters; `reset: true` marks the result of `markThreadUnread`, which with a newer version replaces the local state. `ReadState.unreadCount` is optional: the snapshot (`UnreadThread`) delivers it, `ReadState` of the API carries only `mention_count`. `UnreadCounter.resetFrom` takes the thread's stored `last_message_seq` and `unread_floor_seq` and, without a delivered unread count, computes it as 03 `read_states` defines it (`last_message_seq - GREATEST(last_read_seq, unread_floor_seq)`); messages it saw above that position stay counted on top. `MessageUpdated` and `MessageDeleted` carry the previous local copy of the message, so an edit counts only a mention that was added or removed, and a deletion takes a mention back, also one the server counted. | 04 14, 03 `read_states` and requirement 13 of the brief (code review of M4-08); a `read_state.updated` on another device carries no marker of a reset (see the report). |
| D-M4-08-7 | 2026-10-15 | The file entity is `FileMeta`, so it never clashes with `dart:io` `File` in the apps; `copyWith` of the entities cannot clear a nullable field (a new object does). The package has no runtime dependency (`meta` and `collection` are not needed); `yaml` is a dev dependency for the tests that read the specification. | Note 2 of the brief and the dependency limit of its scope. |

## M4-12 Local database schema v1

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-12-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave24` at `2a72cca`, branch `feat/M4-12-local-store` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. Brief and spec were read from `main`; the brief relies on no core rule or D-W ruling that the base lacks, so nothing is ported first. The base names the chapters in German (`15-client-architektur.md`, `14-suche.md`, `03-datenmodell.md`, `04-sync-protokoll.md`); the text this ticket adds is English. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114): the removed-target kind is `RemovedTargetKind` with `TargetRef` (`AccessTargetKind` is a type of `api_client`, which `local_store` may not import, 15 3.1); `SyncStateDao` also has `markSnapshot` and `resetCursor`; the file entity is `FileMeta` (D-M4-08-7). | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win over the brief. |
| D-M4-12-1 | 2026-10-15 | Cipher `chacha20` (ChaCha20-Poly1305, the SQLite3MultipleCiphers default); the key is set as the first statement in the raw form `PRAGMA key = "x'<64 hex>'"`. The SQLite3MultipleCiphers documentation ("Raw key data (without key derivation)", configuration of SQL pragmas) states that a 64-digit hex key in this form is used directly as 32 bytes for the sqleet ChaCha20 scheme, without a key derivation; `PRAGMA hexkey` would derive. The opener then checks `SELECT count(*) FROM sqlite_master` (wrong key: `LocalStoreKeyError` without the key), `PRAGMA cipher` and FTS5; the hex text lives only in the setup call. | Design note 2; a random 256-bit key needs no derivation, and the check fails closed on a library without encryption. |
| D-M4-12-2 | 2026-10-15 | `hooks: user_defines: sqlite3: source: sqlite3mc` stands in the root `pubspec.yaml`. Verified with the pinned SDK (Flutter 3.47.7): with the define at the root, `PRAGMA cipher` reports `chacha20` and `sqlite3mc_version()` reports SQLite3 Multiple Ciphers 2.6.1 (`test/encryption_test.dart`). | Build hooks read user defines only from the workspace root. |
| D-M4-12-3 | 2026-10-15 | The version rule is one SQL statement per mirror upsert: `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... WHERE excluded.version > <table>.version`, returning whether a row changed; rows without `resource.version` are written with `put` after `decideApply`. Server times are stored as text with milliseconds and UTC (`store_date_time_values_as_text`), local times (`last_accessed_at`, outbox times) as epoch milliseconds. | Design note 3; one statement cannot race with a second writer, and the text form round-trips the entities unchanged. |
| D-M4-12-4 | 2026-10-15 | Removal deletes everything below the targets in one transaction and keeps outbox entries (they fail with `404` and keep their text, 04 8.3 item 4); file metadata goes only when no remaining message attaches the file. The threads of a removed channel come from every table that carries the channel (`threads`, `messages`, `message_attachments`, `runs`, `read_states`), and the files scoped to it (`files.scope_channel_id`) go with their cache rows when no message attaches them, because a thread row or an attachment link may be gone while content stays (code review and red team of M4-12). The cleanup deletes each thread in its own transaction. | 04 8.3; a long cleanup must not block the sync. |
| D-M4-12-5 | 2026-10-15 | `search_index` is FTS5 with the columns `kind`, `target_id`, `thread_id`, `channel_id`, `space_id` (all `UNINDEXED`) and `body`, tokenizer `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`, no stemmer; queries take each word as a quoted prefix term (FTS5 operators are plain text), ordered by `bm25`, with a `snippet` marked by the control characters U+0002 and U+0003. An absent space is stored as an empty text. | Design note 5; 14 6 accepts other word stems than the server, and quoting keeps user input from breaking the query syntax. |
| D-M4-12-6 | 2026-10-15 | `MigrationRules` is the helper of every later step (`discardCache` drops and recreates mirror tables and clears the cursor and the epoch; `keepRows` migrates preserved tables with `alterTable`); version 1 has no step, so `onUpgrade` refuses any other version until version 2 introduces `stepByStep`. `test/drift/survival_test.dart` checks the stored schema of the current version, migrates every pair of stored versions (`from <= to`) with fixture rows in `outbox`, `drafts` and `share_inbox`, fails when a stored version has no fixture rows, and runs the encrypted round trip of version 1. | Design note 4 and 15 6.2; with one version drift generates no migration test, so the survival test is the test M4-36 extends. |
| D-M4-12-7 | 2026-10-15 | `make codegen-check` runs `dart run build_runner build` and `dart run drift_dev make-migrations` in every Dart member whose `build.yaml` configures `drift_dev`, with `tools/ci/codegen_check.py` (the snapshot comparison of the Pigeon step) instead of a temporary copy; `.pre-commit-config.yaml` excludes `drift_schemas/*.json` from the end-of-file fixer. | drift writes the schema file without a final newline and `make-migrations` refuses a stored schema that differs by one byte; the snapshot comparison ignores the ignored build outputs and needs no copy of the workspace. |
| D-M4-12-8 | 2026-10-15 | `host_key_pins` is not in schema version 1; M4-36 adds it with its own schema version, adds it to `MigrationRules.preservedTables` and extends the survival test. | Fixed requirement 10 (specification first). |
| D-M4-12-9 | 2026-10-15 | License: SQLite3MultipleCiphers 2.6.1 is MIT (its repository's `LICENSE`), SQLite is public domain; the `sqlite3` hook bundles a prebuilt library checked by sha256, not sources, so the check is the upstream license file and the version the library reports; `make licenses` covers the Dart package `sqlite3` (MIT). | Design note 8; bundled native code is outside the lock files the gate reads. |
| D-M4-12-10 | 2026-10-17 | The ticket was ported from its integration base onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win (the two paragraphs of 15 section 6 and the section of `docs/quality/licenses.md` sit in the English chapters, the code comments point to `15-client-architecture.md` and `14-search.md`). The drift codegen skip is named `codegen-drift-dart`, and the Pigeon block of the base is not part of this ticket. | `tools/ci/tests/test_make_server_gates.py` on main drops Dart skips by the substring `-dart` (the base matched any `dart`); the Pigeon check belongs to M4-10. |
| D-M4-12-11 | 2026-10-17 | The drift table definitions under `packages/<member>/lib/src/tables/` leave the Dart coverage sum (`tools/ci/lcov_filter.py`) and the Sonar coverage measure (`sonar.coverage.exclusions`) like generated code; they stay in lint and analysis. `test/round_trip_test.dart` adds round trips of the instance, principal, agent, command and personal tables. | `make coverage` measured 58.3 % for `local_store` on main (the builder's `make -k check` never reached `coverage` because `test` stopped first): the 341 lines of table getters only feed the generator, and the code that runs is the generated `database.g.dart`. Without them and with the new tests the member is above `quality.coverage.dart_min_percent`. |

## M4-13 Sync engine: connection and session

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-13-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave27` at `3d4f0f3`, branch `feat/M4-13-sync-connection` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. Brief and spec were read from `main`; the close code `4018` the brief relies on is on `main` (D-W-24) but not on the base, so its gateway part comes first as its own commit (section "Rulings from main: D-W-24"). The base names the chapters `04-sync-protokoll.md`, `15-client-architektur.md` and `08-identitaet.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114): the session class is `GatewaySession`, the backoff `ReconnectBackoff`, the close table lives in its own file `close_codes.dart` (`closeCodeTable`, `reactTo`, `CloseReaction`, `Reconnect`); `AppLifecycle` also has `current`, because `1000` reconnects only while the app is in the foreground and a mobile start in the background must not connect; `GatewayConnection` takes the `AuthSession` of `auth` and the `SyncStateDao` of `local_store` directly. The server gateway of M1-64 and M1-65 is not on this base, so no server behaviour could be compared with 04 6.4. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief. |
| D-M4-13-1 | 2026-10-16 | `ConnectionState(phase, reason)`; the reasons are `none`, `networkUnavailable`, `devicePending`, `deviceReplaced`, `forbidden`, `rateLimited`, `revoked`, `protocolUnsupported` and `closedByServer(code)`. Transitions beyond the diagram of 15 7.2: `4016` goes to `offline`/`devicePending` and reconnects only after `LoginService.pollPendingDevice` reports `active` (a rejected device ends in `signedOut`); `4017` goes to `offline`/`deviceReplaced` and reconnects on the foreground or `connectNow`; `4018` goes to `offline`/`forbidden` and reconnects only on `connectNow`; `4008` goes to `backoff`/`rateLimited` and waits `retry_after=<seconds>` of the close reason; `4015` goes to `offline` and is logged as unexpected; a server `1000` backs off in the foreground and waits `offline` for the foreground otherwise; a drop without a close code backs off, or goes `offline`/`networkUnavailable` while the device has no network. Without a listener `ready` and `resumed` lead to `live`, with one to `catchingUp`, and the listener sets `catchingUp`, `resyncing` or `live` through `SessionControl`. | Brief Design note 3; 15 7.2 shows the added transitions. |
| D-M4-13-2 | 2026-10-16 | The close reactions are one map from code to `CloseReaction` (phase, reason, reconnect mode, `identify`, `renewToken`, `signOut`, `unexpected`), and `reactTo` resolves only what depends on the close reason or the app state (`4008`, `1000`, a code outside the table). `4004` and `4011` renew the token through `RefreshCoordinator.refreshNow` and reconnect at once; a second renewal (`4004` or `4011`) before the next `ready` or `resumed` waits the backoff, so a server that keeps rejecting is not flooded; a refresh that ends the session signs out, a transient failure backs off. `4013` waits uniform in `[0, sync.reconnect_backoff_base]` without raising the counter. `4012` calls `AuthSession.sessionRevoked`, whose `SignedOut(sessionRevoked)` carries `deleteLocalData`. | Brief Design note 4: the test iterates the table. |
| D-M4-13-3 | 2026-10-16 | A network change and the return to the foreground start an attempt at once from `backoff` (and from `offline` with `networkUnavailable`; the foreground also from `suspended`, `deviceReplaced` and a server `1000` in the background) without raising the attempt counter; only a failed attempt draws the next backoff. A network loss during a backoff wait cancels it and goes `offline`/`networkUnavailable`. On mobile no path opens a socket in the background: an attempt that would start there (a backoff timer, a token renewal, an approved pending device) goes to `suspended` and waits for the foreground; a return to the foreground while the own background close is still running connects at once; a server close that arrived before the own close keeps its reaction (code review of M4-13). | 04 6.5, 15 7.2 `Suspended`. |
| D-M4-13-4 | 2026-10-16 | Unknown ops and unknown members of known ops are ignored; client ops coming from the server are ignored like unknown ops. A server frame that is not a JSON object with a string `op`, or whose `d` does not fit the schema of its op, counts as a decode error (`decodeErrors`), is logged with its cause only and skipped; the socket stays. `dispatch` keeps the raw envelope map for `decodeEnvelope` of M4-14. Outgoing `d` drops `null` members recursively. | 04 6.1 (additive changes), 04 6.4 (closing with a protocol code is the server's role), 04 2.2. |
| D-M4-13-5 | 2026-10-16 | Focus overflow: at most `sync.max_focused_threads` IDs are sent; the most recently shown threads win, where a thread counts as shown when it first appears in a `setVisible` call and IDs new in one call count in the iteration order of the set; `setVisible` returns the unfocused rest. Changes within one microtask send one frame; after `ready` the current set is sent when not empty, after `resumed` only a change made while disconnected; an `error` with `validation_failed` for `focus` restores the previous set. | Brief Design note 6; 04 11.3 item 2 and 6.3 item 7 (the server restores the focus on `resume`). |
| D-M4-13-6 | 2026-10-16 | The typing throttle is per connection as on the server: `typing.start` at most once per `sync.typing_min_interval`, `typing.stop` only after a start and it resets the throttle; a start for another thread inside the interval first stops the previous thread. A new socket resets the throttle; frames that cannot be sent (no session) are dropped. | 04 12 item 1: `typing.stop` resets the throttle of the connection. |
| D-M4-13-7 | 2026-10-16 | `disconnected_at` is stored when a socket that had reached `ready` or `resumed` ends, not after a failed attempt, so failed attempts do not move the start of the resume window. `resume` needs a stored gateway session, a stored cursor (`GatewayResume.cursor` is required) and a disconnect less than `resume_window_ms` of the new `hello` ago; otherwise `identify`, with cursor and epoch only when a cursor is stored. The heartbeat sends the stored cursor read at `ready` or `resumed` (0 without one) until the listener sets `lastProcessedSeq`. | Brief Design notes 2 and 5. |
| D-M4-13-8 | 2026-10-16 | Frames are decoded as they arrive; `heartbeat_ack` and `reauth_required` act at once, every other frame waits in the ordered queue behind the listener steps. The bookkeeping of a disconnect (store `disconnected_at`, `onDisconnected`) cannot keep the close reaction from running. | Red team of M4-13: an acknowledgement queued behind a long snapshot or resync step of the listener missed its interval and closed a healthy connection (04 6.3 items 5 and 6). |
| D-M4-13-9 | 2026-10-19 | Every gateway attempt has the deadline `client.sync.connect_timeout` (new key of place `App`, default `30s`, 15 7.2 and 22) from `Connecting` until `ready` or `resumed`. When it passes, `GatewayConnection` invalidates the generation of the attempt, closes an open socket with `1000` and, once the close is done (bounded by the same value again), takes the backoff of a failed connect, so no second socket opens while the first one closes; it does nothing after the session started, while a close the client started itself runs, or when the server already closed the socket (its close code decides the reaction). The `dart:io` transport gets no own connect timeout: the attempt deadline covers the TCP connect, the upgrade and the handshake. | CodeRabbit on the pull request of M4-13, reproduced with tests on the fake clock: a connect or upgrade that never completed, or a gateway that never sent `hello` or never answered `identify` or `resume`, left the connection in `connecting` or `identifying` for good, and network changes and the foreground did not start a new attempt while it was open. The chapters named no client deadline, so the key is new. |

## M4-14 Sync engine: catch-up and reconciliation

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-14-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave30` at `c0ad78b`, branch `feat/M4-14-sync-catch-up` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. Brief and spec were read from `main`; the brief relies on no core rule or contract change that the base lacks (R-SY-14 and R-CL-03 are in the German chapters 04 and 15 of the base, D-W-115 was ported earlier, D-W-100 and D-W-114 are planning rulings), so nothing is ported. The base names the chapters `04-sync-protokoll.md` and `15-client-architektur.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114): the sign-out notice is `SignedOutNotice` (`SignedOut` is an event of `auth`), the REST reads are the port `Fetcher` with `ApiFetcher` over the generated API, `Dispatcher.apply` takes a `DecodedEnvelope` (the raw envelope with the `DecodedEvent` of `api_client`, null when malformed) plus the stored cursor and an `AfterCommit`, `Fetcher.myReadStates` takes a channel because the generated `listMyReadStates` requires `channel_id`, the write path of drafts calls `CatchUpListener.applyDraftPutResult`, and a failed gap fill is retried by `CatchUpListener.onFocus`. The wire mappers (`wire.dart`) and the identity port (`LocalIdentity`, `AuthIdentity`) are files beyond the brief's list. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief. |
| D-M4-14-1 | 2026-10-16 | A gateway page is the run of `dispatch` frames up to the next `cursor` frame or `catchup.complete`; it is applied in one `commitPage` with the `seq` of that frame (never below the stored cursor) and the epoch of the session. In the live phase every durable `dispatch` is its own transaction with `cursor = seq`; ephemeral events apply at once without a transaction. A disconnect drops an unfinished page; its events come again from the stored cursor. | Design note 1; 04 7.1 (the server sends `cursor` at the end of every page) and 04 9 item 2. |
| D-M4-14-2 | 2026-10-16 | `ready` without a stored cursor empties the mirror and applies the whole snapshot with `cursor = head_seq` and the epoch in one transaction and goes `live` at once, because an `identify` without a cursor gets no catch-up and no `catchup.complete` (04 6.3 item 4; red team of M4-14); with a cursor only the parts that are not events apply (active run summaries by version, the counters, the agent presence, the feature flags into a stored instance row). After a whole snapshot the drafts are loaded with `listMyDrafts` when a draft reference is newer than or missing from the local drafts, the read states with `listMyReadStates` for the channels with unread threads, and the instance with `getInstance` (its flags replaced by the snapshot's). | Design note 1; without a cursor the mirror belongs to no known time line; the snapshot names drafts only by version and carries counters without a read state version. |
| D-M4-14-3 | 2026-10-16 | The outbox, the run store and the outbox start are hooks (`onEcho`, `onRunUpdated`, `onLive`), constructor parameters with no-op defaults, not dependencies. `onEcho` runs after the commit for a `message.created` with `client_msg_id` whose author is the person (or when the person is not known yet, after a `resume` in a new process); a truncated `message.created` echoes after its reload. | Design note 2; M4-17 wires them; outbox IDs are UUIDv7 of this device, so an echo of another author never matches an entry. |
| D-M4-14-4 | 2026-10-16 | `access.changed` in the middle of a page applies in its `seq` position (removal inside the page transaction, file bytes and `TargetsRemoved` after the commit) and the rest of the page applies after it. Only `4012` and `session.revoked` of this session stop a page: the page rolls back, the database is wiped (`LocalDatabase.wipe`), `SignedOutNotice(deleteLocalData: true)` is emitted and nothing is applied after that. `4012` reaches the listener as the connection state `signedOut`/`revoked` (`watchConnection`). | Design note 3; 04 8.1 and 8.3 item 2: the gateway withholds only events of removed targets, so dropping the rest would lose events that are never sent again. |
| D-M4-14-5 | 2026-10-16 | `session.revoked` wipes only when its `session_id` is this device's session (`LocalIdentity.sessionId`, the `sid` of `auth`) or the own session is unknown; a revocation of another session of the person (a logout on the phone) changes nothing here. | `session.revoked` has the audience `self` and reaches every device of the person (05 6); the revoked device itself is also closed with `4012` (04 8.5). |
| D-M4-14-6 | 2026-10-16 | Gaps: a `message.created` that continues a loaded range, or arrives in a thread with no loaded range, or lies below every loaded range joins the loaded ranges; otherwise it is stored without joining them, the gap from the end of the highest range below it up to it is recorded, and `Fetcher.range` loads it after the commit (`listThreadMessages` with `after_seq`, page by page); the fetched messages and the whole range up to the new message join the loaded ranges in one transaction. `MessagesDao.watchContiguous` therefore shows only the contiguous range ending at the newest loaded message. A failed fill keeps the gap recorded; `onFocus` retries it. | Design note 4; 04 4: a real gap is missing data, and a thread never opened here shows from its newest message without loading its history. |
| D-M4-14-7 | 2026-10-16 | Personal data: the echo of this device (`updated_by_device_id`) is ignored for drafts, settings and the sidebar; the read state is merged also for the own echo, because `mergeReadState` is monotonic and versioned and the counters come only from the server. A `draft.updated` or `draft.deleted` for a draft the person is typing on this device (`DraftEditing`) is not applied; the next local write wins. | Design note 5 and 04 14. |
| D-M4-14-8 | 2026-10-16 | A page whose transaction fails (a payload that breaks its mapping, a database error) leaves rows and cursor as they were; the listener then applies nothing until the next `ready` or `resumed`, which catches up from the stored cursor. An envelope that does not fit the schema of its type is skipped like an unknown type and the cursor passes it. | A later page must not move the cursor past events that were never applied; `SessionControl` has no close, and a reconnect repeats the page. |
| D-M4-14-9 | 2026-10-16 | When `getSnapshot` fails during a resync, the listener keeps mirror and cursor and retries the snapshot after the reconnect backoff (`sync.reconnect_backoff_base`, `sync.reconnect_backoff_cap`, full jitter) on the same connection; frames that arrive meanwhile wait in memory and apply after the snapshot with the duplicate rule. A disconnect ends the retries; the next session catches up from the stored cursor and gets `resync_required` again. `onLive` runs once per session, also when a `catchup.complete` follows a resync. | Code review of M4-14: dropping the mirror and halting left the app empty on an open connection until the next reconnect; after a restore every client asks for the snapshot at once. |
| D-M4-14-10 | 2026-10-16 | `packages/local_store` gets three methods beyond the brief's list, each with its test: `PersonalDao.deleteSettingIfNewer` and `deleteNotificationPreferenceIfNewer` (for `deleted: true`, 04 14) and `MessagesDao.reactionsOf` (reading the `me` flag of a reaction inside the page transaction, where a stream would wait for the transaction's end). | Fixed requirement 17 and Design note 5 need the deletions; no existing method removes those rows. |
| D-M4-14-11 | 2026-10-16 | `decideApply` decides per event between the version rule and the `seq` rule and drops a versioned type that arrives without `resource.version`; the comparison with the stored version is the conditional upsert of the DAOs (D-M4-12-3), one statement per row. Event payloads, the snapshot and REST answers go through one set of wire mappers from JSON to the domain types; the generated models of REST answers are serialized back to JSON first. The page size for the isolate decision is an estimate over the decoded frames (string lengths plus a few bytes per value). | One rule in one place; the gateway frames are already JSON-decoded by the connection, so their raw size is not at hand. |
| D-M4-14-12 | 2026-10-16 | REST reads that run after a commit (a truncated object, a gap fill, the newest page of a focused thread, the structure of `added` or `changed` targets, drafts and read states after a snapshot) store their result only when, inside the transaction of the write, the target is still held: content needs its channel row and neither the channel, its space nor the thread may have been removed in this process (`SyncContext.removedTargets`); a view of `changed` needs its row. An `access.changed` with `added` (for its spaces and channels and for every thread its structure load lists), a `thread.moved` and a snapshot that lists a target take it out of the removed set (follow-up review of M4-14: a moved-out thread stayed blocked after its new channel became visible). | Code review of M4-14, reproduced: a `getMessage` in flight during `access.changed` with `removed` stored the message and its search row again (04 8.3 item 3, T-DV-01). |
| D-M4-14-13 | 2026-10-18 | The ticket was ported from its integration base onto main with its own commits and the integration fix INT-W33/3 (the doc comments of `fetcher.dart` and `sync_events.dart` name `ApiClient` and `AuthSession`, so `make docs` resolves them); the English chapter 15 of main takes the page boundary of 7.3. The event catalogue of main has two durable types more than the base, `device.pending` and `gdpr.request_due`; neither has a local table, so they join the skipped set of `catalogue_test.dart` and the README. | Tickets are built in parallel on integration bases, main takes each as one pull request with the integration fixes it needs; the other fixes of that wave belong to M6-01. |
| D-M4-14-14 | 2026-10-19 | The snapshot retry of a resync (D-M4-14-9) checks its session after the snapshot fetch and after the follow-up steps of its commit (`markSnapshot`, `_afterCommit`, which always run once the snapshot is committed, because only their reads restore the instance row and the read states) and ends when a new session started; only the retry of the current session sets `lastProcessedSeq`, clears `_resyncing`, sets `live`, reports `Resynced` and drains the held frames. The drain flag names its session (`_drainingSession`): a drain of an ended session no longer makes the next session hold its frames. | CodeRabbit on the pull request of M4-14, reproduced with `resync_session_test.dart`: a disconnect and a new `ready` while the retry awaited its commit set the new session live during its catch-up and reported `Resynced` for it, and frames of a new session that arrived while the old drain was inside a frame stayed in the hold list after that drain ended, so the next `cursor` or `catchup.complete` moved the cursor past them (04 9, D-M4-14-1). |

## M4-17 Sync engine: fake server and composition root

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-17-0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave35` at `4773519`, branch `feat/M4-17-fake-server` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100 and D-W-114 are on the base), so nothing is ported; the API documents of the base hold every operation of Design note 3. The brief's `15-client-architecture.md` and `04-sync-protocol.md` are `15-client-architektur.md` and `04-sync-protokoll.md` on the base; the lines this ticket adds there are English. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the local store is `LocalDatabase`; the REST client is `AuthSession.api`, which the session already builds with its interceptors, so `SyncEngine.create` does not build a second one; `create` also needs `client` (`GatewayClientInfo` for `identify`) and takes `ids`, `draftEditing`, `keepConnectedInBackground`, `allowPlainHttpForTests` and `logHook`; `focus` is an `EngineFocus` over the `FocusManager`; the engine exposes `state` and `states`. Fault names: `delay(duration, operationId:)`, `revoke(deviceId:)`, plus `holdDispatches` and `releaseDispatches`; a lost network is `FakeServer.disconnect`. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-17-1 | 2026-10-17 | One composition root: `SyncEngine.create` builds the parts of M4-13 to M4-16 and connects every hook (echo to `Outbox.confirmByEcho` with `message_id` and `version`; the catch-up's live signal to `Outbox.start`; every disconnect to `Outbox.pause`; `run.updated` to `RunStore.onRunUpdated`; `run.state_changed` to an end state to `RunStore.onRunEnded`; focused runs into the gateway focus through `EngineFocus.focusRun`). The run end is read by a listener that wraps the catch-up listener after it applied the frame, so the dispatcher of M4-14 stays unchanged. | Brief Design note 2: the desktop and the mobile app cannot wire the hooks differently. |
| D-M4-17-2 | 2026-10-17 | The fake's REST side routes by the paths of `openapi.yaml` (a literal segment before a parameter) and keeps a handler registry: `FakeServer.handle(operationId, handler)`; this ticket's handlers register from one file per group under `lib/src/testing/rest/`; an operation without a handler answers `501` and is recorded. | Later tickets add operations without editing this file (Design note 3). |
| D-M4-17-3 | 2026-10-17 | Faults are deterministic: `FaultPlan` is a queue of REST rules matched in order and used up per request (applied after the token and proof checks, so a nonce retry does not eat a fault), and gateway faults act at once or after a fixed delay; IDs of the world are counters. | A failing case must repeat on every run (Design note 4, 15 20 rationale). |
| D-M4-17-4 | 2026-10-17 | Contract checks in the fake with an own validator (`ContractSchemas`) for the subset the documents use, plus `allOf` and the bounds `minimum`, `maximum`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, `minItems`, `maxItems` and `pattern`; no JSON Schema package is in the lock file, so no dependency was added. Invalid request bodies answer `400 validation_failed` with the pointers and are recorded; a response the fake would send against the contract becomes a recorded `500`; a frame of the fake against the contract throws; an invalid client frame closes with `4002`. Every scenario fails on a recorded violation. | The fake can never drift from the contract silently (Design note 5). |
| D-M4-17-5 | 2026-10-17 | The scenarios of 04 16 are functions over the interface `ScenarioClient` (send, offline and online, local messages, outbox, channels, notices, cursor, focus, run document, run state) and a running `FakeServer`; they throw `ScenarioFailure` instead of using `package:test`, so the library has no test framework as a runtime dependency. `test/scenarios/` implements the interface over `SyncEngine`; M4-18 implements it over the app. | One set of flows for the headless and the app tests (Design note 7, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-17-6 | 2026-10-17 | `SyncEngine` accepts only an `https` base URL (gateway `wss`); `allowPlainHttpForTests: true` admits `http` (gateway `ws`) for the fake and the app's test entry point. `tools/arch-check` gains rule 10 as a general rule: a library named `testing.dart` of any member and its `lib/src/testing/` folder are imported or exported only from `test/` and `integration_test/` folders, also relatively inside the own member; 15 3.1 lists it. | DPoP and the device binding assume a protected transport (15 8); test support never reaches a production build (Design note 8). |
| D-M4-17-7 | 2026-10-17 | The gateway's `hello` carries the same nonce as the REST `DPoP-Nonce` and the gateway accepts the current and the previous one, as REST does. | 08 7.3 makes nonces stateless for every process; the client keeps one nonce store, so a gateway-only nonce made the next REST call fail with `use_dpop_nonce` (found by the first scenario run). |
| D-M4-17-8 | 2026-10-17 | `crypto` and `pointycastle` move from `dev_dependencies` to `dependencies` of `packages/sync_engine`, and `yaml` (already in the lock file) is added there: `lib/testing.dart` lives under `lib/` and needs them; the production library does not import them. | Brief Scope (`pubspec.yaml`: the dependencies the fake needs); their licenses (BSD-3-Clause, MIT) are on the allowed list. |
| D-M4-17-9 | 2026-10-17 | `FakeWorld.append` takes `resource.version` from the event catalogue of `domain`: a type the catalogue marks without it never carries one (reactions do not raise the message version, `read_state.updated` and `settings.updated` carry the version only in the payload), and a type that needs it fails the write; the contract test checks every logged event against the catalogue. | 04 9.3; found by the code review: a foreign version made the fake answer `412` to an edit after a reaction. |
| D-M4-17-10 | 2026-10-17 | Fixes after the red team: the outbox's `onConfirmed` stores the `Message` a confirmed `createMessage` or `updateMessage` returned through the new `CatchUpListener.storeConfirmedMessage` (version rule, removed targets skipped), so a sent message never disappears between its response and its echo; after `Resynced` the engine ends the documents of focused runs that the local run summaries list as ended or no longer hold; the fake computes `unread_threads` and the read state's `mention_count` (messages of others after the read position, not deleted). | 04 13.2 item 6, 04 7.3 with 15 7.4, 04 14; red team of M4-17. |

## M4-18 Desktop app shell

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-18-0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave36` at `8493d3f`, branch `feat/M4-18-desktop-shell` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-116 and `auth.device_pending_ttl` in `AppConfig` are on the base), so nothing is ported. The brief's `15-client-architecture.md` is `15-client-architektur.md` on the base; the lines this ticket adds there are English. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the local store is `LocalDatabase` and its wipe `LocalDatabase.wipe`; `openLocalStore` takes `file` and `keys`; the device key client is `DeviceKeyClient` with `ensure`, `sign` and `delete(scope)`, whose `StepUpPrompt` (texts) is a different type from the `StepUpPrompt` port of `auth` (a question); `LoginService` has no discovery call, so E1 calls `getDiscovery` through the generated client; the fake server has no handlers for `getSpace`, `getChannel` and `getThread`, which no flow of this ticket needs against it (the target lookup is tested with a stub client). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-18-1 | 2026-10-17 | `bootstrap.dart` composes per server address (`InstanceServices.open`): the device keys with the `instance_id` of discovery as scope, `InstanceSecrets` with every key prefixed `instance/<instance_id>/`, the database key (32 bytes of `Random.secure()`, stored as `database_key`), the encrypted database `<application support>/instances/<instance_id>/local.db`, the `AuthSession` with browser, loopback receiver and (where present) passkey port, and the `SyncEngine` (`keepConnectedInBackground`). `AppPorts` holds every port and `appPortsProvider` hands it out, so tests replace all of them. On macOS only a release build uses the Data Protection keychain with the keychain access group; a debug or profile build is not signed by a team, has no access group and keeps the items in the login keychain. | One instance never sees the secrets or keys of another, and a wipe can find everything by the instance ID; an access group without a team signature makes the app fail to launch. |
| D-M4-18-2 | 2026-10-17 | One `AccountWiper` per instance for every end of an account: logout, E4 "Cancel", a rejected or expired waiting device, `4012`, `session.revoked`, `401 session_revoked`, and also a refresh token the server no longer accepts (E7 "session expired"). It stops the engine, wipes the database, deletes the instance folder (database files and file cache), both device keys and every instance secret, runs every step even when one fails and keeps only the server address. The controller reacts to the `SignedOut` events of the session and to the engine's `SignedOutNotice` through one guarded path. | 01 5.6 shows "The data of this device has been deleted" on every E7; one path cannot forget a step for one cause. |
| D-M4-18-3 | 2026-10-17 | E1 classifies discovery: a `HandshakeException`, a `TlsException` or Dio's `badCertificate` is "Certificate invalid" with no way to continue and no setting to trust a certificate; `setup_required` (flag or problem) is "Instance not set up yet"; a `min_client_versions` entry of the platform above the build's version (numeric `major.minor.patch`) is "App too old"; everything else is "No instance at this address". Only the integration test entry accepts `http`. The address is remembered after a found instance and asked again at the next start. | R-PR-14: an exception button would let an attacker in the middle defeat DPoP and the device binding. |
| D-M4-18-4 | 2026-10-17 | The router has the paths of 15 11 plus `/onboarding/<step>` and `/settings/...`; `parseLocation` is a pure function that checks IDs with the ID types of `domain`. The redirect follows the onboarding controller: signed out, a content path goes to the current screen and is kept, and after the login it opens. A target that is not in the local database and that REST answers with `404` or `403` shows "Not found or no access"; without an answer it waits. Deep links: macOS registers the URL type in `Info.plist` and the app delegate forwards links; the Windows runner registers the scheme under `HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\Classes` at every start (no administrator rights; MSIX of M7 replaces it), a second start with a link sends it to the running window by `WM_COPYDATA` and exits; both forward over the method channel `bothy/deep_links` (`takePending` for a link that started the app, `open` for later ones). | 15 11; a per-user registration works for a local build without an installer. |
| D-M4-18-5 | 2026-10-17 | Column names `sidebar`, `post_list`, `history`, `session`; the sized columns default to the layout tokens `DsLayout.sidebarWidth`, `listWidth`, `sessionWidth`, never below `DsLayout.columnMin`, and the history keeps at least `columnMin`. One drag is one `putMySetting` of `layout.column_widths` through the outbox (only the widths that differ from the defaults); the dragged widths stay until the stored setting comes back from `user_settings`. "Reset" is `deleteMySetting` (the setting's default is empty). Below `ui.desktop_min_width_four_columns` the session column is not shown, below `ui.desktop_min_width_three_columns` the post list and the history share one place. Until M4-25 the sidebar frame carries the "Log out" button. | 01 5.1 and 11; a column that jumps back while the write travels would look broken. |
| D-M4-18-6 | 2026-10-17 | `CodeText` shows a code in groups (four by default; the verification code of E4 in groups of three as the wireframe of 01 5.6 shows it) in the mono font of the tokens, offers copy without spaces, and its semantics label spells the code with commas between the characters while the visible groups are excluded from semantics. Remaining times are text ("Valid for 23 more minutes") from `expires_at` (E5) or `created_at` plus `auth.device_pending_ttl` (E4) and an injected clock, announced through the `LiveAnnouncer` of M4-07 at most once per `a11y.live_announce_min_interval`. | 01 5.6 and 8: a screen reader must not read "481" as a number. |
| D-M4-18-7 | 2026-10-17 | Build branding before M7: `apps/client/branding.example.yaml` (`app_name` `ExampleChat`, `url_scheme` `chat-example`, `bundle_id` `com.example.chat`, `default_server` `https://chat.example.com`) and `tool/branding_defines.dart`, which writes `build/branding/dart_defines.json` for `--dart-define-from-file` and the checked-in `macos/Runner/Configs/Branding.xcconfig` and `windows/runner/branding.h`; `--check` (and a test) holds the checked-in files equal to the example. The interface shows `branding.product_name` of the instance after discovery; the build's app name only names the window before it. | Design note 7; one source for the Dart side and both runners. |
| D-M4-18-8 | 2026-10-17 | Network status without `connectivity_plus`: `LifecycleNetworkStatus` reports online and emits "network may be back" on every return to the foreground; a lost network shows through the gateway's failed connects and heartbeat (04 6.5). | No new dependency in the license and OSV gates; the engine already retries with backoff. |
| D-M4-18-9 | 2026-10-17 | The desktop composition passes no `PasskeyAuthenticator`, because no package of the workspace bridges WebAuthn on macOS or Windows yet; E2 and E3 offer passkeys only when the ports have one (the tests and goldens inject one). E3 offers "Use this device" (`redeemEnrollmentCode`), "Create passkey" and "Link with" each login service; it offers no "Set up password and one-time code", because the API has no operation that sets a password and a TOTP secret while a code is redeemed (spec gap, reported). | Fixed requirement 13 ("where offered"); P-11: no deviation from the contract. |
| D-M4-18-10 | 2026-10-17 | When the poll of a waiting device ends because the server ended its session first (`401 session_revoked` before `getDevice` could report the revoke reason), E7 shows "not confirmed in time" once the app's own deadline (`created_at` plus `auth.device_pending_ttl`) has passed and "the confirmation was rejected" before it; a revoke reason that arrives is shown as it is. | The device cannot read its revoke reason without a session; the deadline is the only fact it has. |
| D-M4-18-11 | 2026-10-17 | E6 is built as a screen with its states and goldens and has its route `/onboarding/locked`; the onboarding controller never enters it, and "Unlock" does nothing until M5-08 builds the lock. | Brief Out of scope. |
